# app — architecture

> Section 2 names the decisions, section 3 the design, and section 3.10 what is open, by epic.
>
> What the component does, what it does not do and what is still to be decided about it. This is the detail of its card, architecture 3.1 spec. The card stays the summary: if the detail changes what it says, the card is amended in `spec` by the same route as questions to its registers (RULES.md, Across repositories).
>
> Why it was decided so and not otherwise is in [decisions.md](decisions.md); the work plan is in [RUN.md](../RUN.md). The concepts are concepts spec. The documents and decisions of `spec` lie in `spec/docs/` and are cited with the suffix "spec".

## 1. Purpose and boundaries

**Does:**
- the app's shell (architecture 3.1 spec):
  - the account owner's sign-in through the sign-in service;
  - the choice of the learner on a shared device: a child learns under an adult's account and creates no account of their own (R06 spec; privacy 7 spec), and the same account can also hold the profile of the owner themselves as a learner (R26 spec);
  - the start of work — the learner's age and country, consent. The platform stores not the age and not the date of birth, but the age category, the country and the date when the age was given (privacy 3.3 spec). A parent's consent is a signed form, whose scan the operator checks, and before the public launch a card transaction is added to it (R116 spec). That the account owner is an adult is confirmed by their statement "I am 18" with a date (R118 spec). `api` does not yet accept learners from the countries of the EU, the EEA and the United Kingdom (R131 spec);
  - the learner's grade — when the module manifest names grades. The answer is stored in the learner's profile and can be a grade that the module does not name; the grade closes nothing and only places the learner in a difficulty band (architecture 3.1 spec; R49 spec; [the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 11);
- three entries (product 4 spec): Home, the first of the places of the frame, where the engine leads through the connected modules — product 4 spec's "My subjects" —; the skill map, the learner's second way in beside Home; and "A task from my paper", the screen `#/paper` (R162 spec; R71; section 3.10);
- the lesson player and the task screen: the canvas from the package `canvas`, answer slots, the choice of an option, the tutor's panel with the solving steps and hints, the conversation line (architecture 3.1 spec). The tutor speaks of the work only while its panel is open: with it closed, "Check" gives the reading and the program's verdict without `GetRungWords` and `GetCorrectAnswerWords`, and an opening asks them once, for the last submission of the task on screen (the second 2026-11-17 amendment to R148 spec; section 3.3). The solving mode is "by steps" or "self": the default mode is set by the adult, for a new profile it is "by steps", and the learner switches it on a task (product 4 spec; concepts 10 spec):
  - the text of an item of a handwriting sheet `app` shows together with the options of the slots `choice` beside the sheet, not on it; the item line of a workbook is shown by the canvas (architecture 3.1 spec; [the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 14);
  - a lesson `app` leads by its open view: the segments in the author's order — explanations, worked examples and the lesson's tasks, which `api` gives (R58 spec). How exactly — section 3.4;
  - the first slice has three modules: olympiad maths 1–6 on a handwriting sheet, school maths of grade 3 on a workbook and short essays in English. It has no text canvas and no language modules (R41 spec; product 10 spec);
- the sending of work to reading — only on the learner's action. Work is read on "Check", and "Step done" in format v0 does not read the work: it only leads to the next step (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R69 spec; R05; section 3.3). On a pause the app only makes a button blink, without a call to AI (R69 spec; concepts 9.1 spec). An answer by a tapped choice of an option is checked without AI (architecture 4.2 spec); keyboard input and editing in the text of the statement will come in later versions of the format ([the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 14). A verdict is given at each answer slot, and the verdict of an item is made up of them (R56 spec). An item with a rubric has no verdict: the learner gets feedback by the rubric (R04 spec, R75 spec; architecture 4.1 spec). After the check the learner sees what was read, and the tutor's marks lie on a layer of the tutor's own and do not touch the learner's work (R05 spec; architecture 4.1 spec);
- progress and the skill map — by the package `skill-map`, from ready data from `api`: both on the learner's screen and in the section for adults (architecture 3.3 spec); at a module's level an adult set, "the level was set by an adult" with the date, by `level_band` and `level_set_at` of `GetSkillMap`, for every learner (architecture 3.1 spec; R157 spec; R56). The reason code, why the engine chose a task, the adult sees, and the child is not told it (concepts 12 spec). The list of codes is the closed `ReasonCode` of the contract, nine values with `REASON_CODE_ROTATION` (R100, R107 spec). The child is told on their screen, in their language and in words clear for their age, that the adult sees their progress — attempts, rubric marks and repeating traps ([DPIA](../spec/docs/dpia.md), section 6, risk 16);
- the section for adults — behind a PIN from epic E4, without one until then (R56): profiles, tariff, own key, settings, export and deletion, and a module's level — "Easier" and "Harder", a band next to the level's `level_band` by `ProgressService.SetModuleLevel`, with the right `can_manage` at every profile of the account (architecture 3.1 spec; R157 spec; the 2026-11-16 amendment to R99 spec; R56). Deletion is confirmed by a fresh sign-in, not by the PIN (architecture 3.5 spec, 4.5 spec);
- export: `app` assembles it — from the device and from the part that `api` gives. The export format is open, in `spec` (architecture 4.5 spec; R09 spec);
- the disclosure that the learner talks with AI, not with a person (architecture 3.1 spec; privacy 5.3 spec), — to every learner. On top of the AI providers' measures, beside each reply of the tutor there is "Report a problem" for the adult, and learning materials on safe use (R123 spec);
- to a learner under 18, at work with a rubric and in the section "Help", open to them from any screen, — where to turn if something is wrong: to an adult and to the helplines of their country, by name and number, without links, by a list with a date of checking. No reminder about names stands before "Check", on a sheet, an essay or a task from paper: the author's word; the camera's line before the photo of a page and Help's line for an adult stay (R127 spec; R53; Д288 `spec`);
- legal texts — pages of `app` (architecture 3.13 spec): the policy, the terms and the consents `app` gets from `api` by the method `LegalService.GetLegalTexts` and shows as its own pages, without external links, and for a consent, its gist in the screen's language beside the English text and a translation of the version, without force of its own, if there is one (R115, R116, R130, R141 spec). A consent names the text version that the person saw: `CreateAccount` — `privacy-policy` and `terms-of-use`, and a parent's consent, a teenager's own consent and the storage of artifacts — texts of their own (R131, R136 spec). Which versions are in force `api` says; the operator in them is ALT EdTech LLC (R132, R137, R138, R141 spec). How — section 3.8;
- voicing of the tutor's words — for everyone: it is output, not data collection (privacy 6 spec). The card starts it with the device's speech synthesis (architecture 3.1 spec; Т-13): `app` names the voice itself and takes only a local one, by the rule of Т-13. Where the browser does not know this, voicing on the device is turned on by the adult — this is a choice of the system that the adult makes, not a disclosure for the child (R118 spec), — and the text on the screen always stays. How — section 3.5. Later, with the text canvas, `app` will voice its "listen" too by the same speech synthesis — on the event `speak` of the canvas element, which R36 spec names and which `canvas` has not while it has no text canvas (the 2026-10-25 amendment to R07 canvas; sources 5.1);
- the interface in English and Russian (О-2): the learner's language is set by the adult in their profile, and the module's texts and the tutor's words stay in the module's explanation language, each block with its own `lang`. How — section 3.8;
- accessibility — WCAG 2.2 level AA and two AAA criteria. What the task screen does not meet yet — the answer by handwriting, the lines of the workbook and the sending of an answer without confirmation, — `app` names on the page "Accessibility". How — section 3.9.

**Does not:**
- does not get the key, the solution and the traps of an unsolved task. The app receives the open view, which `module-kit` builds by the marks `x-open` in the format's schemas: a field without the mark is closed (R05 spec, R45 spec; architecture 1 spec; repositories 4 spec). The task's solution comes after a correct answer or the showing of the solution (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R05 spec; R82 spec). The task's open view has none of its skills, band and difficulty (R51 spec), techniques, traps, simpler case and leak phrases (R57 spec). The task's technique `app` gets only from `api` — at the step that gives it, or at the learner's request (architecture 3.1 spec; R57 spec);
- does not give a verdict: `module-kit` gives it, in the process of `api` (R03 spec; architecture 3.5 spec). The solving steps `app` leads by the open view, and the exit conditions of a step are events on the device, without the key (architecture 3.1 spec). How — section 3.3;
- does not call AI providers: everything that calls a model is in `tutor`, a library inside `api` (architecture 1 spec; R15 spec). The own key goes with the request to `api` and lives there only in the request's memory (architecture 4.3 spec), and how `app` holds and sends it is section 3.6;
- does not send strokes and the whole photo to the server. What goes to the check is an image or a crop of the work, and a photo from paper is cropped to the task on the device, before the call to AI (R09 spec; privacy 6 spec). From a workbook go the crops of the filled boxes with their places, without the item's text and the scratch work (R53 spec; architecture 4.1 spec). Of the writing history nothing goes to AI — neither the order of writing, nor the log of actions, nor the frames before erasing (R52 spec);
- does not load its parts of the interface at run time. Foreign code is only the widgets of modules, from stage 4, in the iframe sandbox, through the widget host from `canvas` (R16 spec; architecture 3.4, 6 spec);
- keeps no server process of its own: in the first slice there is one service, `api`, and the build of `app` is served by the hosting that `infra` chooses (architecture 1 spec, 3.11 spec, 3.12 spec). The address of `api` and the sign-in parameters the build does not embed: it reads them at start from a file beside itself, `/deployment.json`, — section 3.7;
- does not show public profiles, contact between learners, geolocation, ads and external links (privacy 1 spec, 7 spec); for children, no timers and competitions (product 7 spec);
- does not take voice input: it is not in the first slice (product 10 spec), and for children it comes only after the decision of П-5; the legal questions are decided by the author on Claude's analysis (R112 spec; О-6).

**Data and links:**
- on the device: strokes with time and pressure, the learner's artifacts and the own key — the key only if the adult so decided on this device (architecture 3.1 spec, 5 spec; R84 spec; section 3.6); with tasks from paper, also photos from paper (architecture 5 spec; R162 spec). Artifacts on the server — only if the account owner turned this on for the profile, by the consent text `storage-consent`; by default storage is off, and removing it erases the copies on the server (R113, R131 spec). What lies on the device, how much and until when, is section 3.2;
- also on the device, the context of each submission: the canvas gives it together with the work, and `app` holds it as it is, and by the digest the canvas accepts the context back (architecture 3.1 spec; R36 spec). What is in it is section 3.2;
- the open views of the module manifest, the skill graph, the technique catalog and the solving steps `app` gets from `api` by the package digest of the attempt, once per package, and caches them by it, and to the canvas it gives the manifest by the property `manifest` (architecture 3.1 spec; R36 spec, R62 spec). The open views of a withdrawn version of a module it removes from the cache (R63 spec). Media files come from the device part, which `api` serves (architecture 3.10 spec; R62 spec);
- on a shared device several learners learn under one sign-in of the account owner, and the owner themselves can be among them (architecture 3.1 spec; R06 spec, R26 spec). The owner profile is closed to a child by the same PIN as the section for adults (R84 spec; section 3.6). A teenager's account is П-13;
- only `api` stores learners' data on the server (architecture 1 spec). The learner's profile is the pseudonym, the avatar, the age category, the country and the date the age was given, the presentation style with voicing, the default mode, the interface language, the grade — if the module asks for it, — and for a child also the adult's setting "ask before showing" the solution (privacy 4 spec; concepts 13 spec; R82 spec). The profile's fields in the contract are `AccountService`; there are no interests there until the format gives a framing of a task by interests (R98 spec);
- talks only with `api` and with the sign-in service (architecture 3.1 spec; repositories 2.1 spec). The sign-in service — Firebase Auth through Google (R03 api) — has the addresses of Google that section 3.7 names. The API contracts are protobuf and buf (R11 spec): proto3, over Connect, by unary calls only, the client in `app` is connect-es, and the TypeScript code is the npm package `@altnovatrail/contracts` from the release of `spec`. Within a major package an old build of `app` works with a new `api` (R83 spec). The module format is JSON Schema 2020-12 (R45 spec). With requests to platform AI `app` sends the device's clock offset from UTC, in minutes: by it the family day of the quota ends, and this is not geolocation but account data (R118 spec; concepts 14 spec). The contract `app` ↔ `api` is the package `novatrail.api.v1`: the check of an attempt is `AttemptService` (R97 spec), the account and profiles are `AccountService` (R98 spec), the path from a module to a task is `PackageService`, `ModuleService`, `LearningService` and `ProgressService` (R99 spec), the legal texts are `LegalService` (R130 spec). A refusal is a Connect error with a standard code and the detail `Refusal`, and the AI call outcome is a field of the successful response (R97 spec); a place on the canvas in it is in shares 0–1000 of the crop (R53 spec). What each screen takes from the contract is sections 3.2–3.4 and 3.6. The tutor's words come to the device whole, when the whole text has passed the leak check and the form check, not as a stream (R54 spec, R70 spec). There is no real-time channel in the first slice (architecture 7 spec; Т-25);
- the task's open view `app` reads by the TypeScript types from the npm package `@altnovatrail/spec`, `@altnovatrail/spec/formatv0/open`, and `api` reads the same view by the Go types (R72 spec, R73 spec). While the format is v0, the platform reads only its current version, and `app` and `api` move to a new version together (R44 spec; architecture 3.1 spec; repositories 4 spec);
- for a review of the course of the solution, sends the work again: the server does not store the image (architecture 4.1 spec);
- at build time depends only on `canvas` and `skill-map`, besides the packages of `spec` — `@altnovatrail/spec` and `@altnovatrail/contracts` (R73, R83 spec), — and the icon set Material Symbols (R78 spec): it builds them in by exact version, and in development it connects their working copies (repositories 2.1 spec, 3 spec; R16 spec; architecture 6 spec). The packages enter the app as web components, `canvas` as the element `<novatrail-canvas>` (R36 spec), and are installed from npm, from the scope `@altnovatrail` (R37 spec). The signature of their version is checked by the recipe of `spec` `release-verify` — for an npm lock file, its mode `release-verify-npm` — in CI of the pull request that changes the lock file (architecture 3.1 spec; R77 spec; RULES.md, Hard rules). The types of the canvas element are `@altnovatrail/spec/canvas`, in the same package of types (R74 spec). The package `@altnovatrail/spec` lies in `devDependencies`, by an exact version — the same as the tag of the submodule `spec/` (R73 spec; repositories 1 spec);
- the build is rolled out by `infra` by the same list of exact versions and digests as the services (R106 spec), and the hosting of the build and the domains are with `infra` too (architecture 3.11 spec). The hosting is Firebase Hosting: Google is a processor for it, the link of Hosting with Cloud Logging is turned off, and its period — "a few months" — stands in the written retention policy (R128 spec). The platform's data region is the USA (R109 spec);
- the license is Apache-2.0; dependencies only with licenses compatible with it (R12 spec; R20 spec);
- the screens are described in [the prototype's README](../spec/draft/README.md) (product 4 spec); where the prototype differs from the documents, the documents prevail;
- stage 2 — the first slice with a learner, together with `tutor`, `canvas`, `api`, `skill-map` and `infra` (product 11 spec; repositories 2.1 spec). Before the first child: the parent's verifiable consent (R116 spec), the contracts with the AI providers (R121 spec) and the DPIA (R119, R129 spec; repositories 6 spec; product 10 spec);
- before the slice, the first app: the first build of `app` with `api`, on which the author solves tasks themselves in the browser, locally — with handwriting, lessons and the engine, with sign-in, without children and without deployment. Tasks in it come by lessons, and there is no own key and no tariffs. The order of the tasks of all repositories toward it is led by the cross-repository plan of `spec`, [COORDINATION.md](../spec/COORDINATION.md) (R76 spec; repositories 6 spec).

## 2. To decide when detailed

The items "To decide when detailed" of the card, a subsection each: each names its decision, and its design is in section 3. A question that goes beyond this repository goes «в реестр spec» (RULES.md, Across repositories). The lesson player, which the card places fourth (R58 spec), is subsection 2.8, since the numbers 2.4–2.7 are cited (R01); the interface languages and accessibility are one item of the card and one subsection, 2.7. All eight are decided. The questions of `spec` about the screens of `app` that are not items of the card, and what `app` does while they are open, are in section 3.10.

### 2.1. Framework and build

- **Gist:** what the app is written in and how its build is assembled — the interface framework, the bundler, the type check, the linter, the tests —, and what this changes in the devcontainer. It is decided here: the technologies of each part are its repository's (R11 spec), and so is the framework of each consumer of the UI packages (architecture 6 spec).
- **Decided:** R03; the design — section 3.1.

### 2.2. What is stored on the device and how much

- **Gist:** which data `app` keeps on the device, in which browser storage, how much and until when — for each learner of a shared device —, what of it goes into the export and when it is erased. It is an item of the card, "including the submission context", and the data of the app itself (architecture 3.1 spec, 5 spec; R36 spec). The periods on the server are R128 spec; here, those on the device.
- **Decided:** R04; the design — section 3.2.

### 2.3. Solving steps by the open view

- **Gist:** how `app` leads the learner through the solving steps of a task: how it reads the steps and the exit conditions from the open view, how it knows that a step is passed, what the learner sees and does at a step and how the steps meet the check, — without knowing the key. It is an item of the card: `app` leads the steps by the open view, and the exit conditions of a step are events on the device (architecture 3.1 spec). What the steps need in the open view is format v0 (R57 spec; [the format text](../spec/format/README.md), sections 14, 18 and 19); what they will lack in it goes «в реестр spec», to its next version (R44 spec).
- **Decided:** R05; the design and the path by steps of the three modules of the first slice — section 3.3.

### 2.4. Voicing by the device's speech synthesis (Т-13)

- **Gist:** how `app` voices the tutor's words by the device's speech synthesis: which voices are allowed, what to do if there is no suitable voice, and where voicing is turned on. The card starts voicing with the device's speech synthesis (architecture 3.1 spec); the voice as an AI capability is with `tutor` (architecture 3.6 spec), and the question of the voice itself is Т-13.
- **Decided:** R07; the design — section 3.5.

### 2.5. The adult's own key on a shared tablet (Т-9, О-4, П-17)

- **Gist:** how `app` keeps the adult's own key on a device at which a child learns too: where the key lies, when and with the request of which profile it goes, how it is connected, changed and deleted, and how a child does not get into the account owner's profile. It is an item of the card, and the section for adults with the own key is part of `app` (architecture 3.1 spec); whether `api` accepts the key and the admission function lets it through is R84 spec and Т-20.
- **Decided:** R08; the design and the key's path step by step — section 3.6.

### 2.6. The deployment configuration at run time

- **Gist:** how the build of `app` learns at run time the address of `api`, the parameters of the sign-in service and the other settings of the deployment, so that both the platform and self-hosting take one and the same signed build (architecture 3.1 spec). It is an item of the card; the file's format is the part `deployment/` of `spec` (R85 spec), and the rollout and the hosting are `infra`'s (architecture 3.11 spec; R106, R128 spec).
- **Decided:** R09; the design — section 3.7.

### 2.7. The interface languages (О-2) and accessibility

- **Gist:** in which languages the interface of the first slice is, how translations and the choice of language work, how the interface language goes with the module's explanation language, — and which accessibility `app` keeps to and how it is checked. It is one item of the card (architecture 3.1 spec). Accessibility here, «доступность интерфейса», is accessibility; the vocabulary distinguishes it from the opening of a skill and from access to content — when a skill opens to the learner and which content does not suit their age category (concepts 4, 15 spec).
- **Decided:** R10 and R11; the design — sections 3.8, with the glossary, and 3.9.

### 2.8. The lesson player

- **Gist:** how `app` leads a lesson by its open view: how the segments follow one another — explanations, worked examples and tasks —, how the demo opens by parts and hides, where the state of the demo is kept and what happens to it when the module's version has changed, and how the mark "sequential" is derived. It is the item of the card that came with R58 spec (architecture 3.1 spec).
- **Decided:** R06; the design and the learner's path through a lesson of olympiad maths — section 3.4.

## 3. Design

What section 2 decided: which parts the component consists of, its types and data, how it is linked with other repositories. Subsections 3.1–3.9 go in the order of the decisions, R03–R11; section 3.10 is what else the card assigns to `app`, the questions of `spec` about its screens, the screens built outside 3.1–3.9 and what is open, by epic.


### 3.1. Framework and build

Decided by R03. The app is web, for a phone, a tablet and a computer, without offline and native apps (product 10 spec; R148 spec): the layout by the width, the phone's below 600 CSS px, is section 3.9 (R59). It has no server process of its own: the hosting of `infra`, Firebase Hosting, serves its build (architecture 1 spec, 3.11 spec; R128 spec). The versions are those of `package.json` and `package-lock.json`, and the browsers' and the tools' those of `.devcontainer/Dockerfile`. A third-party package is taken at the latest version released no later than three days before its installation, as in R12 canvas, and the dates of the whole lock file are checked against the registry (R14, R15). The packages of `@altnovatrail` are outside the pause: they are installed after the third-party ones, without a date, and `release-verify-npm` checks their signature (R77 spec); `@altnovatrail/spec` and `@altnovatrail/contracts` are at the submodule's tag (R73, R83 spec). Licenses are by the field `license` of the packages in the npm registry, and for the VS Code extension by its repository (sources 2.1, 2.3).

| Role | Package | License |
|---|---|---|
| framework | `react`, `react-dom`; `@types/react`, `@types/react-dom` | MIT |
| bundler | `vite` — with Rolldown inside; `@vitejs/plugin-react` | MIT |
| CSS minification | `esbuild` | MIT |
| `dev` exception | `lightningcss` and its builds for the platforms — Vite installs them | MPL-2.0 |
| type check | `typescript` | Apache-2.0 |
| Node types — for `vite.config.ts` and the tests in Node | `@types/node` | MIT |
| platform types | `@altnovatrail/spec`; `@altnovatrail/canvas` and `@altnovatrail/skill-map` may each keep a nested copy, the one it names in `dependencies`. These are only types, they put nothing into the build, and `ElementHoldsContract` and `ElementTakesViews` do not let them diverge from the copy of `app` (R73 spec; R15; R34) | Apache-2.0 |
| format and lint | `@biomejs/biome`; the VS Code extension `biomejs.biome`, its version in `.devcontainer/devcontainer.json` | MIT OR Apache-2.0 |
| tests | `vitest`, `@vitest/browser-playwright`, `@vitest/coverage-v8` | MIT |
| test browsers | `playwright`, at the version of the template's Playwright image whatever its date, which Dependabot keeps in `ignore` (R14); Chrome Headless Shell, WebKit and Firefox from that image by tag and digest (R79 spec; R38; R43) | Apache-2.0; the browsers — named exceptions by the 2026-10-22 and 2026-11-13 amendments to R12 spec: Chrome Headless Shell on Google's free terms, WebKit and Firefox |
| screen tests | `@testing-library/react`, `@testing-library/dom`; input — `userEvent` from `vitest/browser` | MIT |
| reading the YAML of the examples and vectors of `spec` in tests | `yaml`, without dependencies | ISC |
| accessibility check — section 3.9 | `axe-core` | MPL-2.0 — a `dev` exception (R11) |
| icons | the script `scripts/icons.ts` on `@iconify/utils`; the set `@iconify-json/material-symbols`, at the version that `canvas` and `skill-map` name (R78 spec; R03; R15) | MIT; the set — Apache-2.0 |
| contracts `app` ↔ `api` | `@altnovatrail/contracts`; `@bufbuild/protobuf`, the version that the contracts name in `dependencies`; the connect-es client — `@connectrpc/connect` and the Connect transport for the browser `@connectrpc/connect-web` (R83 spec; R15) | Apache-2.0; `@bufbuild/protobuf` — `(Apache-2.0 AND BSD-3-Clause)`, and the text of BSD-3-Clause goes into the build's `NOTICE` (R83 spec) |
| legal texts — the Markdown body, section 3.8 | `marked`, the lexer only, without dependencies; the elements are built by code of its own (R27) | MIT; its `LICENSE` also holds John Gruber's Markdown license, and its text goes into the build's `NOTICE` |
| sign-in — section 3.7 | `@firebase/app`, `@firebase/auth` — those that `firebase` pins, not `firebase` itself: its `firestore` pulls in `@grpc/grpc-js` with a high finding (R15) | Apache-2.0 |
| canvas | `@altnovatrail/canvas`, the latest on npm when taken (R15). It reads the version of the module format that `app` reads, sets its styles through `adoptedStyleSheets` and stands under its own CSP (R15); what `app` takes from it is in the sections that use it — the bar and the slot `actions` (section 3.4), `exportPicture()` (section 3.2), the scrolling of the sheet and the keyboard into it (section 3.9) | Apache-2.0 |
| skill map | `@altnovatrail/skill-map`, the latest on npm when taken, with the second entry `./states`, the state of each skill by the rule of the map, for the cards of Home (R62; R34). It sets the styles of its Shadow DOM through `adoptedStyleSheets` and stands under the CSP of `app` — a test of the built page holds this | Apache-2.0 |

**TypeScript.**
- `lib` — ES2022, DOM and DOM.Iterable: the types of the canvas element take the DOM types (R74 spec). `target` — ES2022, as in `canvas`; to revisit when the browsers of the devices become known.
- The strict flags are the same as in `tsconfig.base.json` of `canvas`. Its own are `moduleResolution: bundler` and `jsx: react-jsx`: modules are resolved by Vite, not by `tsc` as in `canvas` with its `NodeNext`. The package `@altnovatrail/spec` is checked under `bundler` too (R73 spec).
- `types` — by an explicit list: in TypeScript 7 it is empty by default. For the app it holds `vite/client`. `vite.config.ts` and the tests in Node have a `tsc` program of their own with `types: ["node"]`, like `tsconfig.node.json` of `canvas`.
- The canvas element in JSX — by the augmentation `declare module "react" { namespace JSX { interface IntrinsicElements { "novatrail-canvas": … } } }` by the types of `@altnovatrail/spec/canvas`, in the file `src/canvas/jsx.ts`, not `.d.ts`: in it the type `ElementHoldsContract` checks the class of the canvas element against the contract of the copy of `app` both ways, and errors in `.d.ts` are skipped by `skipLibCheck`. A trial test in Chromium holds the augmentation on the real element (R15). The types are imported by `import type`; `HTMLElementTagNameMap` and the event map are augmented by the package `canvas` itself (R74 spec). The function `media` `app` gives the element by the type `Media` of the same package (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R74 spec), and `undo()` and `clear()` are outside the contract, and `app` does not call them: "Undo" and "Clear" are buttons of the element itself (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R36 spec).
- The skill map element in JSX — the same way, in the file `src/skills/jsx.ts`: `"novatrail-skill-map"` with the types of the class `NovatrailSkillMap` and `SkillMapView` of the package `@altnovatrail/skill-map`, and the data types — `SkillGraph` and `Manifest` from `@altnovatrail/spec/formatv0/open` of the copy of `app` and `GetSkillMapResponse` from `@altnovatrail/contracts`. The type `ElementTakesViews` checks the class's properties — `graph`, `tiers`, `manifest` and `skillMap` — against these types both ways: in the element they come from its own copy of `@altnovatrail/spec`, and a diverged type is an error of `ci-lint` (R73 spec; R34). `lang` is required, `view` is `learner` or `adult`, `heading-level` is from 2 to 6, the event is `onskillchoose`, in lower case; a trial test in Chromium holds the augmentation on the real element. `src/main.tsx` imports the entry point of `@altnovatrail/skill-map` before the first render, on the second line, after the canvas.
- `tsc --noEmit` only checks, and Vite builds. There are two programs: `tsconfig.app.json` — `src/` with DOM, and `tsconfig.node.json` — the configuration, `scripts/` and the tests in Node; the shared flags are `tsconfig.base.json`, with `allowImportingTsExtensions`: imports go with the extension `.ts`.

**Routes** — in the hash of the address, by code of its own (R14): `/#/<screen>/<part>/…`, the parts percent-encoded. Parsing and building the hash is `src/app/hash.ts`, without DOM, and navigation and the current route for React are `src/app/router.ts`, on `hashchange` and `useSyncExternalStore`; a hash that the build does not know is the home screen. The hosting needs no rules: for every address it serves the one `index.html` at the root. The fragment after `#` the browser sends neither to the hosting nor to `api`, and the address holds only the ID of content, a lesson or a task, and never the ID of a profile, an account or an attempt.

**React and the canvas element** — as `canvas` recorded (R42 canvas; architecture 3.3 canvas):
- the entry point of `@altnovatrail/canvas` the app imports before the first render, in `src/main.tsx`, on the first line: before the element is registered, React writes an attribute, not a property (R20);
- React assigns properties in the order of the JSX: `task` and `history` before `marks`, `workedExample` before `demoState` (R36 spec);
- `tool` and `demoState` the app either does not give at all or gives exactly what `toolchange` and `demochange` said: a new literal on each re-render would return the demo to the beginning;
- events — in lower case, as the element sends them: `onhistorychange`, `ontoolchange`, `ondemochange`. The contract's event map is only these three; `speak` will come with the text canvas (R36 spec, R74 spec);
- a new element for each segment of a lesson — by a new `key` (section 3.4);
- `null` is not written into properties; the attribute `lang` always stands on the element itself — the screen's language, and `lang` in `CanvasProps` is required: without the attribute the canvas names the sheet's region in the page's language, and that is its nonconformity with 3.1.2 (R11; section 3.9);
- the pen mode is one for the whole page and holds until a reload, and `app` does nothing for it; nothing zooms the sheet, which fits the page to the element's width (R73 canvas);
- the writing history and the submission context the app stores through JSON, as they are (section 3.2), and in the CSP it allows `img-src blob:`.

**Vite.**
- `build.cssMinify: 'esbuild'`: `lightningcss` processes nothing of what ships (R03).
- `build.outDir` — a folder of its own, not `dist/`: `dist/` holds only the release files, and in it the shared `release.yml` of `.github` accepts plain files alone — "a release takes files alone" (sources 2.1). So too `canvas`, whose build is in `lib/`.
- The deployment configuration is not embedded: neither `define` nor variables `VITE_*`. Vite replaces them at build time, and `app` reads the configuration at run time from `/deployment.json` — section 3.7 (R09).
- `build.assetsInlineLimit: 0`: Vite does not inline small files as `data:` addresses, and the CSP does not allow them (section 3.7).
- A Vite plugin of its own: at build time it puts the lower layer of the CSP into `<head>` right after `<meta charset>` and puts `deployment.schema.json` and `csp.json` into the build. If anything else turns up before the lower layer, the build stops. In development and in `vite preview` it serves `/deployment.json` from `deployment.local.json`, and on the development server there is no lower layer: the React Refresh preamble is an inline script. The paths of `api` are led to the local `api` by `server.proxy` and `preview.proxy`, and both servers have one port, with `strictPort: true` (section 3.7).
- The development server is on `127.0.0.1`, with its own `server.fs.allow` — `index.html`, `src/` and `node_modules/`: without the list Vite would serve the whole repository (R20 canvas).
- `appType: "mpa"`: neither the development server nor `vite preview` serves `index.html` for a path that does not exist — like the hosting without rules of its own, — and `/deployment.json` without the file answers 404, not a page (R14; section 3.7).

**Packages and licenses.**
- npm from the Node of the stage `toolchain-core`, which `toolchain` is built over. In `.npmrc` — `save-exact=true` and `ignore-scripts=true`, the lock file is of version 3.
- In `dependencies` is everything that the bundler embeds into the build: `react`, `react-dom`, `@altnovatrail/canvas`, `@altnovatrail/skill-map`, `@altnovatrail/contracts` with `@bufbuild/protobuf` and the Connect client, `@firebase/app` and `@firebase/auth` and the package of the icon set `@iconify-json/material-symbols` (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R39 spec). Everything else is in `devDependencies`, and `@altnovatrail/spec` too (R73 spec). On this split depend both `ci-vuln` — `npm audit --omit=dev` looks only at what ships, — and the exception of `lightningcss`: it holds only while the package is `dev` (R39 spec).
- `NOTICE` — the notice of `app` and a line `<name> <version> — <license> — <copyright>` for each package of the lock file without the flag `dev`, transitive ones included, — the license as an SPDX expression, — a line `Text of <license>` with the text of each identifier of their licenses, and for a package with a `NOTICE` file of its own, its text as it is, as a section `Notice of <name> <version>:` (Apache License 2.0, clause 4(d)); `scripts/notice.ts` in `ci-licenses` checks this against the lock file and `node_modules` both ways (R14, R15). A `NOTICE` of their own is in `@altnovatrail/canvas` and `@altnovatrail/skill-map`.
- The build shows `NOTICE` and `LICENSE` itself too — the page of notices about others' code `#/legal/notices` (R31). `src/legal/notices.ts` takes their copy from the repository's files as text at build time, by Vite's `?raw` import, and a test in Node checks it against the files byte for byte; so the page loads nothing at run time and shows the same bytes as the build's archive beside `index.html`. So `server.fs.allow` of the development server names these two files too.
- The licenses of the lock file are checked by the recipe of `spec` `licenses-check-repo` by the list `licenses-allowed`, with the exceptions of `licenses.json`. The exception of `lightningcss` is only for packages with `dev`, with a reason (R39 spec), and npm is not written into this file: it is named in `spec` (the 2026-10-22 amendment to R12 spec). So too `axe-core` under MPL-2.0: it checks accessibility in tests, does not ship and puts none of its own code into what ships (R11).
- `@altnovatrail/spec` — in `devDependencies`, by a copy of its own, not `peerDependencies`. Its version is raised by the move to a tag together with the submodule, and Dependabot `npm` skips it (R73 spec). So too `@altnovatrail/contracts`: its version is the tag's, the same move raises it, and Dependabot `npm` keeps it in `ignore` (the fourth 2026-11-02 amendment to R83 spec; the 2026-11-02 amendment to R21 spec). The contracts' code `app` imports by the path of the code file, as the package exports it — `@altnovatrail/contracts/novatrail/api/v1/attempt_pb.js`, — not by a copy and not from the submodule (R83 spec).
- `canvas` and `skill-map` — by exact version from npm, from the scope `@altnovatrail` (R37 spec), in `package.json`. The signature of their version and of the versions of `@altnovatrail/spec` and `@altnovatrail/contracts` is checked by `release-verify-npm` in CI of the pull request that changes the lock file, and from then on the version is held by `integrity` (R77 spec; RULES.md, Hard rules; R15).
- A working copy of `canvas` `app` connects in development by the recipe `just dev-canvas <folder>`, and it does not get into `main` (R15): only on the development server, by the variable `NOVATRAIL_CANVAS`, `vite.config.ts` points the entry points that the copy's `package.json` exports to its `lib/` and lets the server into its folder. `package.json` and the lock file do not change, and `npm link` is rejected: it edits them, and `release-verify-npm` names a link to a folder and skips it (R77 spec). There are no links in the lock file — `scripts/pins.ts` holds this.

**Tests** — Vitest, in four projects:
- logic — in Node;
- screens with the real canvas element — in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox, an instance of each project per engine, named `<project> (<engine>)`, by the provider `@vitest/browser-playwright`, with `headless: true` explicitly (R45): in the development container there is no `CI`. Testing Library for React renders and queries the screen, and input is driven by `userEvent` from `vitest/browser`: it goes through the Chrome DevTools Protocol and does not fake events, as `@testing-library/user-event` does (sources 2.1). No `act()` wraps such events, so the tests run with `IS_REACT_ACT_ENVIRONMENT` off, which Testing Library's `render` and `act` turn on for their own time, while React reads it only to warn (sources 2.6), and take `act` from Testing Library alone: `biome.json` refuses it from `react` and `react-dom/test-utils` (the 2026-11-16 amendment to R03).
- the coverage — of Node and Chromium alone: Vitest's v8 coverage refuses any other engine (sources 2.5), so `ci-test` runs them with it, and `ci-engines` runs WebKit and Firefox without it, as the check "TypeScript / engines" beside "TypeScript / test" (R45 and its 2026-11-16 amendment);
- Accessibility — section 3.9: two more projects, `forced colors` in Chromium and Firefox — WebKit has no such mode — and `reduced motion` in all three, each with its own file and with the mode of the browser context from the very start — the provider's `contextOptions`, `forcedColors: "active"` and `reducedMotion: "reduce"`, as in `canvas` (R17), — and `axe-core` on each screen, except the subtree of the canvas element; the contrast of the theme's colors — in Node.
- Coverage — `@vitest/coverage-v8`, as one report `coverage/lcov.info`, as the TypeScript wrapper of `.github` expects; the merge waits for `codecov/patch` — at least 90 % of the changed lines (R48 spec; RULES.md, How we work).
- The browsers — Chrome Headless Shell, WebKit and Firefox from the Playwright image by tag and digest, a `COPY` each, in the stage `toolchain`, as R13 canvas; the path to them is `ENV PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH`. The stage is built `FROM toolchain-core` — Node, just, Go and buf, in which the release and the checks without a browser run — and adds the browsers alone, with the libraries of the three engines and the fonts of Playwright's list `tools`, from a source of one snapshot of the Ubuntu archive alone; the default font is WenQuanYi Zen Hei, in Chromium too (the 2026-11-13 amendment to R79 spec; the 2026-11-16 amendment to R40 spec; R43; R51). The browsers are named exceptions by the 2026-10-22 and 2026-11-13 amendments to R12 spec: Chrome Headless Shell is a build of Google Chrome for Testing on Google's free terms.

**Icons** — the set Material Symbols, `@iconify-json/material-symbols`, in the style Rounded: outlined, and the selected one filled (R78 spec). They are embedded by a script of its own, as in `canvas`: `scripts/icons.ts` on `@iconify/utils` writes `src/app/icons.ts` — the viewBox and paths of only those icons that the app takes, from the set's package at the exact version, — and `ci-lint` fails if the module is not the one that the script writes; `just icons` writes it anew. They are drawn by the component `src/app/Icon.tsx`, by SVG elements, without inserting markup as a string. `unplugin-icons` with `compiler: 'jsx'` is rejected: its `@svgr/core` pulls in through Babel `caniuse-lite` under CC-BY-4.0, which is not in the list of `spec` (the 2026-11-10 amendment to R03). There are no Iconify components that load icons from the Iconify API at run time — `@iconify/react`, `iconify-icon`: a request from the device would give the learner's IP address to a third party, and the CSP does not name the hosts of Iconify.
- Which icon goes with which value, the table of architecture 6 spec says; the buttons of the canvas's row are seen in `app` on the task screen. The icon of a value that only `app` has, `app` chooses not from those in the table, and records it here, and those of the closed list in section 3.9 (R78 spec; R60): "Continue" of the lesson player — `arrow-circle-right`, without fill, mirrored right to left (the third 2026-11-02 amendment to R78 spec; the 2026-11-10 amendment to R06; section 3.4); "Read aloud" — `volume-up`, without fill, as is right to left: a speaker is not a direction (R36; section 3.5). The way in's (R61; section 3.10): the language's pill — `language` and `keyboard-arrow-down`; a tick — `check`; the countries' row — `chevron-right`, mirrored; "Add a learner" — `add`. Home's (R62; R79): the camera of "Tasks from my paper" — `photo-camera`; the phone's button of the legend — `info`; a card's Continue — `play-arrow`, mirrored; the kind "Adaptive" and its foot — `autorenew`; "Sequential" — `keyboard-double-arrow-right`, mirrored; a chain's next skill — `arrow-forward`, mirrored; the states and marks of a skill on a module's card and in the legend are the table's. The frame's (R79): Home — `grid-view`, Subjects — `menu-book`, each filled while it is the page; the cognitive map — `hub`; "Grown-ups" — `lock`; This device — `tablet-android`; "All settings" — `settings`. A task from paper's (R71): "Choose from photos" — `photo-library`; a frame's corner — `open-in-full`; the shutter and Retake are the card's camera, a frame's cross `close`, a frame picked the way in's tick, and the note's lock the state "Locked". The profile's menu's (R63): the chevron of its pill — `keyboard-arrow-down`; "Switch learner" — `swap-horiz`; "Sign out" — `logout`, mirrored; "For grown-ups" — `group`. The lesson's (R64): the chevron of the line under its title — `keyboard-arrow-down`, the pill's; a passed segment — `check`; "Lesson complete" — `star`, filled. The task screen's (R65): "Hint" — `lightbulb`; the card's signs — "Not yet" `priority-high`, Check's lock `edit`, the solution shown `description`; feedback `chat-bubble` (R66); a task from paper with no key `help`, Help's (R71).
- An icon's SVG — with `aria-hidden="true"`, without `<title>` and in the color `currentColor`, and the button's caption is visible beside the icon and remains its accessible name, but for the closed list of section 3.9 (R78 spec).
- The set's version is the same as that of the versions of `canvas` and `skill-map` in the lock file of `app` (R78 spec; R12). `scripts/pins.ts` checks it, by a test in Node, in `ci-test`: it reads the `package.json` of the installed `@altnovatrail/canvas` and `@altnovatrail/skill-map` — their `devDependencies` name the set's version, and the npm registry serves them, — and compares it with the set's version in the `dependencies` of `app`. A divergence fails the test, so a Dependabot pull request with a new canvas or skill-map whose set moved is red until a task raises the set in all three, by a row of COORDINATION.md spec (the 2026-11-17 amendment to R78 spec); the set itself Dependabot keeps in `ignore`, and a package without the set in `devDependencies` the test skips. The same test holds `@bufbuild/protobuf` at the version from the `dependencies` of `@altnovatrail/contracts` and the `@firebase` packages at one copy each in the lock file (R15).
- The set's notice — Material Symbols, Google, Apache-2.0, the address `google/material-design-icons` and the version of the set's package — goes into the build's `NOTICE` as its line, together with the notices of everything embedded in it (R78 spec).

**Recipes** — by the contract of the TypeScript wrapper of `.github`, which calls `ci-lint`, `ci-vuln` and `ci-licenses` in the stage `toolchain-core` and `ci-test` and `ci-engines` in `toolchain`, with the browsers; the shared `release.yml` calls `release-files` in `toolchain-core` (R51; R47 .github). A tool that one of the first three or `release-files` needs goes into the core, one that only the tests need into `toolchain`:
- `ci-lint` — `biome ci`, `tsc --noEmit` with both programs, `scripts/versions.ts` — only exact versions in `package.json` — and `scripts/icons.ts --check`;
- `ci-test` — `build`, then `vitest run --coverage` of Node and Chromium, then the release's archive of a second build (R25): the shared `typescript.yml` does not call `build`, and a build that failed fails "TypeScript / test" (R14);
- `ci-engines` — `vitest run` of WebKit and Firefox, without coverage and without `build`: no test in a browser reads `build/` (the 2026-11-16 amendment to R45);
- `ci-vuln` — `npm audit --omit=dev --audit-level=high`;
- `ci-licenses` — after `npm ci`: `just -f spec/justfile licenses-check-repo package-lock.json licenses.json`, `just -f spec/justfile release-verify-npm package-lock.json`, `just -f spec/justfile release-verify spec <tag> parts/deployment-1.tar.gz` — the release file of the part `deployment/` (section 3.7) — and `scripts/notice.ts`, which reads the `NOTICE` of the packages from `node_modules`;
- `build` — `vite build`, into the folder `build/`; for manual use — also `dev`, `dev-canvas <folder>`, `preview`, `icons` and `fmt`;
- `release-files <version>` — the archive of this build, `app_<version>.tar.gz`, as the only file in `dist/` (R25): at the root, the build and beside it `NOTICE` and `LICENSE`, because the bundle does not carry the packages' license comments. `scripts/release.ts` packs it by code of its own — ustar, by name, without time and owner, with the mode `0644` for files and `0755` for folders, `gzip` without name, time and system, — and one build gives the same bytes on any machine, and `ci-test` checks the build's archive against the archive of a second build of the same tree; `deployment.json` is not in the archive, and the recipe checks this (section 3.7). It is signed by the shared `release.yml` of `.github` without the input `npm-package`, and `infra` rolls out the build only with a verified signature (R77 spec; R106 spec). Releases are immutable, and the ruleset `v-tags` forbids the tags `v*` `update`, `deletion` and `non_fast_forward`, without bypass (R77 spec).
- `site-check <origin> <version>` — by hand, after a rollout: the archive of the release and its signature from the release's public address, accepted by `just -f spec/justfile release-verify app <version>`, then `scripts/site.ts` checks the site at `<origin>` against it, section 3.7, "Tests" (R42).

**Devcontainer and CI.** How the devcontainer differs from the template is recorded by a decision (repositories 1 spec), and the CI files are written by the session of this repository, by the texts of architecture 3.3 .github (the 2026-10-21 amendment to R30 spec):
- the stage `toolchain` with the browsers, and the extension `biomejs.biome` with Biome as the default TypeScript formatter, come from the template's kind `typescript` (R79 spec; R12), and the base image is the template's (R13; R49);
- the tag and digest of the Playwright image and the browser revisions in it are those that `playwright` from the lock file expects, or `ci-test` does not find the browser. `spec` raises them in the template's `kinds/typescript.Dockerfile`, and `app` takes them by its next move to a tag; if `app` needs a new `playwright` sooner, it raises all three in its own Dockerfile and writes a row «в реестр spec» (R79 spec);
- `.github/workflows/ci.yml` — the TypeScript job by the shared `typescript.yml` of `.github` with the permission `id-token: write` and the input `engines: true` (the 2026-11-16 amendments to R14 and R45), and the job Mockup of its own: `just mockup-compare` in the stage `toolchain` by `.github`'s action `toolchain`, with the `spec` submodule, its snapshots and reports the artifact `mockup` (R58; section 3.9); `.github/workflows/codeql.yml` — CodeQL with the language `javascript-typescript`; `.github/codecov.yml`; every `uses:` by its SHA;
- Dependabot `npm` with the groups `vitest` — `@vitest/*`, `vite` and `@vitejs/plugin-react` —, `connect` and `firebase`, without major versions of `@types/node`, and with `@altnovatrail/spec`, `@altnovatrail/contracts`, `@bufbuild/protobuf`, the icon set and `playwright` in `ignore`, and `github-actions` with the group `shared-workflows` (R14, R15);
- `.github/workflows/release.yml` by the shared `release.yml` of `.github`, with its list of `paths` and the line in `RELEASE_LINE`, on `workflow_run` of CI, `pull_request` and the button, with `publish: ${{ github.event_name != 'pull_request' }}`: after green CI on `main` and on the button the run publishes the release, and on a pull request it is a trial that builds, signs and checks, publishing nothing (the 2026-11-12 amendment to R25).


### 3.2. Storage on the device

Decided by R04. Here: what `app` keeps on the device, where, how much and until when, what of it goes into the export, and how the learners of a shared device are kept apart. We collect only what the platform cannot work without (privacy 1 spec). There is no offline mode in the first slice (product 10 spec; Т-10), no reading of the work on the device (R66 spec) and no learner model: it lives in the database of `api`, and the engine computes on the server (R80 spec). The device holds only what an attempt, a lesson and the export need, and a cache, so as not to ask `api` for the same thing twice.

**Storage** — IndexedDB, by its own code behind a narrow interface, without a library:
- the profile database — each learner on the device has their own: attempts with the writing history and the submissions, an essay's among them, the state of lessons — the open view, the place and the state of the demo (section 3.4) —, and at the owner profile the own key kept on this device (section 3.6). `app` shows and writes only the selected learner's database, and opens the databases of other profiles only for housekeeping — the expiry, erasure and the page "This device". Deleting a profile is `deleteDatabase` of its database;
- the shared cache — the open views of the module manifest, the skill graph, the technique catalog and the solving steps, by the package digest of the attempt (R62 spec). This is modules' content, not the learner's data: `api` gives them without a token too (R03 api);
- the device registry — the ID of the account that signed in, and the list of profile databases, by which `app` finds the databases that are due to be erased; the device's voices — the voice an adult chose for each language, and the permission for voicing on Android and Linux (R07; section 3.5); Firebase's `projectId` and `apiKey` that the sign-in went with (R09; section 3.7); and the period after which the owner profile closes by itself (R11; section 3.6);
- the device settings — a small database of its own with the device's language that an adult chose (R10; section 3.8). This is neither the learner's data nor the account's: a sign-out and a sign-in of another account do not erase it, while "Erase everything on this device" and the browser itself do. The registry does not suit it — it is the account's, which "Erase everything on this device" and another account's sign-in erase, and the sign-in screen is seen before there is an account;
- the sign-in session is held by Firebase Auth in its own IndexedDB database: `app` sets it up through `initializeAuth` with `indexedDBLocalPersistence` alone, "LOCAL using indexedDB", not through `getAuth`, whose default storage the documentation names only as "platform-specific default dependencies". The session lives until a sign-out — "An explicit sign out is needed to clear that state" (sources 3.1) — and Firebase Auth's `signOut()` erases it;
- the media files of the device part `app` takes from `api` by a `fetch` request and gives to the canvas as a `Blob` — by the function `media` (R04; section 3.7); `LICENSE` and `NOTICE` it takes by the same `fetch` as text and shows on its own page of the module's license, as they are: a link would lead to another origin, the address of `api`, and `app` has no external links (privacy 7 spec; R32). The answers lie in the browser's HTTP cache: `fetch` goes through it, as `<img>` does (R03 api; sources 3.1, 7.1). `app` neither keeps nor erases them: the browser runs the HTTP cache, and `api` does not serve the files of a withdrawn version (R63 spec);
- `app` puts none of its own data in localStorage and sessionStorage: they hold 5 MiB per origin, they are synchronous, and Safari's seven days erase them just as they erase IndexedDB (sources 3.1). What Firebase Auth itself writes in them at a sign-in through Google its documentation does not say, so an erasure of the account clears them too — all of localStorage and sessionStorage of `app`'s origin;
- there is no PIN on the device — `api` holds its hash (R04 api). `app` gets the account's list of profiles from `api` after the sign-in and holds it in memory, and the selected learner is only in memory too: after a reload `app` starts again with the choice of the learner. The owner profile's PIN and its closing are section 3.6 (R08).

Each connection of `app` to IndexedDB closes on the event `versionchange`: otherwise `deleteDatabase` from this or another tab would wait for it, and "The blocked event fires if other clients still hold a connection to the database after their versionchange events have fired" (sources 3.1). `app` says "Erased" only on the success of `deleteDatabase`, and what it failed to erase — the database stayed in the registry — it erases again at the next launch, before it opens anything.

**How the work is written.**
- `app` appends the events `historychange` to the profile database by the key "attempt, event number". A storage whose `from` is not equal to the number of events written has missed events and reads `history` again (architecture 3.3 canvas).
- With each submission the canvas gives its context: on a handwriting sheet, where the crop lies on the sheet, its integers, the group boxes of the stroke groups, the table "group label → stroke IDs" and the submission's digest, and on a workbook, the table "box → stroke IDs" and the digest. A workbook's work is `{ cells, submission }` from `exportWork()`. By the digest the canvas accepts the context back, together with the marks of that submission (architecture 3.1 spec; R36 spec). The group boxes stay on the device: whether they go with the submission is Т-31.
- `app` keeps the history and the context as the canvas gave them, without transformations. IndexedDB stores a record by value, by structured cloning (sources 3.1), and, like JSON, does not round numbers, and the element would not accept back a context with rounded numbers (R36 spec; R27 canvas). A test in the browser checks that the history and the context pass through the element after a write and a read: the sheet opens as it was, and the marks with that context are laid on it.
- For each submission `app` keeps its context, the length of the history at the moment of the submission, and the work for checking itself — the PNG of the crop or the crops of the boxes. The context is needed while the marks of that submission may come (architecture 3.3 canvas), and the work for going over the course of the solution: the server does not store the picture, and the app sends it once more (architecture 4.1 spec).
- The pictures of the work lie as bytes — an `ArrayBuffer` with its type beside it — not as a `Blob`: WebKit in an ephemeral session does not write a `Blob` (R16; measurements 2). On reading it is a `Blob` again. A `Blob` of schema version 1 is read as it is, without migration; a record whose work the build cannot read gives "no work" and stays until the end of the attempt, the expiry or an erasure. The submissions are read by a transaction of their own, after the attempt and the writing history, and a record the browser will not give does not take them down with it; the work of a correct submission, too, is read before the erasing transaction, not in it.
- The cache is written with `durability: "relaxed"`, as IndexedDB advises for caches. The work — with the browser's default setting, without `"strict"`: that one waits for the write to disk at each stroke, and IndexedDB advises it only where the risk of loss matters more than the device's performance and battery (sources 3.1). A power failure right after a stroke may cost the last strokes — the learner sees them and will write them again.

**The end of an attempt, and the essay.**
- An attempt has ended when `api` has said that the task has ended — a correct answer, the showing of the task's solution, the attempt closed "unchecked" on a withdrawn version (R63 spec), or left unfinished: the learner left it or abandoned it when it had wrong answers (R82 spec) — or when the learner has begun the same task anew. An attempt without wrong answers that the learner left gives no evidence (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R80 spec), and on the device its work lies, like that of any unfinished one, for 30 days. If `api` closed the attempt itself, `app` learns of it by the contract and erases it, like any that has ended: the profile's open attempt and open runs it reads in `GetAccount` of `AccountService` (R98, R99 spec), and the end of an attempt comes in the answer to a submission, `Done` or `ShowSolution` of `AttemptService` (R97 spec).
- An essay is the work of a task whose answer slot is bound `sheet`: "the whole sheet is the answer: a work as a whole, such as an essay, which a rubric gives feedback on". The check is closed in the open view together with its kind, while the binding is open, and in format v0 `sheet` takes only `rubric` ([the format text](../spec/format/README.md), sections 14 and 15). So `app` knows an essay even before the check. The device keeps an essay as the work of any task, until the end of its attempt: its history, the context of each submission with the history's length then, and its step state, but neither the crop sent nor what was read nor the feedback, which the essay's screen holds while it is open (R53; R167 spec). The server stores neither the essay's text, nor the justifications, nor the words of the feedback — only how each submission ended and the marks of the criteria (R87 spec) —, so a page reloaded shows no feedback of an earlier submission.

**Works on the server** (R30; R113 spec; [the contracts' text](../spec/contracts/README.md), section 15, "Stored works"). While the profile's `work_storage` is on, `app` puts each work by `PutWork` of `WorkService` whenever it writes it to the device: at each submission of a task and of an essay, and as its attempt closes — read before the device lets the attempt go and sent after it, so that no screen waits for the network —, and switching the storage on in the section for adults puts every work the profile keeps here but an empty one. A work is `StoredWork`: the attempt's ID, the history as the JSON of its array in UTF-8, each submission as the JSON of its context with the history's length then, in order, and the picture that the canvas draws of the history by `exportPicture()` on an element off the page: the PNG of the strokes, black on white, 1000 × 1400 (R83 canvas). `api` sets the kind and the time. The sum of the bytes of the history, the contexts and the picture is at most 4 MiB, 4,194,304 bytes; a larger work does not go. A tab sends its works one after another in the order it read them, so that the last read stands, and a work it put already — the same history and contexts, by SHA-256 — does not go again. A `PutWork` that went through marks the attempt's record with how long a history and how many submissions the server keeps; `CAUSE_WORK_TOO_LARGE`, `resource_exhausted` with no refusal, any other refusal and a network that failed mark nothing, and the work is on this device alone until a later `PutWork` goes through; `CAUSE_STORAGE_OFF` reads the profile again by `GetAccount` for the session and the other tabs, and takes the marks off, as switching the storage off does: the server erased its copies, no other work goes until the profile says off, and a `PutWork` still going marks nothing; each start takes the marks off every profile whose storage `GetAccount` names off — switched off and on again elsewhere between two starts, they stay, Д36; `CAUSE_VERSION_NOT_GIVEN` asks no `CheckPackages`; an `api` with no `WorkService` answers `unimplemented`, and the work stays on the device alone. The page "This device" reads the marks (below). The list of the works on the server and the erasure of one are Д26; going on with an open attempt from the work another device put, `GetWork`, is Д27.

**What lies on the device** — for each learner of a shared device. "How much" is an order of magnitude: the size of the writing history is estimated, and measured with a mouse in Chromium (measurements 1), not with a pen or a finger (Д4). Beyond the column "When it is erased", everything in the profile database is also erased by the deletion of the profile, and everything by "Erase everything on this device", a sign-in of another account and the deletion of the account, while the owner's sign-out erases nothing; the events in order are below.

| What | Where | How much | When it is erased | In the export |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| the attempt's writing history | profile database | from hundreds of KB to a few MB per attempt; the measurement with a mouse — measurements 1 | the end of the attempt; an unfinished one — after 30 days without changes | yes, of an unfinished attempt |
| the attempt's submissions: the context, the length of the history at the moment of the submission, the work for checking | profile database | the context — a few KB, the work — tens of KB | together with the attempt; the work of a correct submission — when the correct answer words have come or their request has ended, and if `app` was closed earlier, at the next launch | the context — yes; the work — no: the history derives it |
| the attempt's step state: the attempt's mode, the step's place, the ticks of the chips, the IDs of the techniques given (R05; section 3.3) | profile database | bytes | the end of the attempt; for an unfinished one — together with its work: after 30 days without changes | yes, of an unfinished attempt |
| the open view of the task or the instance | profile database | a few KB | together with the attempt; a withdrawn version — at once | no: it is the module's content |
| a page from paper, from Start: its photo, its frames, and each task begun on it with its crop, the writing history, where the tracing paper lies, the contexts of its submissions and whether its attempt is over — no verdict, no key (R77; R162 spec) | profile database | the photo up to 4 MiB, a crop tens of KB, the history as an attempt's | with the profile: its deletion, "Erase the profile's data", "Erase everything on this device", a sign-in of another account; neither the end of an attempt nor the expiry erases it | yes, `paper/<id>/<page>.json` |
| the lesson's state — by the lesson's full ID and the package digest, with the run's ID beside them: the lesson's open view, the place — the segment the learner has reached — and the state of the demo of its examples (R06; section 3.4) | profile database | a few KB | the lesson ended — passed, "unchecked", "not suitable" or "abandoned"; 30 days without changes; the withdrawal of the version; the same lesson on another package; another run under the same key | the place and the state of the demo — yes; the open view — no |
| the open views of the manifest, the skill graph, the technique catalog and the solving steps | shared cache, by the package digest | tens of KB per package | 30 days without access; a withdrawn version — at once | no |
| media files, `LICENSE`, `NOTICE` | the browser's HTTP cache | by the package | by the browser's rules | no |
| the sign-in session | the Firebase Auth database | a few KB | the sign-out; a revoked sign-in | no |
| the heartbeat records of `@firebase/app` — the SDK's versions and the days when the SDK ran, up to 30 (section 3.7) | its own database `firebase-heartbeat-database` | bytes | when the SDK has sent them with a request to Firebase Auth; "Erase everything on this device" and the browser itself | no: it is not the learner's data |
| the device registry | a database of its own | bytes | "Erase everything on this device"; the account gone; a sign-in of another account names its own account in it | no |
| the device's voices — the voice an adult chose for each language, and the permission for voicing on Android and Linux (R07; section 3.5) | the device registry, the key `voicing` (R36) | bytes | "Erase everything on this device"; the account gone; a sign-in of another account | no: it is not the learner's data |
| the period for closing the owner profile — 5, 15, 30 or 60 minutes of a hidden page (R11; R08; section 3.6) | the device registry | bytes | with the registry: "Erase everything on this device"; the account gone | no: it is not the learner's data |
| the device's language that an adult chose (R10; section 3.8) | the device settings | bytes | "Erase everything on this device"; the sign-out and a sign-in of another account — no | no: it is not the learner's data |
| the own key — the provider, the key and its last four characters (R08; section 3.6) | the owner profile's database, only with the mark "Keep on this device"; without it — the tab's memory, while the owner profile is open | bytes | "Remove key" — on the page "Own AI key" or "This device" —, a replacement and the mark taken off; without the mark — the closing of the owner profile | no: it is the owner's secret, not the learner's data |

The state of the demo of another version of the module never reaches the element: a lesson's state belongs to a run on one package, and a lesson on another package starts from the beginning (R06; section 3.4).

**How much space.**
- Quotas: Chromium gives an origin up to 60% of the disk; Safari 17 and newer, about 60%; Firefox without persistent storage, the lesser of 10% of the disk and 10 GiB (sources 3.1). A quota is an estimate, not a promise: a write beyond it is `QuotaExceededError`.
- Reduced modes: in Chrome's incognito, about 5% of the disk; with the setting "Clear cookies and site data when you close all windows", about 300 MB; and private modes erase the data at the end of the session (sources 3.1).
- The estimate of the writing history: a point is `x`, `y`, `t` and `p` (`@altnovatrail/spec/canvas`), about 40–70 bytes in a record; a stroke is tens of points, because the canvas writes no point closer than 0.1 unit to the previous one (architecture 3.1 canvas); a work is up to hundreds of strokes. That is from hundreds of KB to a few MB per attempt — orders of magnitude below the quota. `app` sets no limit of its own: the end of the attempt and the 30-day expiry hold the volume.
- The measurement with a mouse in Chromium is within this estimate: a work of hundreds of strokes is hundreds of KB, and no limit of its own is needed (measurements 1); a pen and a finger give more points a second, and their measurement is Д4.
- If a write did not go through, `app` tells the learner that the work was not saved on the device, and the page "This device" tells the adult.

**What is erased when** — by events:
- the end of an attempt — its writing history, its submissions, the step state and the task's open view, of an essay too (R53). The work of a correct submission `app` erases not at once but when the correct answer words have come or their request has ended: the work goes with that request, although "correct" has already closed the attempt. If `app` was closed earlier, the next launch erases it: a repeat of the request would not bring the words (R04);
- the expiry — at launch `app` goes through the databases of all the profiles in the registry and erases the unfinished attempts, but for the pages from paper, and the lessons that have not changed for 30 days, and from the shared cache the records it has not accessed for 30 days, as R42 canvas-api does;
- a lesson ended — passed, "unchecked", "not suitable" or "abandoned" — its state: the open view, the place and the state of the demo. The lesson's state on the former digest is erased the same way when `api` gave the same lesson on another package (section 3.4);
- the withdrawal of a version — its open views from the shared cache and from the profile databases, and the state of the lessons on its digest, as soon as `app` has learned of the withdrawal from `api` (R63 spec): about withdrawal it asks `CheckPackages` of `PackageService` at launch and after the refusal `CAUSE_VERSION_NOT_GIVEN`, the answer `410`, and the end of a run or of an attempt "unchecked" (R99 spec);
- the account owner's sign-out — the sign-in session alone: the databases of the profiles, the shared cache, the registry and the web storage stay, and the same account's next sign-in opens them, its unfinished works that are not on the server too (П-8; R04). It asks nothing; a `signOut()` that failed leaves the session where it was;
- a sign-in of another account — if the registry holds another account ID, `app` erases everything of the former one before it opens anything. The device settings are not the account's data, and it does not touch them (section 3.8). A user signed in without a NovaTrail account gives no account ID: `api` answers them with the refusal `CAUSE_NO_ACCESS`, "no access" — and if the registry names an account at that time, this is a sign-in of another account too, and `app` erases what is left of the former one the same way (the comment of `AccountService` in `novatrail.api.v1`; R98 spec; R04). The registry holds one account, so several accounts are not kept apart on one device (Д44);
- a `projectId` or an `apiKey` in `/deployment.json` other than in the registry — the Firebase Auth database and the origin's web storage, before `initializeAuth`, while the learners' data stays until the sign-in: if another account has signed in, the rule above erases it (section 3.7);
- the deletion of a profile on this device — its database, at once;
- the deletion of a profile on another device — at launch `app` compares the profile databases with the account's list of profiles from `api` and erases the extra ones. It erases only by an answer of `GetAccount` that has come, and for the account that the device registry names: a network failure erases nothing (R98 spec);
- the deletion of the account — `api` answers that the account is gone, and `app` erases everything of the account and signs out (architecture 4.5 spec), while the device settings stay;
- the buttons of the page "This device" — a profile's data or everything, and at the owner profile also the own key; "Erase everything on this device" erases the device settings too;
- "Remove key" — on the page "Own AI key" or "This device" —, a replacement of the key and the mark "Keep on this device" taken off — the own key (section 3.6);
- the browser itself — when space is short, after seven days in Safari without the app being touched, or when the user cleared the site's data. The browser erases the origin's storage whole (sources 3.1), so after that `app` starts with the sign-in. While `app` is open, this shows by the event `close` of the IndexedDB connection. But that event also comes on corrupted data or an I/O error (sources 3.1), so `app` opens the registry anew: no registry — the data on this device was erased, and `app` says just that; the registry is there — the data could not be read, and `app` says that, erasing nothing.

**The learners of a shared device.** Each one's data is in their own database, and `app` shows only the selected learner's database. This is `app`'s own order, not a security boundary: the origin is one, and whoever holds the device can read all the databases with the browser's tools. The PIN is a barrier of the interface (R07 canvas-api; R04 api), and what cannot be undone is confirmed by a fresh sign-in (architecture 4.5 spec).

**The export from this device** — the column "In the export": unfinished attempts with the contexts of their submissions and the step state, an unfinished essay's among them — the device's file of version 4 has its field `essays` written empty (R167 spec) —, and for unfinished lessons, the place and the state of the demo; and the pages from paper, each its file `paper/<id>/<page>.json` of version 4, `src/paper/export.ts`, while the rest of the export waits for epic E4 (R77). The cache, the session, the registry, the work for checking and the own key do not go into the export: these are modules' content, housekeeping data, what is derived from the writing history, or the owner's secret (R08). Nor does the export say anything of the mark "Keep on this device". Works from other devices are exported from them (architecture 4.5 spec). The format of the export is the epic E4 of `spec`, and the first app does not need it (R09 spec, R76 spec).

**The page "This device"** — `#/adults/device`, in the section for adults, which opens without a PIN (R29; R04):
- for each profile, the owner first — the number of unfinished tasks and "about" how many bytes the profile's data takes: `app`'s estimate by the records of its database — strings in UTF-8, a number 8 bytes, a picture by its own size. The browser does not name the space of one database. A profile without a database on the device — "no data"; a database that cannot be read — "can't be read", and it can be erased even then. A database whose erasure waits for the browser — "not all of it was erased", and the button stays, to erase again;
- at each profile — a warning that its works are only on this device and will be lost if the site's data is erased (R113 spec). It goes by works: `copiedOnServer` in `DevicePage.tsx` lifts it only while the profile's `work_storage` is on and every work the device keeps of it — each attempt — is marked as kept on the server as far as the device holds it, or the device keeps nothing of it; a work over 4 MiB, a refusal and a network that failed leave it (R30; "Works on the server" above);
- for the whole site — the estimate `navigator.storage.estimate()` (sources 3.1); `navigator.storage.persisted()` and the button `persist()`, while the browser has not agreed, and for a browser without this API neither the line nor the button. An adult presses it, and the first sign-in calls it once by itself: Firefox asks with a window, while Chrome and Safari decide by themselves, by how the site is used (sources 3.1). If the browser refused, the page says just that: the data is kept, but the browser may erase it;
- a warning: the browser may erase the site's data by itself when space is short, and Safari after seven days without the app being touched. The advice is to add the app to the Home Screen: its web app is exempt from the seven days. The data of the web app from the Home Screen lives apart from Safari, so one has to sign in there anew, and the unfinished works from the tab will not move there (sources 3.1). The warning stands with persistent storage too: whether `persist()` saves a Safari tab from the seven days the primary source does not say (sources 3.1);
- "Erase the profile's data on this device" — its database through the registry, and what the browser did not erase, the next launch erases;
- "Erase everything on this device" — the sign-out, then everything of the account, as at its deletion — the databases of the profiles, the shared cache, the web storage and the registry last —, and beyond that the device settings and the heartbeat database of `@firebase/app`. Its connection is held by the SDK, whose `openDB` sets no handler of `versionchange`, so the deletion is `blocked` while the tab is open and goes through when the connection closes (R16). After the erasure — the sign-in screen and "home";
- after the profiles, "Voicing" — the device's voices by language and the permission on Android and Linux (section 3.5);
- at the owner profile, if the key is kept on this device — the line "The own key is kept on this device" with the provider and the last four characters, and the button "Remove key" — the same as on the page "Own AI key" (section 3.6).
