Every erasure asks first, and the question takes the focus and gives it back (section 3.9). The device's language is on the first page of the section (R29). The closing period of the owner profile is not on the page: the section has no PIN. At the account's first sign-in on this device the choice of the learner calls the same `persist()` itself, with no line or button (R04).

**Code** (R16):
- the databases: `novatrail-settings` — the device's language; `novatrail-registry` — the account, the sign-in project and the account's databases; `novatrail-cache` — the open views by `[digest, view]`; `novatrail-profile-<profile ID>` — the attempts, the writing history by `[attempt ID, n]`, the submissions, the work of a correct submission until the correct answer words, the lessons by lesson ID, the store `ownKey` with its one record `key`, the own key kept on this device, which `usage` does not count (R47), and the pages from paper: `pages` by page ID with the index `takenAt`, `photos` by page ID and `paperTasks` by attempt ID with the index `page` (R77). Their versions and the table of the stores, keys and indexes are in R16 and `src/storage/schema.ts`;
- the interface — `src/storage/backend.ts`: the operations of IndexedDB that `app` takes. Its implementations are `src/storage/idb.ts` on the browser's IndexedDB and `src/storage/memory.ts`, a storage in memory for the tests in Node, with IndexedDB's order of keys, the cloning of records and the rollback of a failed transaction; it can also be a store whose records the browser does not give (`unreadable`), and the tests in Node check that the attempt and the history come without the submissions. `src/storage/device.ts` holds one connection per database and says that a connection was taken away or that a database is newer than the build;
- the rules — `src/storage/registry.ts`, `profile.ts`, `cache.ts`, `settings.ts`, `erase.ts` — the erasure events that go beyond one database — and `start.ts` — the launch. Each write of the work says whether it was saved, and the task screen tells the learner "Your work isn't saved on this device" (section 3.3, "Code"). In `profile.ts`, `keptWork` and `workOf` give the work as bytes and read both forms, and `usage` and `profileUsage` count for the page; in `erase.ts`, `eraseProfile` and `eraseDevice`, and "Erase everything" is `Session.eraseEverything()`; in `browser.ts`, `siteStorage`: `estimate()`, `persisted()` and `persist()`; the page is `src/adults/DevicePage.tsx`;
- the launch — in `src/boot/Boot.tsx`, after `/deployment.json` has been read and before the app, and the app gets the storage through `useStorage()`. What to do with a connection that the browser or another tab took away is decided by `src/boot/storage.ts`: the browser closed the connection — the screen "The data on this device was erased" or "The data on this device can't be read"; another tab opened the database at a new version — an offer to reload; erased it — everything erased, a deleted profile — nothing (R16). The launch is one per tab, under StrictMode too;
- the open views, the cache and the files of the device part — `src/packages/` (R16):
  - `views.ts` — a view from the shared cache, otherwise from `api`, and then into the cache. `keptManifests` gives, in one pass and without a request, the manifests that the cache holds — per module, of the latest version — and by them the module cards of Home and Subjects name a module without a current version; and one and the same view asked for twice at once `api` gets in one request. The form of a cache record is R16. The build's window of versions is `FORMATS` of `views.ts`: a view of another version does not go into the cache, and `app` offers to reload, and a view that the cache holds in a version outside the window is thrown away, and `app` asks `api` anew. Only a view that was read as JSON goes into the cache, and a view of a package whose withdrawal this tab has already learned of does not. A request waits 30 s by its own signal, not by a header. `CAUSE_VERSION_NOT_GIVEN` from any service calls `CheckPackages` — this is the client's event `withdrawn`; before the sign-in there is no cache, and the view is simply read;
  - `checks.ts` — `CheckPackages` about the digests whose open views lie in the shared cache and in the profile databases — without the attempts whose task view the withdrawal has already taken — at the launch of `app`, once the storage has started, and on the signs of withdrawal. What was withdrawn is erased (`versionsWithdrawn`), and about a digest that `api` does not know `app` asks again after 60 s. One check at a time, and a reason that comes during it gives one more after it;
  - `files.ts` — a file of the part at the address of `api` `/packages/sha256-<hex>/device/<path>`, each segment of the path percent-encoded, and a path with an empty segment, `.` or `..` is not a path of the part; `fetch` without headers: `200` — a `Blob`, `404` — no such path, `410` — the version was withdrawn, and `CheckPackages`, `503` — a repeat after `Retry-After`, but no later than 30 s, and without it and on a network failure, a repeat after the client's growing pauses; a body cut off on the way is a network failure too. `media(digest)` gives the canvas the function `media`, and `text(digest, path)` gives `LICENSE` and `NOTICE` as UTF-8 text, as they are, and `404` — "no file"; `license.ts` reads both one after the other for the page of the module's license, and `410` — "the version is no longer given" and one `CheckPackages` (R32; section 3.10, "Licenses of modules");
- the sign-in and the account — `src/account/session.ts` (R19): after each sign-in the registry remembers the sign-in project, and by the answer of `GetAccount` the former account, what the registry holds for erasure and the profiles not in the list are erased before the app opens, with "Try again" while another tab holds a database; on "no access" the client erases (R18), a network failure erases nothing. The sign-out, `Session.signOut()`, is the sign-in service's `signOut()` alone and gives whether it went through; `eraseEverything()` is `signOut()`, then `eraseAccount` and `eraseDevice`, and erases nothing if the sign-in service did not sign out. The first sign-in — the registry has not named this account before — has the choice of the learner call `persist()`, with no line (R04);
- the erasure events are called where `app` learns of them: the withdrawal of a version — `src/packages/checks.ts`; the sign-in, "Erase everything" and the account — `src/account/session.ts` and `src/boot/Boot.tsx`; the end of an attempt, a correct answer and a closed attempt — `src/task/Task.tsx` and `src/next/useEngine.ts`, by `attemptOver` (section 3.3, "Code"); the end of a lesson — `src/lesson/Lesson.tsx`, by `Profile.lessonEnded` (section 3.4, "Code"). The attempt's step state is `steps` of its record, whose form `src/task/steps.ts` reads (R20);
- the essay (R53): in `profile.ts`, `addEssaySubmission` appends a submission of the essay's attempt with its context and the history's length, without its crop; the attempt's end, the term and a withdrawal go as for any attempt. The expiry also erases every history whose attempt the device keeps no more, as an essay ended under schema versions 3 and 4 left it. Schema version 5 takes off the store `essays` of versions 3 and 4 with every essay in it (R16);
- the works on the server (R16 and R30): in `profile.ts`, `work` reads a work as the device holds it — the history and the submissions with their contexts —, `works` names every attempt the profile keeps, `markStored` puts the mark `stored: { history, submissions }` on the attempt's record, `clearStored` takes every mark off, and `usage` says by `stored` whether the server keeps all of the profile's works as far as the device holds them; `attemptOver` with `works` reads the work before the erasure and sends it after. `src/works/stored.ts` builds `StoredWork` and holds the 4 MiB, the answers and the tab's queue, `createSender` with the storage as the tab knows it; `src/works/picture.ts` draws the picture by the canvas; `src/works/useWorks.ts` is the sender of the learner chosen, for `Task.tsx`, `Essay.tsx` and `useEngine.ts`, and `src/adults/WorkStorage.tsx` makes its own for switching on and off. Tests: `stored.node.test.ts` — the form, the limit, each answer and its marks, the order of the sends; `picture.browser.test.tsx` — the canvas's PNG and a work of the form `api` checks; the screens in their own tests;
- the pages from paper (R77): in `profile.ts`, `keepPage` writes a page at Start with its frames, its photo once and the tasks begun, a task begun anew in its earlier attempt's place, and erases the oldest pages past `MAX_PAGES` (R170 spec), `keepPaperWork` a task's work, `pages` lists them newest first with no photo read, `photo` reads a page's photo alone for its thumbnail, and `page` reads one whole; `src/paper/PaperContext.tsx` writes a task's work half a second after it changes and at once when the page goes or the tab is hidden, and `open` puts a page of the history on the screen of solving; "Your pages" is `src/paper/YourPages.tsx`, which makes each row's thumbnail with `thumbnailOf` of `src/paper/capture.ts`, and where a page the device keeps no more leaves the list and the line why stays until the pop-over closes, the last page too; `src/paper/export.ts` builds the page's file of the export. Tests: `profile.node.test.ts`, `export.node.test.ts`, `YourPages.browser.test.tsx`, which shows the thumbnails, their skeleton and a blank sheet, and `PaperScreen.browser.test.tsx`, which keeps a page with its work, opens it again from "Your pages" and finds one gone;
- `src/storage/idb.browser.test.ts` and `src/storage/canvas.browser.test.tsx` hold in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox, in CI (R45): an opening without creation does not set up a database — an interrupted upgrade of a new database does not leave it behind; `versionchange` closes the connection, and a deletion and a new version from another connection go through; a connection that does not close by itself gives `blocked`; bytes and numbers pass a write and a read without rounding; a workbook's work goes into IndexedDB as bytes with a type and is read as a `Blob`, and a `Blob` of schema version 1 is read — in Chromium and Firefox only: a context of Playwright is an ephemeral session, where WebKit writes no `Blob` (measurements 2); the writing history and the submission context pass through the canvas element after IndexedDB, and the element rejects a context with rounded numbers. What a device alone shows is Д13.

### 3.3. Solving steps

Decided by R05. `app` leads the solving steps itself, on the device: the steps and their exit conditions are in the open view of the solving steps, the chips "givens and unknown" in the open view of the task, and no condition needs the key (architecture 3.1 spec; R57 spec). The steps do not call AI: the work is read only on "Check", and "Step done" does not read it (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R69 spec).

**Where the steps come from.** The open view of the solving steps `api` gives by the package digest of the attempt, once per package, and it lies in the shared cache (R62 spec; section 3.2). The types are `SolvingSteps` and `Step` from `@altnovatrail/spec/formatv0/open`: a step has `title`, `gives` and `exit_condition`, and an optional `description`, how to go through the step, in the module's explanation language ([the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 18; R108 spec). The steps are one set for all the tasks of a module, a task cannot replace them, and in a module without steps the tasks are solved without them.

**The screen in the mode "by steps".** At the top of the tutor's panel stands the step's card (R67):
- "Step n of m", which unfolds the list of the steps — the titles in order, with the place of each: passed, current and not yet open. A tap on a passed step returns to it, and only "Step done" leads forward;
- the current step — its title, and beside it its `description`, if it has one — with its own `lang`, like all the module's content (section 3.8) —, what the step gives — the chips and the technique cards — and a line of the interface on what will open the button (R108 spec);
- the button "Step done" — at every step but the last. The last one ends on "Check": it has no next step, and its exit condition locks nothing — `app` shows the line of that condition, and `answer_slots_filled` opens "Check". In the format examples the last step is `none`;

The canvas is open at every step: a step does not lock the work, and the learner writes when they want. There are no timers and no automatic advance (product 7 spec).

**Exit conditions** — the lock of the button "Step done":

| Condition | When the button is open | Event on the device | `app`'s line on the step |
|---|---|---|---|
| `givens_ticked` | all the `givens` and `unknown` that the task has are ticked; for a task without them the step passes by itself | a tap on a chip in the panel | Mark what we know and what to find |
| `answer_slots_filled` | there is something in each answer slot | the canvas's `historychange` — `app` reads `filled` again; a tap on an option of `choice` | Write an answer in each slot |
| `none` | at once | — | none |

`answer_slots_filled` — by the slot binding (the second 2026-11-01 amendment to R36 spec; R42 canvas):
- `anywhere` and `sheet` — the slot is the only one in the task, and the condition is `filled[0][0]`: whether the sheet has a stroke that `exportWork()` would take. It is met with the first stroke, so it only opens the button and does not lead further by itself;
- `choice` — its own choice of an option: `filled` of a sheet with only `choice` slots is `null`;
- a workbook box — `filled[item − 1][box − 1]`, at each box of each line;
- `zone` is taken by no module, and the canvas element does not accept a task with it.

`filled` changes before `historychange`, and assigning `task` and `history` changes it without an event, so `app` reads it also after it has opened the task. `app`'s lines on the step are the interface: their language, rules and words are section 3.8, and they are written without gender and without addressing the learner (R10).

**The chips** are shown and ticked by `app` (architecture 3.9 canvas): `givens` as a list, `unknown` apart, as the module gave them. A tap ticks a chip, a second tap unticks it. There is no wrong tick: the chips retell the statement ([the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 14). For a task with only `givens` or only `unknown` the step waits for those there are. If a step gives `givens` but has another condition, the chips are visible, but ticking them is not required.

**Technique.** When a step whose `gives` gives `techniques` begins, `app` names to `api` the attempt and the step's place. `api` answers with the IDs of the task's techniques — empty if the task has no techniques — and records which technique it gave ([the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 19; R57, R82 spec). A step that gives only `givens` does not go to `api`: "givens and unknown" are open, `app` shows them from the task's open view, and `api` does not record that the learner saw them (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R05). A technique is closed, and `app` does not know whether the task has one. The names and descriptions `app` takes from the open technique catalog in the cache (R62 spec) and shows them as a card on the step; empty — the step shows only its title. The IDs of the techniques given `app` keeps with the attempt, and a return to the step and a relaunch do not ask `api` for them anew. In the contract this is `BeginStep` of `AttemptService`, and "Step done" sends no request (R97 spec).

**Modes.**
- The default mode is set by an adult for each profile on the profile page of the section for adults (R29; section 3.10), and for a new profile it is "by steps". Each task begins in it: `app` takes it from the profile of the selected learner as `GetAccount` or the answer of `UpdateProfile` gave it — in this tab or in another, by `BroadcastChannel` — and an attempt that has no step state on this device yet begins in it. An attempt already begun keeps its own mode.
- The learner switches the mode on the task screen at any moment (product 4 spec). Their choice holds until the end of the attempt and is kept with its step state, and it does not change the default mode. The steps are kept meanwhile: back in "by steps", the learner goes on from the step where they were.
- The default mode is a field of its own in the learner's profile on the server, not part of the presentation style (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R05; privacy 4 spec; concepts 15 spec). It follows the learner to any device, and does not go to AI; in the contract it is a field of the profile of `AccountService` (R98 spec).
- "Self" — there is no steps panel, a technique comes only on request, and the tutor answers only when called ([the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 18; concepts 10 spec). On the button the mode is called «Самостоятельно»: in Russian «сам» gives away the learner's gender (section 3.8).
- A module without steps has no switch: the screen is as in the mode "self".

**"Check", the pause and the hint.**
- "Check" — in both modes and at any step. It is open when the same holds as for `answer_slots_filled`: an empty sheet and empty boxes do not go to AI. A tap on an option of `choice` only chooses, and "Check" sends: the value goes to `api` and is checked without AI (architecture 4.2 spec).
- The pause — 6 s without a stroke: "Check" blinks, if it is open, in both modes. Neither reading nor AI (R69 spec). One blink per pause — shorter than 5 s and no more than 3 times a second — and it comes again only after a new stroke or a choice of an option. With `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` there is a still highlight instead, and the blinking says nothing to a screen reader (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R05; section 3.9). "Step done" does not blink on a pause: at the step "solve" with a slot `anywhere` it is open from the first stroke, and the blinking would call onward before the answer.
- "Hint" — always, in both modes, from the start of the task. It does not send the work (R69 spec). By R82 spec:
  - before the first wrong answer — "givens and unknown" from the task's open view, the technique and the task's leading question, `leading_question`, which `api` gives: the request `GetHint` goes for them, and `api` records what was given. The leading question is a closed field of the format, only this answer carries it, and empty means the task has none; `app` shows it beside the technique, with its own `lang`. The open attempt holds it in `requested_leading_question`, and when the attempt is continued — after a relaunch and on another device — `app` shows it from there. After the first wrong answer the question is no longer given (R108 spec; the 2026-11-08 amendment to R82 spec; [the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 14; [the contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md), section 15);
  - after a wrong answer — the words of the current rung, which the attempt keeps, once more, without AI, and if there are none, the rung's fallback text. When the rung's words were not asked because the tutor's panel was closed, "Hint" opens the panel, and the opening asks them once, by AI and with the work, in place of `GetHint` (the 2026-11-17 amendment to R82 spec);
  - "Explain differently" — the same rung in other words: one call of AI per rung, without the work, with the task's past questions. A second time on the same rung `api` does not give it, and in the mode without AI (О-5) there is no button.

**After the check.**
- "Not yet" — the learner stays on their step. `app` asks for the tutor's words, sending the work once more — only while the tutor's panel is open, or once when it opens (the second 2026-11-17 amendment to R148 spec, the 2026-11-17 amendment to R97 spec) —, and shows the rung's question and the marks on the tutor's layer (architecture 4.1 spec). One can go back to the plan and the technique with a tap on the steps bar.
- "Unreadable" — with a reason, and the learner writes it again (R56 spec; concepts 9.1 spec). The reasons are the format's `blank` and `notation` and four more from the observation: `illegible` — cannot be made out, `ambiguous` — reads two ways, `no_final_answer` — no final answer, and `several_final_answers` — several final answers (R86 spec; [the contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md), section 10). The words of each are by R10, and a reason that the build does not know is a general line. An AI failure or a quota used up is a neutral phrase (R04 spec).
- From the fourth wrong answer, when the hint ladder has been passed, `app` offers a child to call an adult or to show the solution, and to a learner of the category `adult` the button "Show the solution" is visible from the start of the attempt (R82 spec). "Call a grown-up" is the interface of `app`: `api` notifies no one. If the adult's setting "ask before showing" is on for the child — by default it is on —, the showing goes as an adult action behind the PIN of the section for adults, and `api` rejects the learner's request with the reason "an adult is needed", and `app` says just that. The showing closes the attempt with the outcome "solution shown", and only then or after a correct answer does the task's solution come to the device (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R05 spec).
- Whether the task has ended `api` decides: a correct answer, the showing of the task's solution, "unchecked" on a withdrawn version (R63 spec). The steps panel closes, and the screen after the check follows (section 3.10).
- An essay — reading and feedback by the rubric, without a verdict (R75 spec). A submission ends with feedback, "unreadable" — `blank` or `illegible` — or "unchecked", and the attempt stays open: the learner writes again and closes it themselves with the button "Done", open after at least one submission — and then the task is solved (R87 spec). Feedback without words — they did not pass the leak check or the rule of the feedback — `app` shows with its own phrase "no feedback this time", not with the neutral one ([the contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md), section 15). The marks of the criteria and their justifications are not on the device, and the adult sees the marks of the criteria in progress (R87 spec).

**The task screen in the contract** — `AttemptService` (R97 spec; [the contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md), section 15):
- a submission — `SubmitWork`, and for an essay `SubmitEssay`; the rung's words — `GetRungWords`, "explain differently" — `ExplainDifferently`, a hint — `GetHint`, a technique — `BeginStep`, showing the solution — `ShowSolution`, the correct answer words — `GetCorrectAnswerWords`, "Done" — `Done`;
- a refusal — the detail `Refusal` of a Connect error, and the AI call outcome — `Outcome` in the answer. A cause that the build does not know `app` reads by the error's code, and such an outcome as an AI failure;
- the request for the rung's words carries the number of the wrong answer, `wrong_answers` from the answer to the submission, and the submission's work: the server does not store the crop. The work of a correct submission `app` holds until the correct answer words, although "correct" closes the attempt (R04);
- a mark's name — by its kind, number and place (section 3.8), and `app` gives the marks to the canvas element by the property `marks` together with the submission context that it kept (R74 spec);
- the idempotency key — `idempotency_key` — is carried by the requests that must not be done twice: from the task screen `SubmitWork`, `SubmitEssay`, `GetRungWords`, `GetCorrectAnswerWords`, `ExplainDifferently` and `ShowSolution`, and also `ChooseNext`, `BeginAttempt`, `RequestChildConsent` and the PIN requests. `app` makes a key for each such action, and each repeat of it goes with the same key, while `GetHint`, `BeginStep` and `Done` carry no key and repeat by the state of the attempt. A repeat of what is done already gets what was recorded, without AI, and where the server does not store the words — for an essay, the rung's words and the correct answer words — an answer marked `repeated` and without words; the clock offset goes only with requests to platform AI (R118 spec). The client that keeps these rules is section 3.7, "Code" (R18).

**The step state** — the attempt's mode, the step's place, the ticks of the chips and the IDs of the techniques given — lies with the attempt in the profile database, and is erased and exported as section 3.2 says. After a relaunch the learner goes on in the same mode and from the step where they were. Each task of a lesson is an attempt of its own (architecture 3.5 spec) and begins from the first step.

**The path through the steps of the three modules of the first slice** — the steps from the format examples (`spec/format/examples/solving-steps/valid/`) on the tasks of concepts 16.1, 16.4 and 16.5 spec. No condition needs the key: `givens_ticked` looks at open fields, `answer_slots_filled` at `filled` and its own choice, `none` at nothing.

Olympiad maths, `olympiad-math.yaml`, the task `ladder.yaml` — a slot `anywhere`, two chips `givens`, `unknown`, and the techniques `draw` and `small-numbers` (concepts 16.1 spec):

| Step | Exit condition | Event on the device | What the tutor says | To `api` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1. Understand the task, `gives: [givens]` | `givens_ticked` | a tap on the chips "The rungs are 30 cm apart.", "It is 180 cm from the lowest rung to the highest." and "How many rungs there are." | the step's title, the chips, the line "Mark" | — |
| 2. Make a plan, `gives: [techniques]` | `none` | — | the title and the cards "Draw the problem" and "Start with small numbers" from the catalog | the start of step 2 — the answer: `draw`, `small-numbers` |
| 3. Solve it | `answer_slots_filled` | the first stroke on the sheet: `filled` is `[[true]]` | the title, the line "Write an answer" | — |
| 4. Check the answer | `none` | — | the title; the button is "Check" | on "Check" — the sheet's crop for reading (architecture 4.1 spec) |

School maths of grade 3, `school-math-grade-3.yaml`, the task `workbook-page.yaml` — five lines of the workbook and six boxes, without chips and techniques (concepts 16.4 spec):

| Step | Exit condition | Event on the device | What the tutor says | In `api` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1. Fill in the boxes | `answer_slots_filled` | a stroke in each box: `filled[item − 1][box − 1]` — the line "47 ÷ 6 = □ r □" has two boxes | the title, the line "write an answer" | — |
| 2. Check each line, `gives: [techniques]` | `none` | — | the title; the task has no techniques — there are no cards; the button is "Check" | the start of step 2 — the answer is empty; on "Check" — the crops of the filled boxes (R53 spec) |

Short essays in English, `short-essays.yaml`, the tasks `essay.yaml` — an answer slot `sheet` with a rubric — and `best-first-sentence.yaml` — an answer slot `choice` (concepts 16.5 spec). The module has one set of steps, so the task with a choice, too, goes through "Plan the essay" and "Write it":

| Step | Exit condition | Event on the device | What the tutor says | In `api` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1. Plan the essay, `gives: [techniques]` | `none` | — | the title; the example tasks have no techniques — there are no cards | the start of step 1 — the answer is empty |
| 2. Write it | `none` | — | the title | — |
| 3. Read it again | `none` | — | the title; the button is "Check", enabled when the sheet has a stroke (`sheet`) or an option is chosen (`choice`) | on "Check": for `essay`, the reading of the essay and the assessment by the rubric (R75 spec), for `best-first-sentence`, the value of the option, without AI (architecture 4.2 spec) |

The example tasks of the workbook and of the essays have no techniques, though the steps of these modules give them: such a step shows only its title. The step "pronounce" of the language steps will come with voice input (concepts 7 spec; О-6), and the state of a widget as a new exit condition, from stage 4 (architecture 3.4 spec).

**Where the prototype differs** ([the prototype's README](../spec/draft/README.md)): it reads the answer on a pause, which R69 spec rejected, goes only forward, puts "Check" after the correct answer, lets only the adult set the mode, and has "Show me how to start", which format v0 lacks (R57 spec). The documents take precedence.

**Code** (R20):
- the entry — `src/task/`: the screen `#/task` opens an attempt whose ID is only in memory (`AttemptContext.tsx`): the open attempt outside a lesson that `GetAccount` named — "Continue the task" on Home — or a new one by "Try it again", `BeginAttempt` with `previous_attempt_id`. A new task by the engine choice is begun by "Next task" or "What's next" (R24), and a lesson's task by the lesson player (section 3.4, "Code"): without them `BeginAttempt` has nothing to begin with (sources 7.5). Without an attempt, `#/task` leads home in its own place in the history — "Back" will not return here — and an ended one stays on the screen with its result until the learner leaves (`TaskRoute.tsx`). What the screen learned about the attempt — that it began or closed it — is kept with the profile that `GetAccount` gave, and after "Switch learner" too;
- reading — `Task.tsx`: `GetOpenAttempt` — the task's open view in the build's window of versions (`OpenViews.parseView`) and the package digest, by which the manifest, the solving steps and the technique catalog come from the shared cache; from the profile's database, the writing history and the state of the steps, and without a record on this device, a new record with a clean sheet (`resumed` in `attempt.ts`). The number of wrong answers is always the one that `api` holds; if it is not the one recorded here, there was a submission on another device, and the options are taken from the last submission; the step techniques that `api` holds fill the steps that have none recorded; and a verdict stands only at the option whose verdict it is. After the end of the attempt the screen writes nothing more. `CAUSE_ATTEMPT_CLOSED` — the screen "This task is already over" with the reason and "Try it again", the attempt's work on the device is erased, and for "unchecked" also `CheckPackages`; `CAUSE_NOT_FOUND` — home;
- the screen — `TaskScreen.tsx` in `TaskLayout.tsx` (R65): the top bar — Back "Back to Home", the title, the profile's pill and the mode's —, the canvas over the whole window, and the foot — the lines, the verdict's card `VerdictCard` and the row Choose an answer · Hint · Show the solution | Check. The bar and the foot float over the canvas, a pointer reaching the sheet between their pills, and the canvas takes the room they cover as `--novatrail-canvas-pad-top` and `-bottom` (R73 canvas) — but a task from paper, with no statement, whose sheet runs up under the top bar (R71); the tutor's panel and the debug window open over it and move nothing (R67, R72). In a lesson the pill stands at the end of the lesson's bar, `AtTopEnd` (R64);
- the tutor's panel — `TaskLayout.tsx` (R67): an `<aside>` "Tutor" of glass, 400 wide at the window's right over the canvas, which keeps the window's width, the lines above it and the foot keeping clear of it (`data-beside`); below 600 (`src/app/narrow.ts`) a sheet from the bottom, half the window or by its grabber all of it, the canvas `tools-hidden` and the row hidden under it. Its head — the quota's share (`shareText`), voicing, ×; its body — the step's card by steps, then the region "Conversation": the verdict's line (`VerdictLine`), the hint, the words with "Report a problem"; its foot — Hint and "Explain differently". Closed, the step's pill (`StepPill`) and the round button (`TutorButton`) open it; over, it holds the result (R65);
- the opening of the panel (R21): the tutor speaks of the work only while it is open. By steps it opens as the task begins and with the choice of "by steps"; on my own it is closed till called — by the tutor's button, the step's pill, "Hint" and "Tutor" of the result, `aria-expanded` —, and back on my own from "by steps" it stays open while it holds the tutor's words or a hint. A verdict and the essay's feedback never open it, and an attempt gone on with a hint or a rung's words starts with it open. Open, "Not yet" and "Correct" ask the words at once, as below. Closed, the check ends at the verdict, and the screen holds in memory the last submission that gave "not yet" or "correct" — its work and its number of wrong answers —, whose words the opening asks once, `GetRungWords` or `GetCorrectAnswerWords`, "Hint" in place of `GetHint`. Showing the solution and leaving the screen drop it, the work of "correct" going from `correctWork` by `wordsCame`. Words of an earlier request that come while it is closed are neither said nor shown, nor their marks, until it opens;
- the items — `Items.tsx`: the options stand in the pop-over of "Choose an answer", `ChoiceGroups` (R65); there an item is a `<fieldset>` with its text or "Item n", an answer slot `choice` is radio buttons, and an item with several answer slots has "Answer n". A task's one item has its reading in the card (R21). The verdict is at the chosen option, outside its caption and as the radio button's description, and at the item's text, by a word and a sign; the summary "Not right yet: n items of m" is `role="status"`; a changed option takes off the verdict of its answer slot and item;
- "Check" — enabled when every answer slot has something in it; disabled, it is `aria-disabled` with the line "Choose an answer in each item", which its press puts in the card (R65). `SubmitWork` — `tapped_choices` in the task's order, and the key is one per submission of these options and the same at its repeat. "Correct" closes the attempt: the work on the device is erased, the panel gives way to the result — "Correct", the solution from `SolvedTask` (`Solution.tsx`: paragraphs and pictures by `blob:` with `alt`), "Try it again" — and the options can no longer be pressed; the way back is the bar's (R57);
- the steps — `steps.ts`, without the DOM, and `StepsPanel.tsx`: the state of the steps — the mode, the place, the farthest step, the ticks of the chips, the techniques by the step's place, the chosen options and the number of wrong answers — is in the attempt's record, and a foreign shape is read as its absence. The list is an `<ol>` "Steps" with `aria-current="step"` and the word of the place, folded under the button "Step n of m", `aria-expanded`, and again when the step changes; the step's title is an `h2`, and it takes the focus when the step changes; "Step done" is under `aria-disabled` and the line of the condition. `BeginStep` — once per step whose `gives` gives techniques, in the mode "by steps"; a failure asks again on the return to the step;
- the pause — `pause.ts`: one blink of "Check" — three flashes of 1 s each — after 6 s without a stroke and without a choice, if it is enabled, and again only after a new action;
- the hint, "Explain differently" and showing the solution (R22): "Hint" is beside "Check", always; the hint is a bubble of the panel's conversation (`Hint.tsx`), as the part on "Check", the pause and the hint above gives it, and on continuing what was given comes from `requested_technique_ids` and `requested_leading_question`. "Explain differently" is a button at the panel's foot (`ExplainButton` in `TutorWords.tsx`), shown while it takes a press, `ExplainDifferently` with the number of the wrong answer, once per rung: the right is spent by an answer and by the refusals "already asked", "not current" and "no rung", while the quota, the limit and admission leave it. "Show the solution" — to a learner of the category `adult` from the start of the attempt, to a child from the fourth wrong answer, through the question "Once the solution is shown, the task is over" (R65); `ShowSolution` as the learner's action — the result "Solution shown" with the solution, and "an adult is needed" and "the ladder is not passed" as a line;
- handwriting (R21): "Check" for a task with handwriting is enabled by the element's `filled`, with the line "Write an answer on the sheet" while the sheet is empty; on it — `exportWork()`, the submission into the database with the context and the length of the history, and `SubmitWork` with the crop (`workOf` in `attempt.ts`) and the clock offset; a repeat of an unanswered one is the same key and the same work, and a new stroke is a new key. Under an item of handwriting — "Read: …" or the reason "unreadable" (`Items.tsx`); the AI call outcome is a line by "Check". "Not yet" calls `GetRungWords` with the same work, at once while the tutor's panel is open, else when it opens: the words are a bubble of the panel's conversation (`TutorWords.tsx`), with `lang`, and the line `role="status"` "The tutor's words came". While they are not there, the block stands with the line "The tutor is writing…", and for "Explain differently" the same line stands under the earlier words. The element gets the tutor's marks (`marksOf`) after rendering, with the submission's context, and the rejected ones are dropped. "Correct" — the work of the correct submission into `correctWork`, `GetCorrectAnswerWords` with it, while the panel is open or once it opens, and `wordsCame`. The "Tutor" block names the quota and the limit of platform AI by a line both under the rung's fallback text and after a correct answer without words, while an AI failure and "not admitted" give no line by the words. The line is one: the words of the same check and the refusal of "Explain differently" do not repeat the one said by "Check", and the model's words take it away. The quota says when it comes back — "today", "tomorrow" or a date by the device's calendar (section 3.8; R37). After a restart the rung's words come from `OpenAttempt.rung`, and there are no tutor's marks: the open attempt does not carry them (`OpenAttempt` in `learning.proto`);
- the essay (R53): `Task.tsx` reads by `isEssay` (`essay.ts`) a task with a slot `sheet` and opens `Essay.tsx` and `EssayScreen.tsx` in place of the task screen, with the same layout, `TaskLayout.tsx`, steps, history, "Hint" and voicing — `useStepTechniques`, `useKeptWork` and `useOwnVoicing` of `shared.ts` —, and without a rung and "Show the solution". "Check" sends the crop by `SubmitEssay`; `sentOf` and `noteOf` turn its end into the feedback, "No feedback this time", the reason of "unreadable", the outcome of "unchecked" or a lost feedback of a repeat, which the screen holds in memory alone, and the device keeps each submission's context with the attempt (section 3.2, "Code"). "Done" — `Done` with `own_key_provider` — opens by the count of submissions, `api`'s and those sent from the screen since, and gives "Essay finished" with the criteria, the solution and the next task. No reminder about names stands above "Check" of an essay or any other sheet (R53). "Report a problem" stands at the feedback with words, `TUTOR_REPLY_ESSAY_FEEDBACK` (`Essay.browser.test.tsx`); its row and card — R66; its panel opens by steps and with a hint, not with the feedback, whose card says where it is (R67);
- the workbook (R54): a task on a workbook goes through the task screen as handwriting does. "Check" opens when each box holds a stroke, by `filled`, with the line "Write an answer in each box" until then, and sends `exportWork()`'s crop of each filled box with its place, `WorkbookWork`: the draft, what falls in no box, goes nowhere. The list of lines under the canvas (`Items.tsx`) gives each line's verdict, and a line of more boxes than one each box's verdict with "Read: …" or the reason, as a nested list, one row a line, the canvas kept in sight (R66); the summary counts lines. The marks stand on a box, `answer_slot`, named with their line and box. Tested in the three engines with the real element and the format's `workbook-page.yaml` (`Workbook.browser.test.tsx`);
- tested in Chromium with the real canvas element (`Task.browser.test.tsx`): the steps, the mode, the choice from the keyboard, the verdicts, a repeat, a closed attempt, a restart, another device, unsaved work and the blink.

### 3.4. The lesson player

Decided by R06. `app` leads a lesson itself, on the device, by the lesson's open view: the segments go in the author's order, and the engine does not reorder them (R58 spec; architecture 3.8 spec). In a lesson `app` gets no key: a worked example is not a task, it has neither answer slots nor a key, and the lesson's tasks go as ordinary attempts (section 3.3).

**Where a lesson comes from.**
- A lesson, or a task outside a lesson, is chosen by `engine` (architecture 3.8 spec). `api` gives the lesson whole, as its open view — `id`, `title` and the segments with all their fields, without skills and age categories — and only to a learner whom the lesson and each of its tasks suit; so the device part has no lessons (R58 spec and its 2026-10-28 amendment; R62 spec). Neither the lesson's title nor its texts contain the keys and the leak phrases of its tasks, after a task segment too (`lesson.no-leaks`; [the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 21).
- With the lesson comes the digest of the package the lesson began on: all the lesson's tasks are attempts on this package, and a withdrawn version or a version outside the window ends the lesson "unchecked" (R07 api; sources 4.2). `app` takes the module manifest for the canvas element from the shared cache by this digest (R62 spec; section 3.2). In the contract the lesson is given by `OpenLesson` of `LearningService` — as a lesson run with its own ID on the package digest (R99 spec).
- The types are `Lesson`, `Segment`, `WorkedExample` and `DemoPart` from `@altnovatrail/spec/formatv0/open`. A segment is exactly one of three: `explanation`, `worked_example` or `task`. Neither a segment nor a part of the demo has an ID: a segment is named by its place in the lesson, from 1, and a part of the demo by its place in `demo` ([the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 21).

**The lesson screen.**
- The top bar is on every segment, as the mockup's `lesson` (R64): its glass pill holds Back, named "Leave the lesson", the lesson's title and, under it, the line of the segment shown, "Worked example · 2 of 4", which opens the segments in a pop-over of section 3.9 — the lesson's title and its module, the segments by places, with the kind of each — explanation, example or task — and which are passed, which is current and which are not open yet, and "Leave the lesson". The profile's pill stands at the bar's other end (section 3.6). So the learner sees the lesson's title before each of its tasks too, as the format requires.
- Under the bar is the segment itself — an explanation a card, its heading its caption, an example and a task the rest of the window —, and at the bottom, in a row of glass, "Continue", its icon after its word. On a worked example "Continue" stands in the canvas's own row instead, after its "Next" and "Demo": a child `<button slot="actions">` of the element, in its slot `actions` (R84 canvas; R06).
- The strings of the bar, the pop-over and the buttons are the interface: their language, rules and words are in section 3.8. The lesson's title and the module's are texts of the module, in the explanation language, with their own `lang`. The segments are a list where the current segment has `aria-current`, and a segment that does not open is `aria-disabled` with a line saying why (section 3.9).

**An explanation** is paragraphs and pictures, like the parts of a task statement ([the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 14), and numbered lists, the part `numbered_list`: two texts or more in order, without numbers of their own ([the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 21; R111 spec). `app` draws them itself, without the canvas element: a picture is an `<img>` with its `alt`, and a list is an `<ol>`, and the numbers 1, 2, 3 are put by it, not by the text. `app` takes the picture's file from the device part that `api` serves, by the same `fetch` request as for the canvas, and shows it by a `blob:` address: the CSP does not let `<img>` reach `api` directly (section 3.7). The response lies in the browser's HTTP cache (section 3.2).

**A worked example** is a new element `<novatrail-canvas>` per segment, with a new `key` (section 3.1). An example can also be on a workbook, with lines, like a task: the canvas shows it, and `app` gives it the same properties (R108 spec; [the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 21). `app` gives it the attribute `lang`, then `manifest`, `workedExample` and, if this run has a saved demo state, `demoState` — in this order, because a state before the example is a `TypeError` (R36 spec).
- The parts of the demo are opened one at a time, and the whole demo is hidden and shown, by the learner, with the element's buttons "Next" and "Demo" (R42 canvas). A new demo begins with `{ opened: 0, hidden: false }`.
- The element sends the event `demochange` only on the learner's press. `app` writes its `detail` into the lesson state, by the example's place, and gives the element this same object, not a new literal (section 3.1).
- The learner does not write on the example's sheet: the example has neither answer slots, nor steps, nor "Check", and it does not serve as evidence (R58 spec).

**A lesson's task** is an attempt on the lesson's package, the same as outside a lesson (architecture 3.5 spec): the task screen of section 3.3 under the lesson's top bar, from the first step, with its own "Check" and hint. A lesson names a template as it names a task, and the learner gets one instance, by the seed of their attempt (the 2026-10-27 amendment to R58 spec; R60 spec), and the instance's open view comes with the attempt (section 3.2). When the task has ended — a correct answer, the showing of the task's solution or "unchecked" — is decided by `api`, by the hint ladder and the showing of the solution (architecture 3.5 spec; R82 spec).

**"Continue" and back.**
- "Continue" is the button of `app` to the next segment. It is not called "Next": that is the name of the element's button that opens a part of the demo (R42 canvas). "Continue" is a value of `app`'s own, not "next" of the icon table of architecture 6 spec (R78 spec), and its icon is `arrow-circle-right`: an arrow in a circle looks different from the arrow of "Next", `arrow-forward`, and from the chevron of "Demo", `keyboard-arrow-right`, and on the screen of a worked example all three are seen at once (the 2026-11-10 amendment to R06).
- On an explanation and an example "Continue" is enabled at once: the demo does not lock it. An example is neither a task nor evidence, and locking the button would mean putting pressure on the learner (product 7 spec), and they can return to the example.
- On a task "Continue" appears only when `api` has said that the task has ended — after the screen after the check (section 3.10). The next segment goes then, and not earlier ([the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 21). There is no automatic move, as on the steps (section 3.3).
- Back is by a row of the segments' pop-over, to any passed explanation and example. An example opens as a new element with its own saved demo state. The place in the lesson does not change from this: "Continue" on a passed segment leads to the next passed explanation or example, skipping the segments of ended tasks, and after the last of them, to the place; the line returns to the place at once.
- The pop-over shows the segment of a task that has ended as passed but does not open it: the attempt is closed, and solving the task again is a new attempt, and that is an engine choice. Its work and its open view are erased besides, an essay's too (section 3.2; R53).
- From an unfinished task the learner goes to an example or an explanation and comes back: the attempt has not ended, its work and the state of its steps lie in the profile's database (sections 3.2 and 3.3), and the task's new element gets `task` and `history`.

**The place in the lesson** is the segment the learner has reached. It moves on "Continue", and also when `api` has said that the task at the place has ended: the place moves to what comes next, though the learner still sees the screen after the check. So after a restart the lesson will not open on a task that has ended.

**Opening a lesson.** When `api` gives a lesson, there comes with it which of the lesson's attempts have ended and which has not, if there is one — the task of a segment (`OpenLesson`, and after a restart and on another device, `GetLessonRun` of `LearningService`; R99 spec). The lesson opens on the later of two places: its own, from the lesson state, and the one that comes after the last ended task by the answer of `api`. An unfinished attempt opens as the same attempt.
- Leaving in the middle of a lesson sends nothing: the lesson state lies in the profile's database until the term of 30 days without changes erases it (section 3.2). When `api` gives this lesson again on the same package, `app` opens it at the place and with the demos as they were.
- Another device: the lesson state lies only where the learner went through the lesson. On a device without the state the lesson opens after the last ended task, and if none has ended, on the first segment; the demos start from the beginning, and the passed segments before this place open back, as usual. An unfinished task opens as the same attempt, but with a clean sheet: the work lies on the device where it was written (section 3.2).

**The end of a lesson.**
- The usual end of a lesson is always "Continue" on the last segment, whatever its kind: then `app` tells `api` that the lesson is passed — `CompleteLesson` of `LearningService` — and repeats this until `api` answers. If `api` refused with `CAUSE_SEGMENTS_NOT_ENDED`, the refusal carries the places of the ended task segments, and the lesson opens on the first that has not ended (R99 spec). If the last segment is a task, then at its end `api` answers that there are no more segments, and the learner presses "Continue", as after any task. Otherwise `api` would not learn that the learner read to the end the explanation after the last task, as in `gaps-and-posts.yaml`, and a lesson without tasks, like `reading-model-essays.yaml`, would never end (`spec/format/examples/lesson/valid/`).
- Then `app` erases the lesson state and shows the end screen: the lesson's title, "Lesson complete" and a button to what the engine chose. It has no score, grade or comparison (product 7 spec), and the reason code of the choice is seen only by an adult (concepts 12 spec; R29 spec).
- `api` itself ends a lesson only in an unusual way, and then `app` does not send "lesson passed". There is no early end of a lesson in the first slice (R107 spec). If the lesson was ended "unchecked" — the version is withdrawn or outside the window (R63 spec; R07 api) —, "not suitable" — the module is disconnected, it has no current version for the learner, or the lesson no longer suits their age category — or "abandoned" — 30 days without requests —, a neutral phrase (R04 spec) and a button to the engine choice. The end of the run comes in an answer or as the refusal `CAUSE_RUN_ENDED` with its reason (R99 spec). In each case `app` erases the lesson state.

**Another version of the module.** Within a run the version does not change: the lesson holds the digest of its package (R07 api). `app` keeps the lesson state by the lesson's ID and the package digest, so the same lesson on another package is a new run: from the first segment and with the demos from the beginning. Beside the key it holds the run's ID, which `OpenLesson` gives, and drops the state of another run under the same key: the lesson on the same package could have ended and begun again (R99 spec; R06).
- `app` erases the earlier state as soon as it has learned that its run has ended: `api` ended the lesson for any reason — "unchecked", "not suitable", "abandoned" —, `app` learned of the withdrawal of the version (section 3.2), or `api` gave the same lesson on another package — then `app` erases the state of this lesson on all other digests before it opens the lesson. So a run that ended while the learner was on another device, or did not come in at all, does not lie on this one until the term of 30 days.
- Comparing examples by content is not needed, and the element would not make this comparison anyway: it checks a state only against the number of parts and takes one that fits by number silently, even if the parts are different (R42 canvas; sources 4.2). The state of another package never reaches the element.
- A record that the element would reject — `opened` not an integer from 0 to the number of the example's parts, or `hidden` neither `true` nor `false` — means a corruption of the database. `canvas` does not expose its own check (sources 4.2), so `app` checks these two fields itself: it does not give such a record to the element, and the demo begins from the start.

**The engine choice.**
- The next lesson or task is chosen by `engine`, and it does not reorder the lesson's segments (architecture 3.8 spec). A task outside a lesson is the task screen of section 3.3 without the lesson's header. The reason of the choice is not named to a child (concepts 12 spec).
- There is no consolidation within a lesson and no early end in the first slice: at the end of a segment `api` does not call the engine (R107 spec; R05 engine). `app` learns what comes next from the field `next` of the answer at the end of an attempt — the next segment, "no more segments" or the engine choice (R99 spec). Consolidation, if it comes, is a new reason of the contract, which `app` takes with its move to a tag.

**The mark "sequential"** — for a module whose skill graph is a chain (R58 spec; [the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 21):
- a chain — each skill of the module stands in its own tier, and the module has no fewer than two skills. Anchor skills give no tiers and are not counted; a module without a graph and with one skill has no mark. A redundant link — a skill needs both `a` and `b`, and `b` needs `a` — does not break the chain: the order of the skills does not change from it. So the 2026-11-02 amendment to R58 spec defines a chain;
- `app` does not compute the tiers itself: `module-kit` computes them when it builds the package, and `api` gives them to the skill map, `skill_tiers` ([the format text](../spec/format/README.md), sections 12 and 25). A module is a chain when each tier of `skill_tiers` has one skill. So the mark and the tiers of the skill map will not diverge: there is no second count;
- the tiers are needed before the first attempt too, for the current version of each connected module: `app` gets them as the open view of the skill graph with tiers — `GetSkillGraph` of `PackageService`, by the package digest of the module's current version (R99 spec);
- the mark is seen on the module's card on Subjects, as the kind "Sequential" on Home's (R79), and on the module's page in the section for adults. It says that the skill graph puts the module's lessons in one row: a lesson comes after the lessons that teach the skills before its skills, and the engine chooses which of the lessons of one skill to give ([the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 21). It is not in the lesson's header: it is about the order of lessons, not of segments. The words of the mark are the glossary of section 3.8. On the screen (R34) it is the line "In order" in `src/subjects/CardHeading.tsx` at each card of a module with a current version — on Subjects, connected and in the catalog, in the list of skill maps and on the page of the profile's modules in the section for adults — and it is computed by `isSequential` in `src/subjects/modules.ts` by the `skill_tiers` of the graph's open view from the shared cache. The cards do not wait for the graphs: the mark goes onto a card when its graph has come — the hook `useSequential` in `src/subjects/` — and a graph that has not come gives no mark;
- in the format's examples the graph of olympiad maths is 17 skills in three tiers, and it is not a chain (`format/vectors/tiers/olympiad-math.yaml`), while the graph of the module `with-lessons` — two skills, `counting-gaps` and `time-calendar` after it — is a chain. Whether a module of the first batch has the mark, its graph tells.

**The learner's path through a lesson of olympiad maths.** The path goes through the lesson `gaps-and-posts.yaml` from the format's examples (`spec/format/examples/lesson/valid/`) — "A lesson of the official module of olympiad mathematics". The lesson teaches the skill `counting-gaps`, and in the official module its task `gaps-ladder-1` is the ladder task `task/valid/ladder.yaml` (concepts 16.1 spec), with an answer slot `anywhere`, chips and the steps of `olympiad-math.yaml` (section 3.3). In the examples module `with-lessons` the same lesson lies with a short task `gaps-ladder-1` — without chips, techniques and steps: this module has no steps, and the task there is solved as in the mode "self".

| Segment | On the screen | Event on the device | In the profile's database | In `api` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1. Explanation | the top bar "Gaps and posts", the line "Explanation · 1 of 4"; a paragraph about posts and gaps and the picture `fence-posts-and-gaps.png` with its `alt` | "Continue" — enabled at once | the lesson with the package digest; the place is 2 | the lesson came from `api` together with the digest |
| 2. Worked example | a new element: the statement "A path is 20 m long…", a sheet with a grid, the parts closed — `{ opened: 0, hidden: false }` | "Next" three times: `demochange` — `{ opened: 1, hidden: false }`, a paragraph; `{ opened: 2, … }`, the picture `path-in-gaps.png` in the sheet area `left: 100, top: 150`, 800 × 200; `{ opened: 3, … }`, a paragraph with the answer. "Demo" — `{ opened: 3, hidden: true }`. "Continue" is enabled all the time | the demo state of place 2 — after each `demochange`; the place is 3 | — |
| 3. Task `gaps-ladder-1` | the task screen under the lesson's top bar, from the first step: "Understand the task", the chips, the steps up to "Check the answer" (section 3.3) | strokes, chips, "Check"; back to the example and back again — the demo `{ opened: 3, hidden: true }`, the task's work in place | the attempt: the writing history, the submissions, the state of the steps (section 3.2); at the end of the task the place is 4 | an attempt on the lesson's package; reading and verdict; `api` says that the task has ended — a correct answer or the showing of the solution — and that next comes the next segment |
| 4. Explanation | "Each time, count the gaps first…"; segment 3 in the segments is passed and does not open | "Continue" on the last segment | the lesson state is erased | "lesson passed"; then the engine choice |

After segment 4 — the end screen: "Gaps and posts", "Lesson complete" and a button to what the engine chose. In the format's examples module `with-lessons`, where the same lesson lies, the lesson `days-and-dates` comes after it by the graph: its skill `time-calendar` stands after `counting-gaps`.

- **A restart** on segment 3: `app` finds the lesson state by the ID `gaps-and-posts` and the digest and opens segment 3 — the attempt with the writing history and the state of the steps. The example on segment 2 opens with `{ opened: 3, hidden: true }`, as it was left.
- **A change of the module's version:** the version on which the lesson began was withdrawn. `api` ends the lesson "unchecked" (R07 api), `app` shows a neutral phrase and erases the lesson state. When the engine gives `gaps-and-posts` again, now on the new version, the key — the lesson's ID and the new digest — finds nothing: the lesson goes from the first segment, and the demo from `{ opened: 0, hidden: false }`. Had the new version's example two parts, the element would reject the earlier `{ opened: 3, hidden: true }` with a `TypeError`, and had it four, it would take it silently, with other parts; neither happens, because the element does not get the earlier state.
- **Another device:** the task of segment 3 ended on a computer, and the learner opened the lesson on a tablet. There is no state there, and `api` says that the task of segment 3 has ended: the lesson opens on segment 4, and the example on segment 2 from the start of the demo.

**Where the prototype differs** ([the prototype's README](../spec/draft/README.md)): the mark *Sequential* stands at module packages and leads them "lesson by lesson in the author's order", while by R58 spec it is derived from the graph, and the next lesson is chosen by `engine`; the prototype has no demo. The documents take precedence.

**Code** (R23):
- the entry — `src/lesson/`: the screen `#/lesson` opens a lesson run whose ID is only in memory (`LessonContext.tsx`): the runs begun, from `open_lesson_runs` of `GetAccount` — Home's "Next up" with "Continue the lesson", and a module's card — through `GetLessonRun`, and by an engine choice of a lesson, `OpenLesson`, by "Next lesson" or "What's next" (R24). The block names the run by the lesson's title from its state on the device, with the module's `lang`, and without the state, "A lesson: {module}", where the module's name is a placeholder element with its own `lang` (section 3.8). Without a chosen run, `#/lesson` goes home in its own place in the history (`LessonRoute.tsx`); a run that has ended leaves the list after "Switch learner" too;
- reading — `Lesson.tsx`: the lesson's open view in the build's window of versions, the manifest from the shared cache by the digest, and the lesson state from the profile's database — by the lesson's full ID (the module ID of the manifest, `lesson` and the lesson's ID; [the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 10), the digest and the run's ID; `readLesson` erases a record of another digest or run (R16). The place is `openingPlace` in `place.ts`, without the DOM: the later of its own, the one after the last ended task, and the segment of the open attempt, past ended tasks, except for the last segment. The lesson state is written on opening, at each move of the place and after each `demochange`, and a write that did not go through is the line "Your work isn't saved on this device";
- the screen — `LessonScreen.tsx` and `SegmentBar.tsx`, by the mockup (R64): `Screen`'s layout `lesson`, the whole window, its top bar's pill with Back — `IconButton` with the caption "Leave the lesson" —, the `h1`, the lesson's title with the module's `lang`, and under it, by the bar's `under`, the line, a button of 44 over the whole heading, named "{line}: segments of the lesson". Its pop-over, "Segments of the lesson", hangs from the pill, 6 below it, by `PopOver`'s `anchor`: the lesson's title and the module's, a `<nav>` with the `<ol>` of rows 52 high — the number in a circle, a tick in place of a passed one's, with the number out of sight, the kind and "passed", "now", "later" or why it does not open; its words its name (2.5.3) —, and "Leave the lesson". A press on a row that does not open closes it and is a line of the screen with the row's place and kind: "5 Task: not open yet" — a bare "Not open yet" under the heading of the segment shown would read as said about that segment. Under the bar is the `h2` "Worked example · 2 of 4", to which the focus goes when the segment changes: the explanation's caption, out of sight on an example and a task; at the bottom, "Continue" with the icon `continue` after its word;
- the explanation — `Explanation.tsx`: paragraphs, pictures — the shared `src/packages/Picture.tsx`, the same as for a task's solution — and `numbered_list` as an `<ol>`. The example — `Example.tsx`: a new element per segment, `lang`, the manifest, `media`, `workedExample` and `demoState` in this order, and `demochange` writes `detail` into the state by the place and gives the element the same object; a record that the element would reject is not given (`demoOf` in `run.ts`);
- the lesson's task — `BeginAttempt` with the run's segment and a key, the same at a repeat, when the learner has reached the segment, and the open attempt at the place comes from `LessonRun.open_attempt`. `Task.tsx` in a lesson is without its own screen, "Try it again" and "Back to Home" (`TaskInLesson`), and the `next` of the answer to `SubmitWork` and `ShowSolution` goes to the player: `segment` moves the place at once, `segments_ended` leaves it on the last segment, and an empty one means a repeated `GetLessonRun`. An attempt closed "left" or "abandoned" gives "Begin the task again", and an attempt that `api` does not know, a repeated `GetLessonRun` and a new attempt of the segment. The refusal `CAUSE_SEGMENT_UNAVAILABLE` leads on if the segment has ended by itself, and otherwise to the first task that has not ended, even if it is before the place; a refusal without places means a repeated `GetLessonRun`. The quota, the limit, "not admitted" and an answer without an attempt are a line of the task segment, with the run open;
- the end — `CompleteLesson` on "Continue" on the last segment, again on "Continue" after a failure; `CAUSE_SEGMENTS_NOT_ENDED` — the first task segment that has not ended, with the line "The lesson has a task to finish". The end of the run for any reason — by an answer or by `CAUSE_RUN_ENDED` at opening, at the start of a segment, at a repeated reading and at "lesson passed" — erases the lesson state; "unchecked" also calls `CheckPackages`. The end screen is `LessonEnd`, the mockup's card in the middle of the window, `Screen`'s layout `end`, the profile's pill above it: the module's tile, `src/subjects/ModuleTile.tsx`, as on Home's cards, and its title, the lesson's title, "Lesson complete" after a star, `lesson-complete`, or the phrase of the reason (`endKey` in `run.ts`) and "Back to Home";
- the engine choice (R24) — `src/next/`: `NextStep.tsx` — "Next lesson" or "Next task" by the choice from the answer about the end of an attempt outside a lesson (`next.choice` of `SubmitWork` and `ShowSolution`) or of a run (`RunEnd.choice` of `CompleteLesson` and in the refusal `CAUSE_RUN_ENDED`), and without it, "What's next", `ChooseNext`; "nothing to give" and a choice of an unknown kind (`givenOf` in `choice.ts`) — the line "There is nothing more to offer for now" with the outcome. A lesson by the choice is the player, `OpenLesson`; a task is `BeginAttempt` with `choice_id`, and the refusal `CAUSE_ALREADY_EXISTS` gives the open attempt from `GetAccount`. Home has "What's next" at the foot of "Next up" while no attempt is open, and nothing beside "Continue the task" (R79). `ChooseNext` and the start of a task by the choice close the attempt open outside a lesson: before them there is a question, wherever the button stands, and after them the attempt's work on the device is erased (`attemptOver` in `src/storage/profile.ts`, shared with the task screen). `#/task` takes the attempt begun from it itself, and `#/lesson` the new chosen lesson; tested in Chromium — `NextStep.browser.test.tsx`, the results of a task and of a lesson, and the path through `App`;
- tested in Chromium with the real canvas element (`Lesson.browser.test.tsx`): the lesson's path above — an explanation with a picture and a list, the example and its demo in the database, the task, the explanation, "Lesson complete" — a restart on segment 3, another device, another run under the same key and a corrupted demo record, an example on a workbook and the end by `segments_ended`, an empty `next`, showing the solution, a task abandoned and one not begun, each end of a run; the entry is through `App` (`AccountGate.browser.test.tsx`).

### 3.5. Voicing

Decided by R07. `app` voices the tutor's words with the device's speech synthesis — `speechSynthesis` from the Web Speech API (sources 5.1). Voicing only repeats the text: it always stays on the screen. The boundary with `tutor` is decided by R56 tutor: the same text that passed the checks of `api` is voiced, the model writes no separate text for speech, and `tutor` takes no part in voicing by the device (sources 5.1). Voicing is for everyone: it is output, not a collection of data (privacy 6 spec), as long as the text does not leave the device. So `app` chooses the voice itself, below.

**When.** Voicing is in the first slice, stage 2 — product 10 spec names it under "In" (R12) —, and not in the first app (R76 spec). Voicing by an AI provider and speech recognition wait for stage 3, and speech-to-speech for the tutor's words is closed (Т-13; R56 tutor). Voicing by the device needs no real-time channel: the tutor's words come whole (R54 spec; Т-25).

**What is voiced.**
- By itself, if the learner's voicing is on: the tutor's words that come from `api` — the rung's question, a hint, feedback by a rubric or a fallback text (architecture 4.1 spec; R70 spec). With them, the lines of the tutor's panel: the verdict, the reason "unreadable" and the neutral phrase (section 3.3). Voicing begins once the whole text is on the screen, past the leak check and the form check (R54 spec, R70 spec).
- By the button with a speaker beside the text — always, when there is a voice for its language, and with voicing off too:
  - a repeat of the tutor's words;
  - the task statement — paragraphs, items, lines of a workbook and the options of `choice`;
  - the chips, the step's title and the technique card (section 3.3);
  - the paragraphs of a lesson's explanation and the opened parts of a worked example, except pictures (section 3.4).

  These are the module's open texts, which the learner reads anyway (R05 spec).
- Not voiced are the buttons and captions of the interface and the `alt` of pictures: a screen reader reads them, and how `app` works with it is in section 3.9. `app` does not voice the learner's work, except their words that feedback by a rubric quotes (R70 spec).
- `app` does not call the button "listen": concepts 8 spec keeps that word for an action of the text canvas. The words of the button and of the lines of voicing follow the rules and the glossary of section 3.8: the button is "Read aloud", without gender.

**Language.** Each text sounds in its own language:
- the module's texts and the tutor's words — in the module's explanation language, `languages.explanation` of the manifest: a module has one language, as a BCP 47 tag (R46 spec; [the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 11);
- the strings of `app` — in the interface language (section 3.8): on the learner's screens, the language of their profile, and before the choice of the learner and in the section for adults, the device's language.

Voices are needed for both, and by the recommendation of О-2 these are English and Russian. The example manifests of the three modules of the first slice have the explanation language `en` (`spec/format/examples/manifest/valid/`), and there is no target language in the slice: it has no language modules (R41 spec). If there is no voice for the language of a text, only that text is silent.

**Which voice.** `app` always names the voice itself — the `voice` of each `SpeechSynthesisUtterance` — and leaves the browser no choice. Without a named voice the specification tells the browser to take its default voice, and it "can be a local or remote speech service". Chrome picks such a voice only by language, does not prefer a local one, and for `ru` it has the network voice «Google русский» (sources 5.1). The voice for a language L:
1. The candidates are the voices from `getVoices()` whose `localService` is true, whose name has no "Online" and whose `lang` matches L. First the same tag matches, then a tag that begins with L — `en-US` and `en-GB` to `en`: this is the basic filtering of RFC 4647, "Basic Filtering". If there are none, the same for L without its last subtag: `pt-PT` too will match `pt-BR`. Case does not matter, and `_` in `lang` is read as `-`: Chrome on Android gives `en_US`. The voices' `lang` has a region, `ru-RU` of «Google русский», and the explanation language of the example manifests, `en`, has none (sources 5.1).
2. The voice that the adult chose for L on this device, if it is among the candidates.
3. Otherwise the candidate with `default`, if the language has one such, otherwise the first in the list. WebKit sets `default` on every voice (sources 5.1), so in Safari, without the adult's choice, the first one the list gives is taken. Д5 checks on devices which voice turns out first, and the adult sees the voice and changes it on the page "This device".
4. Otherwise there is no voice for L.

`app` reads the list of voices at launch and rereads it on `voiceschanged`: in Chrome the list does not come at once, network voices disappear without a network, and the adult can add a voice in the system (sources 5.1).

**What `localService` knows** is only what the browser knows, not the system (sources 5.1; architecture 8 spec; Т-13): voicing on Android and Linux with the adult's permission is a choice of the system that the adult makes, not a disclosure (R118 spec).

| Platform | What `localService` says | What `app` does |
|---|---|---|
| Chrome on a computer | `false` for the network voices of Google — "Google US English", «Google русский»: Chrome sends their text to `www.google.com/speech-api`. These voices are only in Google's Chrome, and not in ChromeOS. `true` for the system's voices | takes the system's voices |
| Edge | voices with "Online" in the name are synthesized by Azure, and the text goes to Microsoft; the others, on the device, "no information is sent to Microsoft servers" | does not take a voice with "Online" in the name, even if `localService` is true |
| Safari | `true` for every voice: WebKit gives only the voices of Apple's system and sets `default` on each | takes the system's voices; all have `default`, so the adult's choice comes first |
| Firefox on Windows and macOS | `true` for the system's voices | takes them |
| ChromeOS | there are no network voices of Google, and ChromeOS gives its own network voices only to its own feature "Select-to-speak" | takes the system's voices |
| Android | Chrome — `true` for every voice: it builds a voice from the language and the system's engine and does not mark it as network, though an Android voice may require a network — `Voice.isNetworkConnectionRequired()` (architecture 8 spec). Firefox — `true` only if the engine says that it synthesizes on the device. What Google's engine does, Google does not say | voicing is off until the adult turns it on on this device |
| Linux | Chrome does not mark the voices of speech-dispatcher as network, and speech-dispatcher has a module of the cloud Edge TTS. Firefox calls local only the speech-dispatcher voice named "default" | as on Android |

**Android and Linux.** `app` learns the platform from what the browser names: from `navigator.userAgentData.platform`, where there is one — its values are "Android", "Chrome OS", "Fuchsia", "iOS", "Linux", "macOS", "Windows", or "Unknown" (sources 5.1) — and otherwise from the user agent string, where "Android" is checked before "CrOS", and "CrOS" before "Linux". So ChromeOS does not fall into Linux. On Android and Linux voicing is off until the adult turns it on on this device, on the page "This device". The page says why: here the device's system chooses the voice, and the browser does not know whether it synthesizes on the device or sends the text to the network. The permission lies in the device registry, which the owner's sign-out leaves, and goes with "Erase everything on this device", the account gone and a sign-in of another account (section 3.2): another account decides for itself. Until the permission, the button with the speaker is disabled, and its line says that on this device an adult turns voicing on.

**No voice.**
- When a language has no candidate, the button with the speaker is disabled, and the line beside it says that this device has no voice for this language. The text stays; there is no voicing.
- `app` takes neither a voice of another language nor a remote voice: a foreign voice would read an English text with a Russian pronunciation, and a remote one would send the text off the device, bypassing `api`.
- The page "This device" names the languages without a voice and says how to add a voice in the system — in words, without links (privacy 1, 7 spec): in Windows voices are added in the speech settings, in macOS the voice is downloaded from Apple, in Android, the voice data for the language (sources 5.1).

**Settings.**
- Whether voicing is on, and its speed — "normal" or "slow" — are part of the presentation style in the learner's profile on the server (concepts 13 spec; О-23). The default mode is a field of the profile of its own, not in the presentation style (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R05). Voicing follows the learner to any device.
- For a new profile of the category `under_13` voicing is on, and for `from_13_to_consent`, `from_consent_to_18` and `adult` it is off ([the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 11). The presentation style follows the learner's age, and it can be chosen separately (R41 spec; privacy 3.3 spec).
- It is changed both by the learner — with the switch "Voicing" of the task screen, `SetVoicing` (R36) — and by the adult, on the profile's page of the section for adults, `UpdateProfile`, whether voicing is on and the speed separately (R29); that page says that the tutor's words are read by a voice of the device, and that each device's voice is chosen on its page "This device". It is one setting, without a separate default of the adult such as the mode has (R05): it does not change the content.
- It does not go into a call to AI: of the presentation style only what changes the words goes to the model (О-23; privacy 4 spec). In the contract it is the profile's fields and `SetVoicing` of `AccountService`, whether voicing is on apart from the speed (R98 spec).
- The voice is on the device, by language. Each device has its own voices, the `voiceURI` of one voice differs from device to device, and Chrome on Android does not let one choose a voice within a language: for it a voice is a language (sources 5.1). The choice lies in the device registry (section 3.2).
- The "normal" speed is `rate` 1, the voice's own speed, and "slow" is `rate` 0.75 (R36). The number is not tuned on devices (Д5), and on each engine the speed `1` sounds its own way (sources 5.1). The persona's voice — its tone and pitch — will come with the persona (concepts 10 spec; О-13), and until then the pitch is the default.

**The page "This device"** (section 3.2) gets a section "Voicing":
- for each language of the build — the interface of every profile and device is in them — and the explanation language of each module whose manifest the device's cache holds (R36) — which voice is taken and a choice among the candidates, with a sample button;
- on Android and Linux, why voicing is off, and the button "Turn voicing on on this device".

**One synthesis for the whole app** (R36 spec). One module of `app` owns the speech synthesis, and later the "listen" of the text canvas will go through it too.
- A new text cuts off the previous one — `cancel()` — rather than queuing after it. Speech is stopped by leaving the screen, a change of learner, a second tap on the speaker and a hidden tab.
- `app` does not use `pause()` and `resume()`: in Chrome on Android `pause()` only stops the speech, and `resume()` does nothing (sources 5.1).
