- The text goes by sentences: each has its own `SpeechSynthesisUtterance`, and the sentences of one text queue in the synthesis one after another, and only a new text cuts them off. In Chrome a long utterance is cut off after about 15 s (sources 5.1), and feedback by a rubric can be up to 80 words (R70 spec).
- `app` marks a tap after which a text will come — "Check", "Hint" or the speaker — at once, before the request to `api`, with an empty `SpeechSynthesisUtterance`. On iPhone and iPad WebKit lifts its restriction only if `speak()` is called within a tap, and without this it silently says nothing; the tutor's words come later than the tap, and by then the restriction would have remained. For Chrome any earlier tap on the page is enough, and without one it answers with the error `not-allowed` (sources 5.1).
- A synthesis error is silent: the text is on the screen. Firefox gives no error code (sources 5.1), so `app` does not tell errors apart.

**Signs in words.** The module's texts are plain text without formulas ([the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 14), but with signs: in the format's examples, `7 ×` and the boxes of a workbook line, "20 ÷ 5 = 4 gaps", `2/8`. How the synthesis reads a sign depends on the engine, so before the synthesis `app` writes the signs as words by the table of the explanation language — `×`, `÷`, `−`, `=`, `<`, `>`, the box of a workbook line and a fraction. On the screen the text does not change. This is the code of `app`, not the model, and after the checks of `api` (R56 tutor). The table for `en` and `ru` is in "Code" below (R36).

**The path of a hint on four devices.** A learner of the category `under_13`, voicing on. The task `ladder.yaml` of olympiad maths, the explanation language `en` (concepts 16.1 spec; section 3.3), and the interface in Russian. The learner presses "Hint", and `api` gives the question whole.

| Device | Voices | What `app` does |
|---|---|---|
| iPad, Safari | the voices of Apple's system, all with `localService` and `default` true | the tap on "Hint" lifts WebKit's restriction with an empty utterance, before the request; the question comes and goes onto the screen, and `app` voices it with an English voice — the one the adult chose, and without a choice, the first in the list |
| Windows, Chrome | Microsoft David and Zira, if they are added in the system — `true`; "Google US English" — `false` | takes the voice the adult chose, and without a choice, the system's voice with `default`, for example Microsoft David; never takes "Google US English" |
| Windows, Chrome, no Russian voice | Microsoft's English voices; «Google русский» — `false` | the question in English sounds, while a line of the interface in Russian — for example, the verdict «Пока нет» after the check — is silent. The page "This device" says that there is no Russian voice and how to add one |
| Android, Chrome | all voices — `true` | the speaker is disabled, with the line that on this device an adult turns voicing on. When the adult has turned it on on the page "This device" — as on the iPad |

**Tests.** Beyond the tests of "Code" below, what is derived from the browsers' code — WebKit's silence without a tap and the lifting of the restriction with an empty utterance, the cut-off of a long utterance in Chrome, `lang` on Android, an empty list of voices, Apple's first voice without a choice and the recognition of the platform — is checked by hand on iPad, Android, Windows and Mac — Д5, with the checks of Д10 —, and with it how the engines read the signs that are not in the table of signs — Д18.

**Code** (R36):
- synthesis — `src/voice/speech.ts`: the class `Speech` behind a narrow interface, `SynthLike` and `UtteranceLike` — what `app` calls on `speechSynthesis` and sets on `SpeechSynthesisUtterance` —, so that the tests in Node give it their own lists of voices. `say` gives each phrase the voice of its language, cuts the text into sentences and sends each as its own utterance with `voice`, `lang` and `rate`; before a new text, `cancel()`, while the words that come after the verdict of the same check queue behind it while it sounds and do not cut it off. `prime` is an empty utterance on a tap, `stop` is `cancel()`. The browser's synthesis is `src/voice/browser.ts`, one for the app, and a hidden tab stops the speech (`speechOf`);
- the voice and the platform — `src/voice/voices.ts`: `candidates` and `voiceFor` are the rule "Which voice" above, `platformOf` is the rule "Android and Linux": the values "Fuchsia", "Unknown" and empty of `userAgentData.platform` are not a value, and then the user agent string names the platform. iPadOS calls itself Mac, and it need not be told apart: the empty utterance goes on every tap of "Check", "Hint" and "Explain differently" everywhere while voicing is on and allowed, and "Read aloud" speaks by itself within the tap. `rateOf` is the speed of "Settings", `VOICING_SPEED_UNSPECIFIED` and an unknown value being "normal";
- symbols in words — `src/voice/spoken.ts`, by the first subtag of the explanation language:

  | Sign | `en` between operands | `en` alone | `ru` between operands | `ru` alone |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | `×` | times | times sign | «умножить на» | «знак умножения» |
  | `÷` | divided by | division sign | «разделить на» | «знак деления» |
  | `−` and a hyphen between numbers with spaces | minus | minus | «минус» | «минус» |
  | `=` | equals | equals sign | «равно» | «знак равно» |
  | `<` | is less than | less-than sign | «меньше, чем» | «знак меньше» |
  | `>` | is greater than | greater-than sign | «больше, чем» | «знак больше» |
  | a box of a workbook line | box | — | «окошко» | — |

  A sign is read as an operation when an operand stands beside it on either side — a digit, a box or a bracket: "18 ÷ 2 =" of a line whose answer is written by hand, and "= 40" after a box. Without an operand the sign is named: "Write <, > or =". A fraction `a/b` with a denominator from 2 to 12 is read by the name of its parts: "3 quarters", "1 half"; «одна четвёртая», «две пятых», «3 четвёртых», «21 двенадцатая». Otherwise "5 over 36", «21 делённое на 100». A language without a table sounds with its signs as they are. Sentences are cut by `Intl.Segmenter`, and without it by a full stop, a question mark, an exclamation mark or an ellipsis followed by a space;
- what the button reads — `src/voice/texts.ts`: for a task, the paragraphs of the statement, the items, the workbook lines with boxes and the options of `choice`; for a hint, the givens, the unknown, the technique cards and the question; for a step, the name, the description, the chips, if the step gives them, and the cards; for an explanation, the paragraphs and the items of lists; for a worked example, the statement, the lines and the open parts of the demo. A box that the demo has already filled sounds as its value, while a hidden demo does not sound at all, nor do the boxes of its lines: the demo is hidden whole (R42 canvas). Pictures and `alt` are not read;
- the voicing of a screen — `src/voice/VoicingContext.tsx`: `VoicingProvider` around the learner's screens in `src/app/App.tsx` gives them the profile, whether voicing is on, the speed and the device record, and a change of screen or of learner, voicing turned off and the permission taken back — by whatever path they came — stop the speech. Until the device record is read, nothing sounds. `useVoicing` is `silent`: why a language will not sound — "no permission" or "no voice" —, `read`, by the button, `auto`, by itself, only when voicing is on and allowed, and `prime`. Outside the learner's screens — in a test of the screen itself — voicing is off;
- the buttons — `src/voice/ReadAloud.tsx`, "Read aloud" with the icon `read-aloud`: at the task statement above the work, at the tutor's words, at a hint, at a step, at an explanation and at a worked example. A second tap while its text sounds is a stop, a tap of another button cuts off the former text, and a button that leaves the screen stops its own. While it is not known whether the text will sound — the device record is not read, the module's language has not come —, the button is inactive without a line. Where the text will not sound, the button stays, `aria-disabled`, and the line beside it is its description, `aria-describedby`, not a status (section 3.9). The task's solution has no button: "What is voiced" does not name it. `src/voice/VoicingToggle.tsx` is "Voicing", `aria-pressed`, in the head of the tutor's panel while it is shown and above the canvas while it is closed (R36): on my own the panel stays closed until called, and the learner has to turn voicing on before the first words. `SetVoicing` sends only `enabled` and does not touch the speed, and the session takes the profile from the answer, as a save of the profile page does, and tells the other tabs of it (`onSaved` in `src/account/AccountGate.tsx`). Turning voicing off stops the speech at once; a failure gets its words, `role="alert"`; voicing that is on but mute gets a line saying why;
- what sounds by itself — `src/task/Task.tsx`: after "Check", "Correct" or "Not right yet: …", the neutral phrase of the AI call outcome and the line of the quota or of the platform AI's limit in the screen's language (R36), and after them the rung's words or the correct answer words in the explanation language; on "Hint", what it gave: the givens, the unknown, the cards and the question, or the rung's words; on "Explain differently", the new words. Words that come once the task screen has left do not sound. Not sounding by themselves are the lines at the items — what was read and why it cannot be read: they stand at the item, not on the panel, and the screen reader reads them —, the task's solution and the words with which the attempt opened after a reload: no tap called them;
- the page "This device" — `src/voice/DeviceVoices.tsx`, the section "Voicing" after the profiles, in the device's language: the languages are `en` and `ru` of the build and the explanation languages of the modules whose manifests the cache holds; each has a group of radio buttons named by the language: the candidates, the one taken now marked, and a choice is written at once, without "Save" — it is a setting of the device, not a change of context (section 3.9). A sample, in English or in Russian, in the voice's language, is only for the build's languages, and on Android and Linux only after the permission: before it the text could go to the network. A browser without the Web Speech API gets a line that it does not read aloud;
- the device record — `src/storage/voicing.ts`: the registry's key `voicing`, with `allowed` and `voices`, a `voiceURI` by the language tag in lower case. Reading does not create the database; a record that cannot be read is nothing chosen and no permission; "Erase everything on this device" and the account gone erase it with the registry, and a sign-in of another account erases the record itself; the sign-out leaves it (`src/storage/erase.ts`; section 3.2). The page announces the record to the other tabs — the channel `novatrail-voicing` —, and the learner's screens there read it again;
- tests: Node — `voices.node.test.ts`, the voice rule on the voice lists of the table of platforms above and of "The hint path" — Chrome's network voices, Edge's "Online", `default` on each WebKit voice, Android's `en_US`, a Chromebook and Linux without voices —, the base filtering and the subtag, the adult's choice, a single `default`, the platform by `userAgentData` and by the user agent string, the speed; `spoken.node.test.ts` — the texts of the modules of the first slice — the workbook lines of `times-tables-d3`, "Write <, > or = between the two fractions." and the fraction lines of `comparing-fractions-d1`, "1/2 of 10 =", the hyphen-minus, the brackets and the expressions of `olympiad-math`, the text of `english-essays` without signs — and on `ru` with synthetic texts of the same kinds; `texts.node.test.ts`, `speech.node.test.ts` and `storage/voicing.node.test.ts`. The browser — `voice.browser.test.tsx`: the button and its line, the section "This device" with the choice, the sample and the permission on Android, a failure of the write, and **the path "no voice" on the engine's real `speechSynthesis`**: in the container Chromium and Firefox have no voices, and WebKit only the four English voices of flite, each `localService: false`, which `app` does not take (measurements 4); in `Task.browser.test.tsx`, what sounds by itself with voicing on and what does not with it off, the learner's button and its failure; accessibility checks — the task screen with voicing on and no voice, and the page "This device", on which the container's Chromium is Linux.

### 3.6. Own key

Decided by R08. An own key is the account owner's key at an AI provider (concepts 14, 15 spec): with it the calls go on behalf of the key's owner, and the provider links each request to their billing account (privacy 5.2 spec). `app` does not call the providers (architecture 3.1 spec): the key goes to `api` with a request and lives there and in `tutor` only in the request's memory — not in the database and not in the logs (architecture 4.3 spec; R04 api). The first app has no own key: the author's work is read by Claude models with a key from an environment variable (R76 spec; [COORDINATION.md](../spec/COORDINATION.md), «Первое приложение»). In the first slice the own key is for adults (product 10 spec; О-4).

**Who has it.**
- Only the owner profile as a learner, of the category `adult`. One account holds both the owner profile and the profiles of their children (R26 spec), and `api` accepts the key only with a request of an `adult` profile and checks this itself (R04 api). The children's profiles have neither a key field nor a line about it: children learn through platform AI (О-4), and an own key is closed to a learner under 18 at every provider (R122 spec).
- An account without an owner profile as a learner connects no own key: the key is needed by the one who learns themselves.
- There is one key, at one provider. To change the key is to enter a new one: the former one is erased only when the new one has passed the check.

**The provider.** The adult chooses it themselves — from the list that `api` gives for the owner profile by the admission function of `tutor` (architecture 3.6 spec; Т-20). Now these are OpenAI and Anthropic: Google's models are closed with an own key too (R47 spec), and so are other providers, until their terms are checked (R65 spec). The list is not built into `app`, so neither a new provider nor self-hosting needs a new build. `app` does not guess the provider by the look of the key: an error would send the key to another provider.

**Connecting** — in the section for adults, on the page "Own AI key" (architecture 3.1 spec). The key belongs to the owner profile, so the page works only when the owner profile is chosen. If another learner is chosen, it says that the key is to be connected in the owner profile and opens it — the PIN of the section for adults has already been asked, and it is the same. Before the key field the page says in words, without links (privacy 1, 7 spec):
- the key goes to NovaTrail's server with each request of your profile that calls AI and lives there only while the request runs — not in the database and not in the logs;
- the provider links each such request to your billing account and your terms with it;
- the PIN does not protect the key: a key saved on this device can be got at by whoever holds the device;
- how to limit the damage: a separate OpenAI project with a hard spending limit and a key of that project, or a separate Anthropic workspace with a monthly limit and a key bound to it alone — not in the default workspace, where a limit cannot be set. An Anthropic key without a binding chooses the workspace by a header of each request, and the limit of one workspace does not hold it. An Anthropic key can be given a lifetime, and OpenAI advises one for its project keys. If the key may have fallen into other hands, it is revoked at the provider (sources 6.1; R55 tutor).

Below the text — the choice of the provider, the key field and the mark "Keep on this device", not set by default. On "Connect" `app` asks `api` to check the key with a request without the learner's content, on behalf of the owner profile — `CheckOwnKey` of `AccountService` —, and the list of the providers that the admission function opens to an own key for this profile is `ListOwnKeyProviders` (R84, R98 spec). The answer is: the key is accepted, the key is wrong, the key has no rights, or the provider is closed for this profile; a value that the build does not know `app` names as a failed check. Beyond the checks `api` lets the account make, the check is refused `CAUSE_KEY_CHECKS_EXHAUSTED` with when the next check opens, `next_key_check` ([the contracts' text](../spec/contracts/README.md), section 15): the page says when — today, tomorrow or on its date, by the device's clock —, or, with no moment given, to try later, and the key stays in its field (R47). The page shows an accepted key by its provider and its last four characters, and never whole.

**Where it lies.**
- By default, only in the tab's memory, in the code module of `app` that holds the key while the owner profile is open. Once the profile has closed, there is no key, and at the next opening the adult enters it again.
- With the mark "Keep on this device", the provider, the key and its last four characters lie in the database of the owner profile (section 3.2), and `app` reads them when the owner profile is open. The mark belongs to this device: on another one the adult decides again. It can be removed or set on the page "Own AI key" at any moment: a removed mark erases the key from the database, while in memory the key stays as long as the owner profile is open; a mark set puts the key from memory into the database.
- Whether the device is shared only the adult knows, so the adult decides — as with the permission of voicing on Android and Linux (section 3.5).
- The key is never in localStorage or sessionStorage (section 3.2), in the page's address, in the logs and error messages of `app`, or in the export. The widgets of modules, from stage 4, work in the iframe sandbox with a unique opaque origin (architecture 3.4, 8 spec) and do not see the storages of `app`.

**What protects the key — and what does not.** There is no encryption in the browser, and the page "Own AI key" says so plainly. The origin's storage is one for the whole app, and whoever holds the device reads it with the browser's tools (section 3.2). A non-extractable Web Crypto key would close only exporting it: the specification itself says that "any application that shares the same scheme, host, and port have access to the same storage partition" and that "Any application or user that has access to the device storage may be able to recover the key material" (sources 6.1). The PIN is a barrier of the interface (R07 canvas-api; R04 api). The damage is held by other measures:
- the key goes only with the owner profile, and `api` checks this once more;
- by default there is no key on the device;
- the owner profile is locked by the PIN and closes by itself — below;
- the spending limit at the provider holds the key on all devices, even a leaked key of its own project or workspace, if the key is bound to it (R55 tutor);
- the key is revoked at the provider: at OpenAI a revocation takes effect "within a few seconds" (sources 6.1).

**The request.**
- The key and the provider go with each request of the owner profile that calls AI: with the work for reading, with the analysis and the hint by rung, and with "explain differently", which calls AI without the work (R10 api; architecture 4.1 spec). `app` names the provider without the key already at the beginning of the attempt, so that `api` chooses the AI route (R84 spec). In the protocol this is the field `own_key` — the key and the provider — on each such request and the field `own_key_provider`, the provider without the key, on `BeginAttempt`; to a request of another profile `api` answers with the refusal `CAUSE_KEY_NOT_FOR_PROFILE` ([contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md), section 15; R84 spec).
- Only the code module that holds the key gives it out, and only while the owner profile is chosen. A request of a child's profile never carries the key, and if it did, `api` would not accept it (R04 api).
- The key goes only to `api`: `app` has no addresses other than `api` and the sign-in service (architecture 3.1 spec).

**A refusal of the key** — without silently substituting platform AI.
- The refusal comes as the outcome "limit reached" with a cause — the owner's limit, the provider's tier limit, the balance or the payment — or as a refusal of the key itself: wrong, revoked, not accepted for this profile (R55 tutor; R84 spec). The owner as a learner sees what architecture 4.1 spec prescribes: at reading, "unchecked" and the neutral phrase, at the analysis and the hint, the rung's fallback text, and beside it a line with the cause.
- In the section for adults, beside the key — the line of the limit, when access returns, if the provider's answer names it (R80 api), and what to do about that limit: raise the spending limit, top up the balance, check the payment, or, at the tier's ceiling, replace or remove the key. `api` names the time beside the ceiling of the tier alone, 00:00 UTC on the first day of the next month, and never beside the owner's own limit (R80 api); `app` says it by the device's clock and in the screen's language — today, tomorrow or on its date, as the quota does (R37) —, and with no time, or one already past, none (R47).
- `app` moves to platform AI only by an action of the owner — "Remove key" on the page "Own AI key" or "This device": "Don't use own key" is the same action (R08). Platform AI means other contracts and the platform's quota (concepts 14 spec), and `app` will not spend it behind the owner's back.
- `app` does not count the spending of the own key: the platform keeps no limit of its own and accumulates no spending of the own key — neither `tutor`, nor `api`, nor the device (R55 tutor).

**Replacing and deleting** — on the page "Own AI key". A replacement is a new key with a check: the former one is erased from memory and from the database only when the new one is accepted, and a new key that has not passed the check changes nothing. "Remove key" — on the page "Own AI key" and at the owner profile on the page "This device", one and the same action — erases the key from memory and from the device. At the provider the key stays working, and the page advises revoking it there if it is no longer needed.

Each tab has its own key in memory. A deletion, a replacement and a removed mark `app` reports to its other tabs through `BroadcastChannel`: they erase the key from memory, and after a replacement take the new one from the database, if it is stored there, or ask for it to be entered again.

**When it is erased** — the events of section 3.2, since the key lies in the database of the owner profile:
- "Remove key" and a replacement;
- the closing of the owner profile — the key in memory, without the mark;
- the deletion of the owner profile on this device and on another one, "Erase the profile's data on this device" and "Erase everything" on the page "This device";
- the mark "Keep on this device" removed — the copy in the database;
- a sign-in of another account, the deletion of the account and the answer of `api` that the account is gone (architecture 4.5 spec); the sign-out of the account owner leaves it;
- the browser itself, when it erases the origin's storage (section 3.2).

The key has no lifetime of its own on the device: the key's lifetime is set by the provider — at Anthropic it is chosen when the key is issued (sources 6.1).

**The export** — without the key and without any note of it. The export is learners' data (architecture 4.5 spec; R09 spec), and the key is the owner's secret (privacy 5.2 spec): an export file is stored and forwarded.

**The owner profile on a shared device** (R84 spec): the side of `api` is R04 api, the side of `app` R08, and the providers' terms allow giving an own key to `api` (privacy 10 spec). The PIN and the closing below come with the children's profiles, E4 of [run/EPICS.md](../run/EPICS.md). The subject of the rights is the account: there is one token, and the profile is a parameter of the request. The PIN is a barrier of the interface in `app`, one for all devices: 4–6 digits, a hash in `api`, no more than 5 failed tries in 15 minutes. The side of `app`:
- if the account holds at least one profile whose category is not `adult`, the owner profile stands in the choice of the learner with a lock and opens with the same PIN as the section for adults — at every opening;
- the owner profile closes, and the key leaves memory, on "Switch learner" and on a reload — always: the chosen learner lives only in memory anyway (section 3.2). On an account with a child's profile, also when the page has been hidden for no less than the time the adult set on this device — by default 5 minutes: for example, the screen went dark or the adult went to another app. The adult changes the time on the page "This device" — 5, 15, 30 or 60 minutes: closing for protection is also a time limit by WCAG 2.2.1, and 2.2.1 allows turning the limit off or changing it tenfold, and 60 minutes is twelve times the default (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R84 spec; R08; section 3.9). "Don't close" is not among the times (R84 spec): a child on a shared tablet would stay in the owner profile, on the AI routes for adults and on the owner's own key. `app` learns of the hiding by `visibilitychange`, and on return compares the time by the device's clock, `Date.now()`, not by `performance.now()`: the monotonic clock may not run while the device sleeps. If the clock on return shows less than at the hiding — it was changed —, the profile closes too. 5 minutes is enough to copy the key at the provider in another app and come back;
- while the page is visible, the owner profile does not close by itself: the owner may think over a task for long, and an inactivity timer would close the profile in the middle of work. An adult who hands over a tablet with its screen lit presses "Switch learner": the button is on every screen of the profile — in the bar of buttons, or in the menu of the profile's pill, which stands at the top right of the frame of Home, Subjects and the skill map (R63; R79), the lesson and its end (R64) —, and the header of the owner profile differs from the children's profiles. That the screen lock hides the page, how the clock runs on a sleeping tablet and how often the tablet turns its screen off by itself is checked on devices, Д7;
- the PIN field is `<input type="password" inputmode="numeric">`. Pasting is allowed — it is a mechanism that WCAG 3.3.8 names —, and there are no `autocomplete` hints, so as not to call the password manager needlessly: on a shared tablet it would fill in the PIN for everyone who knows the device's code. The browser may offer to save the password without a hint too, and `app` will not block that — it is a failure of 3.3.8. So the section for adults says plainly: on a shared tablet, do not save the PIN in the browser or a password manager, and delete a saved one. The error says that the PIN is wrong and how many tries are left, and when `api` has closed the input — no more than 5 failed tries in 15 minutes (R04 api) —, when the input opens. This is carried by the answer of `api` to the check, `PinCheck` — whether the PIN matched, how many wrong tries are left and, if this one closed the input, when it opens —, and to a closed input comes the refusal `CAUSE_PIN_LOCKED` with the time (R98 spec). There is no counter on the device: it would diverge from the server's;
- without children's profiles, the owner profile opens without a PIN: there is no child on the account. The mark "Keep on this device" is then too the adult's choice: the device may be someone else's;
- a fresh sign-in instead of the PIN was rejected by R04 api: on a shared tablet every switch would become a sign-in through Google.

**What the child sees.** In the choice of the learner, the owner profile with a lock. In their own profile, nothing about the key: neither a line nor a button, and their AI calls go through platform AI (О-4). The page "Own AI key" is in the section for adults, behind the PIN (architecture 3.1 spec).

**The key's path, step by step.**

| Step | Where the key is | Does it go with a request of a child's profile | What the child sees |
|---|---|---|---|
| entry | the field of the page "Own AI key", in the section for adults behind the PIN | no | nothing: the section is behind the PIN |
| check | a request to `api` with the key and the provider; in `api`, only in the request's memory | no | nothing |
| storage | the tab's memory while the owner profile is open; with the mark, also the database of the owner profile | no | nothing: there is no key in their profile |
| request | with each request of the owner profile that calls AI — in `api` and `tutor`, only in the request's memory, and from there to the provider | no: the code module gives the key only while the owner profile is chosen, and `api` will not accept a key with a profile that is not `adult` | nothing |
| refusal | the key stays in place; the cause — to the owner as a learner and in the section for adults | no | nothing |
| owner profile closed | erased from memory; with the mark, only in the database of the owner profile | no | the owner profile with a lock |
| deletion and erasure | nowhere on the device | no | nothing |

**Two paths.**
- *A shared iPad.* The account holds the owner profile as a learner and a child's profile of the category `under_13`. The owner connects an Anthropic key from a separate workspace with a monthly limit and a lifetime of 30 days, and does not set the mark. After studying, the owner presses "Switch learner" — the key leaves memory —, and the child chooses their own profile: their hints go through platform AI. The child taps the owner profile — a lock and the PIN. In the evening the owner opens their profile with the PIN and enters the key again.
- *A laptop of one's own.* An account without children's profiles. The owner profile opens without a PIN, and the adult sets the mark "Keep on this device": the key lies in the profile's database and is in place after a reload and after signing out and in again. "Erase everything on this device" erases it with the whole account.

**Tests.**
- In Node: `src/ownkey/holder.node.test.ts` — no key without the owner profile open, nor for another profile, and the provider alone where a request takes no key; the key shown by its provider and last four; the device only with the mark, a removed mark erasing it there; a new key in place only once accepted; a key not kept gone on "Switch learner", a reload and the sign-out; another tab's message; a refusal or a limit kept beside the key. `src/storage/ownkey.node.test.ts` — the record, the database of version 1 brought to version 2, and the key gone with the profile's data, everything on the device and the account's erasure.
- In Chromium (section 3.1): `src/adults/OwnKeyPage.browser.test.tsx` — the key in none of the browser's storages without the mark and in the owner profile's database with it, each answer of the check and the checks exhausted, a replacement and a removal, a refusal and a limit beside the key; `DevicePage.browser.test.tsx` — the kept key at the owner profile and its removal; the requests that carry the key or its provider — `Task.browser.test.tsx`, `Lesson.browser.test.tsx` and `NextStep.browser.test.tsx`.
- The closing of the owner profile on a hidden page — after the default time, the one the adult chose and with the clock set back, on a fake clock — is tested with its code, in E4.

**Code** (R47):
- the holder — `src/ownkey/holder.ts`, `KeyHolder`, one per tab, with no DOM: its state — closed, opening or open, with the provider, its name, the last four characters, whether it is kept, and a refusal beside them — never holds the key, which is a private field; `aiFields` gives the key or the clock's offset, and `routeFields` the provider alone or nothing. `src/ownkey/OwnKeyContext.tsx` makes it with its `BroadcastChannel` `novatrail-own-key`, the message `{profileId}`, and `useKeyHolder()` gives it to the screens;
- the gate — `src/account/AccountGate.tsx` opens the holder when the learner chosen is the owner profile `adult`, closes it on every other step, and renders the learner's screens once it is open, so that the first `ChooseNext` already names the provider of a key kept here;
- the requests: `own_key` — `SubmitWork` with a work, `GetRungWords`, `GetCorrectAnswerWords`, `ExplainDifferently` in `src/task/Task.tsx`; `own_key_provider` — `ChooseNext` and `BeginAttempt` in `src/next/useEngine.ts`, `OpenLesson`, `GetLessonRun`, `BeginAttempt` and `CompleteLesson` in `src/lesson/Lesson.tsx`, `BeginAttempt`, `ShowSolution` and `SubmitWork` without a work in `src/task/Task.tsx`, `Done` in `src/task/Essay.tsx`. A refusal of the key or its limit closes the attempt's AI by `closesAI` of `src/api/outcome.ts`, with the line of `src/api/words.ts` — the cause of the limit and when access returns, if the outcome names them, by `dayText` —, and stays beside the key for the page, which says it again with what to do about that limit, `Refusal` in `src/adults/OwnKeyPage.tsx`;
- the page — `src/adults/OwnKeyPage.tsx`, `#/adults/own-key`, from the first page of the section for an account with the owner profile `adult`: the four paragraphs above, the provider from `ListOwnKeyProviders` — or, with an empty list, the line that no provider takes an own key on this server, and no field —, the key in an uncontrolled `<input type="password" autocomplete="off">`, the mark, and the six ends of "Connect" — `CheckOwnKey`'s five and the refusal by the account's checks exhausted, in the alert below the form; connected — the provider and the last four, the mark, "Replace the key" and "Remove key". With no learner chosen it opens the owner profile by "Open my profile", for now without a PIN;
- on the device — `src/storage/ownkey.ts` and `ownKey`, `keepOwnKey` and `dropOwnKey` in `src/storage/profile.ts`; the line of a kept key with "Remove key" — `KeptKeyLine` in `src/adults/DevicePage.tsx`, and "Erase the profile's data" removes the key in memory too;
- on `api`'s stub, in the dev mode: Anthropic at `adult`, `CheckOwnKey` answered by the key's ending (R72, R73 api), and `own_key_limit` on the endings `-limit`, `-limit-owner`, `-limit-tier`, `-limit-balance` and `-limit-payment`, `-limit-tier` with `access_returns` (R66, R80 api). `just walk` connects a key on the stub — one ending in `-invalid` refused, then a synthetic one accepted and kept —, goes through the task of the skill map on it, removes it on «This device», and holds that the key goes with `CheckOwnKey` and the requests that call AI alone (the second 2026-11-14 amendment to R44; section 3.7); then it connects a key ending in `-limit-tier`, checks the task in progress on it, and finds the tier's line with when access comes back on the task and beside the key, before it removes that key — a leg not yet run (Д22).

### 3.7. Deployment configuration

Decided by R09. The build of `app` is one signed archive both for the platform and for self-hosting (architecture 3.1 spec). Everything that depends on the deployment `app` reads at run time: Vite replaces environment variables at build time (section 3.1), and what the build knows is signed together with it. Copies of the platform differ only by the file `/deployment.json` and the hosting's headers.

**The settings** — format 1:

| Field | What | Example | Check |
|---|---|---|---|
| `format` | the version of the file's format; this build understands 1 | `1` | an integer, exactly 1; another number — "this build does not understand this format" |
| `api` | the origin of `api`: requests, open views and the media files of the device part (R62 spec) | `https://api.example.org` | a URL equal to its origin: without a path, a trailing `/`, a query, a fragment or credentials; `https`, and `http` only on loopback — `127.0.0.1`, `localhost` or `[::1]` — for both `api` and `app` itself: otherwise the work and the ID token would go over the network in plain text |
| `signIn.service` | the sign-in service | `firebase` | only `firebase`: another sign-in service is another adapter both in `api` (R03 api) and in the code of `app` |
| `signIn.apiKey`, `signIn.projectId`, `signIn.appId` | the Firebase project's parameters, as the console gives them | — | non-empty strings |
| `signIn.authDomain` | the domain of Firebase's sign-in pages | `example-project.firebaseapp.com` | a host name without a scheme or a path: the SDK itself builds `https://<authDomain>/__/auth/…` |

```json
{
  "format": 1,
  "api": "https://api.example.org",
  "signIn": {
    "service": "firebase",
    "apiKey": "<the Firebase web app's key>",
    "authDomain": "example-project.firebaseapp.com",
    "projectId": "example-project",
    "appId": "<the Firebase web app's ID>"
  }
}
```

- There are no secrets in the file: the browser of everyone who opened the app gets it. Firebase keys "do not need to be treated as secrets", and the Firebase configuration object is "non-secret identifiers" (sources 7.1). Keys of AI providers, the key of the service account of `api` and tokens are never in it (RULES.md, Hard rules).
- There are no fields beyond the format. Google Analytics' `measurementId`, if it was copied from the Firebase console with the rest, is an error, not silently enabled analytics (privacy 1 spec).
- The widgets' domain is stage 4 (R16 spec). It will come with format 2, and `app` will check that its host does not match the hosts of `app` and `api` and is not within them. That the domain is a separate registrable one is checked by the rollout.
- The format is the part of `spec` `deployment/`, and its version 1 is this format, R09 ([the part's text](../spec/deployment/README.md); R85 spec with the amendments of 2026-11-08 and 2026-11-09). The build has no schema of its own: it carries a copy of `schemas/deployment.schema.json` from the release of `spec` it stands on, as `deployment.schema.json` at the archive's root, and by it names the version it reads (the second 2026-11-09 amendment to R85 spec).
- At the platform the file names `api` by its Cloud Run address, `https://<service>.run.app`, and `authDomain` as `<project>.firebaseapp.com`; the origin of `app` itself is `https://<project>.web.app`, and only it does `api` let through by CORS.

**Where from and how it is read.**
- The file lies at the root of the origin of `app`, beside `index.html`, but not in the release archive: the rollout writes it, not the build. `app` itself also stands at the root of its origin: the browser's storage is one per origin, and "Erase everything on this device" erases all of the origin's localStorage and sessionStorage (section 3.2), so `app` cannot share its origin with another app.
- First of all at the start — before the sign-in and before any other request — `app` calls `fetch("/deployment.json", { cache: "no-cache" })`. In the mode `no-cache` "Fetch creates a conditional request if there is a response in the HTTP cache" (sources 7.1): a new file after a rollout `app` sees at the next opening, even if the hosting caches longer. The path is absolute, from the origin's root: the screens live in the address's hash, and the document always stands at the root (R14), but even so the file does not depend on where it is asked from.
- The answer is good only if its code is 200, `redirected` is false, the MIME type is `application/json`, and the body is no larger than 16 KiB, tens of times more than needed. The type is compared without parameters: `application/json; charset=utf-8` is good. The size `app` measures by the body read, not by `Content-Length`: with compression and chunked transfer this header may be absent. The usual SPA fallback serves `index.html` with the code 200 for a missing file, and the type cuts it off. An answer with the code 5xx, 408 or 429 is like a network failure: the hosting may come back to life, wait for the request or stop throttling, and `app` offers to try again.
- The file is read at every start and is not put into storage. `app` has no service worker: there is no offline mode in the first slice (product 10 spec).

**The check at the start** is strict:
- the fields are exactly those that format 1 names. An unknown, extra or missing field is an error, and the error line names the field's path, for example `signIn.authDomain`;
- there are no defaults, and the platform's address is not in the build: a copy without the file will go neither to the platform's `api` nor to its Firebase project;
- the check comes before any request to the network: a wrong file does not get as far as `initializeApp`.

The rules are written in the schema of the part `deployment/`, a copy of which lies in the build, and the schema is their one source for both `app` and the rollout. If `app` checks the file with its own code rather than with the schema itself, the tests run the part's examples, `examples/deployment/`, and its reading vectors, `vectors/reading/`, through the code: each case has the origin where `app` is open, the file, and what the reading gives ([the part's text](../spec/deployment/README.md), sections 5–7). Only one rule is checked at run time alone — `http` only with `app` open on loopback: the schema does not know where `app` is open, and the platform's rollout does not write `http`. Just as strictly `api` checks the facts file by R57 tutor: «опечатка в имени факта не должна молча закрыть путь» (sources 7.1).

**Trust in the file.** The signature holds the build's path up to the hosting (R77 spec; R106 spec), and the browser does not check signatures: it gets `/deployment.json` from the same hosting as `index.html`. The file cannot be checked by a hash, like `fetch` with `integrity`: the hash would have to be known at build time, and each deployment has its own file. A wrong file would take the learners' work to someone else's server, so three measures hold it:
- the rollout checks the file against the schema from the same signed build;
- the rollout list names the file's digest beside the build's digest, and a change of the file goes through review, like the whole list (R106 spec);
- the CSP header with exact addresses lets the page go nowhere but `api` and the sign-in service.

**What the learner sees.** The failure screens are static, from the build itself, in the device's language: they have neither a profile nor a sign-in yet (section 3.8). The words follow the rules and the glossary of section 3.8, without addressing the child, and the line for whoever deployed the copy is in English (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R09). Each is a white card of 460, radius 28, in the window's middle, its heading at 22 bold and what to do the primary button of its width (R69).

| What happened | How `app` learns of it | What the learner sees | Requests to the network |
|---|---|---|---|
| `app` is open in a frame of someone else's page | `window.top !== window.self`, even before the file | a static screen without the interface and without data | none |
| no connection | the request for the file did not get through, or the hosting answered with the code 5xx, 408 or 429 | "No connection" and the button "Try again" | only the retry for the file |
| there is no file or it is not the right one | a code not 200, not 5xx, not 408 and not 429, a redirect, a type not `application/json`, more than 16 KiB, not JSON | "This app isn't set up" and the line "A grown-up needs to help", and below, the line for whoever deployed the copy: what is wrong | none |
| the file is wrong | the check at the start | the same screen, and the line names the field | none |
| the build does not understand `api` | `api` answered `unimplemented` or sent an open view of a format version that the build does not read (R83 spec; [contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md), section 15) | an offer to reload, no more often than once per opening of the tab: the contract does not carry the version of `api` | — |
| after a rollout a file of the former build is gone | the event `vite:preloadError` (sources 7.1) | an offer to reload | — |

`app` does not reload the page itself: in the middle of a task only the learner's tap does it, and by then the work is already in the profile's database (section 3.2). The screen "This app isn't set up" erases nothing on the device: when the file is fixed, the learner continues from the same place.

**CSP** — in two layers. The policies of `<meta>` and of the header act together: a request goes through only if both let it, and the second policy "can only further restrict" the first (sources 7.1).

| Directive | The lower layer — `<meta>` in the signed `index.html` | The hosting's header by `csp.json` | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| `default-src` | `'none'` | the same | everything not named is closed |
| `script-src` | `'self' https://apis.google.com` | the same | the app's own modules and Vite's `modulepreload` are `'self'`; the gapi script is loaded by the sign-in SDK; without `'unsafe-inline'` and `'unsafe-eval'` |
| `style-src` | `'self'` | the same | the build's CSS is in files; the styles of the canvas element are `adoptedStyleSheets`, outside `style-src` (the second 2026-11-02 amendment to R36 spec), and the element sets the sheet's layout by its properties `--across` and `--direction` through the CSSOM, `style.setProperty` — under the header without violations, by the test of the built page in the three engines |
| `img-src` | `'self' blob:` | the same | the pictures of the canvas and of the lesson's explanations are at `blob:` addresses (architecture 3.3 canvas; section 3.4); their files go through `fetch`, so the address of `api` is not needed here |
| `connect-src` | `'self' https:` | `'self' {api} https://identitytoolkit.googleapis.com https://securetoken.googleapis.com` | the requests to `api` and to Firebase Auth; the build does not know the address of `api` |
| `frame-src` | `https:` | `https://{authDomain}` | the hidden iframe of Firebase Auth |
| `font-src` | `'none'` | the same | there are no fonts of its own — only the system's (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R09; section 3.9) |
| `object-src`, `base-uri`, `form-action` | `'none'` | the same | no plugins, no substitution of the base address, no sending of forms |
| `frame-ancestors` | none: it does not work in `<meta>` | `'none'` | `app` does not open in a frame of someone else's page |

- The lower layer stands in `<head>` right after `<meta charset>`: a `<meta>` policy is "not applied to content which precedes them", and Vite puts its scripts, `modulepreload` and styles at the end of `<head>` (sources 7.1). The build plugin checks this (section 3.1).
- `csp.json` is an object "directive → string of sources" with the places `{api}` and `{authDomain}`: this form is what the rollout of `infra` reads (R17 infra; the third 2026-11-09 amendment to R85 spec). It did not become part of the format `deployment/`: it follows the build's code, not the deployment ([the part's text](../spec/deployment/README.md), section 1). The plugin builds the lower layer from it too: a place becomes `https:`, and `frame-ancestors` goes. The rollout builds the header by substituting the file's values. So the list of directives of `app` and of the rollout is one, and a new version of `app` that needs a new address brings it itself. A change of the form of `csp.json` or of the archive's layout `app` writes as a row «в реестр spec» before the release that carries it: otherwise the rollout will refuse the build both at the check of the pull request and at the release.
- `build.assetsInlineLimit: 0`: by default Vite inlines small files as `data:` addresses (sources 7.1), and the CSP does not allow them.
- Vite's nonce, `html.cspNonce`, does not fit: the value has to change "for each request" (sources 7.1), and `app` has no server of its own.
- Where the hosting sends no headers of its own, only the lower layer acts: the app's own scripts and the sign-in script run, and others do not, while requests go to any `https:`. Custom response headers are set, for example, by Firebase Hosting with the attribute `headers`, by Cloudflare Pages with the file `_headers`, by nginx with `add_header` (sources 7.1). Against a frame of someone else's page only the check of `window.top` then holds. Such hosting does not suit the platform, and self-hosting on it is weaker protection, and the operator has to know this (Т-17). The platform stands on Firebase Hosting (R128 spec).

**Sign-in** — by a pop-up window (R09).
- The SDK is the packages `@firebase/app` and `@firebase/auth` at the versions of `package.json`, those that a release of `firebase` pins, not `firebase` itself (R15). What is derived from the code below is read from the SDK's code, not checked on devices (Д8; sources 2.3).
- `app` sets Firebase up so: `initializeApp` with the parameters from `signIn`, explicitly; `initializeAuth` with `indexedDBLocalPersistence` (section 3.2) and `browserPopupRedirectResolver`; the sign-in is `signInWithPopup` with the Google provider. `getAuth` is not used: it looks for parameters and an emulator in the environment itself, in `__FIREBASE_DEFAULTS__`, while `app` has its parameters only from the file. The `postinstall` script of the package `@firebase/util`, which reads `FIREBASE_WEBAPP_CONFIG`, does not run: `.npmrc` has `ignore-scripts=true` (section 3.1).
- `authDomain` is `<project>.firebaseapp.com`: the window opens the sign-in page there, `https://<authDomain>/__/auth/handler`, and the hosting of `app` needs nothing. Sign-in by redirect is rejected (R09): it needs `authDomain` on the domain of `app` and `/__/auth/` on the hosting.
- The domain of `app` has to stand in the authorized domains of the Firebase project: the SDK compares the page's origin with this list, and an IP address only by an exact match (sources 7.1). For the local run `127.0.0.1` stands there. This is a step of the operator of each deployment (R106 spec).
- What the device calls at Google (sources 7.1, 7.5) — the script `https://apis.google.com/js/api.js`, the hidden iframe `https://<authDomain>/__/auth/iframe`, the window `https://<authDomain>/__/auth/handler` with Google's sign-in pages in it, and requests to `identitytoolkit.googleapis.com` and `securetoken.googleapis.com`; in WebKit, Google's page in that iframe also asks `www.googleapis.com` (measurements 3). On an iPad, in Safari and in mobile browsers the SDK loads the script and the iframe at every start, even when there is a sign-in already. The script runs in the origin of `app`, with all its rights. The sign-in service writes the IP address into its logs itself, and their retention periods are in the written retention policy (R128 spec; privacy 4 spec), and on an iPad it gets this address at every start. These addresses are named by architecture 3.12 spec.
- Firebase stores the session under a key that contains `apiKey` (sources 7.1): with a change of `apiKey` the former record would stay in its database, with the refresh token, and `signOut()` and the erasure of R04 would not find it. So the device's registry remembers the `projectId` and `apiKey` with which the sign-in went. If they are different in the file, `app`, before `initializeAuth`, erases the Firebase Auth database and the origin's web storage, as an erasure of the account does, and does not touch the learners' data, and the owner signs in again. Another Firebase project is another account, and R04 erases its former data at the sign-in (section 3.2).
- Firebase Auth does not close its database `firebaseLocalStorageDb` on `versionchange` — only on `pagehide` (sources 3.2). While another tab holds the former project, the erasure of this database gets `blocked`: the deletion itself will go through when that tab closes, and until then `app` counts it as not done — it does not clear the web storage, the registry keeps the former project, the next launch compares again, and the sign-in in this tab is not set up (R16). The comparison is made by the storage's launch (section 3.2, "Code"), and `Boot` sets up the sign-in.
- Whether the window is closed the SDK checks by its reference to it, and the header `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy: same-origin` breaks this reference — this is derived from the SDK's code (Д8). So the hosting of `app` does not send such a header (Т-17).
- On a computer the SDK calls Google with nothing before a tap of "Sign in with Google": the gapi script and the hidden iframe come with the window, and then the SDK checks the origin with the request `getProjectConfig` to `identitytoolkit.googleapis.com`. This is just what the test of the built page checks, under the CSP header, with a network log and Google's answers as stubs (sources 7.5) — and in WebKit, which the SDK takes for Safari, the start's loads. On an iPad and Android it is not checked (Д8).
- gapi's module now and then sends Google a usage counter, a `fetch` to `https://apis.google.com/js/gen_204`, wherever it runs: in one flush of a hundred, and nothing waits on it (sources 7.6). The header's `connect-src` does not name it and refuses it — Google gets less, and the sign-in does not need it. The test of the built page and `site-check` take this one refusal in WebKit, where gapi runs at every start, for no violation (R45; measurements 5).
- `@firebase/app` also keeps its own database on the device, `firebase-heartbeat-database`: once a day, the versions of the SDK's packages, not the browser string, with the UTC date, up to 30 records. It sends them in the header `X-Firebase-Client` with the requests to Firebase Auth — the same sign-in service that gets the IP address — and erases them after sending; there is no switch for this (sources 7.5). There is no learner's data in it, but it is one more thing the sign-in service learns about the device: the days when `app` was opened. `spec` holds it as part of the sign-in service's processing — privacy 2 spec, architecture 3.12 spec and the DPIA (R26).

**One signed build — at the platform and in self-hosting.**
1. The release: `release-files` packs the build — at the archive's root `index.html` with the lower layer, `assets/`, `deployment.schema.json` and `csp.json`, without `deployment.json`: this layout is what the rollout of `infra` reads (R17 infra; the third 2026-11-09 amendment to R85 spec), — and also `NOTICE` and `LICENSE` (R25; the 2026-11-11 amendment to R85 spec): the rollout does not reject extra files and releases them to the site, at `/NOTICE` and `/LICENSE`. The shared `release.yml` of `.github` signs the archive (section 3.1; R77 spec).
2. The rollout of `infra` by the rollout list (R106 spec): it checks the archive's signature (R77 spec) and lays it out on the hosting, takes the file's values from the list and checks them against `deployment.schema.json` of this build, writes `/deployment.json`, builds the CSP header by `csp.json` and sets the cache headers (Т-17; R106 spec). It takes the file's schema from the submodule `spec` beside the build's schema and refuses a build of another format version. The build, its file and the header go out in one release of the hosting: the strict check will not accept a file of another format, and between two steps of the rollout each new opening would show "This app isn't set up". A rollback is the former list: the former build with its own file, also in one release.
3. Self-hosting takes the same archive and checks it with the same recipe. The operator writes their own `deployment.json` — their own `api` and their own Firebase project: without a Firebase project the copy will not sign in (Д4 api) —, puts the copy's domain into the project's authorized domains and sends the same header, if the hosting can.
4. The build's bytes are the same for everyone: nothing in them depends on the deployment, and only `deployment.json` and the header differ.

**The format version together with `api`.**
- While the format is v0, `app` and `api` move to a new version together: if the open view changed, a new `app` with a new `canvas` comes out together with `api` "in one list of exact versions (Т-19)" (repositories 4 spec; R44 spec). The list pins the build of `app` and the image of `api` together, and there are no versions in `deployment.json`: the file says where `api` is, not which it is.
- The hosting serves `index.html` with `no-cache` (Т-17), so a new opening takes the new build. An open tab may hold the former one, and that `api` has moved on it learns from `unimplemented` or an unknown format version, and `app` offers to reload (R83 spec).
- By R106 spec (Т-19), a build of `app` that moved to a new format version rolls back only forward, and within a format version a rollback is a revert, because within a major contract package an old build of `app` works with the new `api` (R83 spec).
- Self-hosting takes the pair from the same list and rolls it out the same way.

**The local run** is in the devcontainer, without a deployment (R76 spec):
- an own Vite plugin (section 3.1) serves `/deployment.json` from `deployment.local.json` both on the development server and in `vite preview`. This file is in `.gitignore`, and the repository holds `deployment.example.json` with places for the values;
- `api` in it is the origin of `app` itself, `http://127.0.0.1:<port>`: `server.proxy` and `preview.proxy` lead the paths of `api` to the local `api`. Otherwise it cannot work on loopback: the format accepts `http` on any loopback port, while the lower layer of the signed build lets `api` through over `http` only as `'self'` — it puts `https:` instead of the place ([the part `deployment/`](../spec/deployment/README.md), section 5; R26). So the lower layer in `vite preview` too lets the requests through as `'self'`, and `http` is allowed because both `app` and `api` are on loopback. The development server and `vite preview` have one port — `server.port` and `preview.port` with `strictPort: true` —, so the file suits both: a second server on a busy port fails to start instead of silently moving to another one. The plugin checks that `api` in the file is the origin of the server that serves it;
- `signIn` is a dev Firebase project on the Spark plan, sign-in through Google (R03 api), and `127.0.0.1` stands in its authorized domains. On the development server, «Sign in with Google» signs in by the custom token of `api`'s test account instead, while `api`'s `just dev-app` keeps one in the volume `novatrail-dev-token` that the container of `app` mounts read-only (R67 api): the development server hands it out at `/__novatrail/custom-token`, and the sign-in takes it by `signInWithCustomToken`, with no Google window, so that the path runs in a browser without the author (R146 spec; R44). Without the token the Google window opens; `vite preview` and a release's build have no such path;
- on the development server there is no lower layer: the React Refresh preamble is an inline script. `vite preview` serves the build with the lower layer and the header built by `csp.json` the same way as the rollout will build it — so both layers are checked locally;
- a working copy of `canvas` — by the recipe `just dev-canvas`, section 3.1 (R15), and the local platform of `infra` (architecture 3.11 spec) will take the same file format;
- the local `api` is in its own devcontainer, on the Docker network `novatrail-api`, on the port 8080 of all interfaces and without a published port (sources 7.5). The container of `app` stands on two networks, its own and `novatrail-api`, with `--network-alias=app` (R19, R43), and the proxy goes to `NOVATRAIL_API`, without it to `http://api:8080`, the container of `api` by its name on the network (R44). The requests to `api` go from the origin of `app`, behind its proxy, so they need no CORS; `API_APP_ORIGIN` of `api` is the origin of `app` on loopback, `http://127.0.0.1:5173`, as README of `api` has it in «Run on the dev projects», and rejects nothing there.

**Code**:
- the reading of the file — `src/config/deployment.ts`, without the DOM: the schema step by its own code, with the `pattern` patterns from the schema itself — a test compares them with the submodule's schema —, and all the paths it rejects, in the order of code units; then the rules `deployment.api-origin` and `deployment.loopback`. The request and the answer — `src/config/load.ts`: the code, the redirect, the type, 16 KiB by the body read, UTF-8 and JSON; a cut-off body is like a network failure;
- the start — `src/boot/Boot.tsx`: in a frame, a screen without requests, otherwise the file before everything else, and the app gets it through `useDeployment()` only once read. The failure screens — `src/boot/Screens.tsx`, their words are the dictionaries of `src/i18n/`, by the glossary of section 3.8, and the device's language is the choice in `novatrail-settings` (section 3.2), otherwise the lookup of RFC 4647 by `navigator.languages` (section 3.8, "Code"). The line for whoever deployed the copy is with `lang="en"`, and `<html lang>` is the screen's language. The offer to reload stands above any screen, and `vite:preloadError` `app` suppresses itself (`preventDefault`). Where no screen is known yet — the start, the session reading the account, the owner's key opening —, `src/app/Waiting.tsx`, the mockup's wait: the sky or the page at once, the spark after 0.3 s, never a white page (R78);
- the part `deployment/` — from a release file of `spec`, `parts/deployment-1.tar.gz` with its signature `.sigstore.json` beside it, not from the submodule: the code and the tests take the files of `spec` from its releases (R21 spec), and only the job "TypeScript / licenses" has the submodule in CI. The file enters the code only once `release-verify` has accepted it (RULES.md, Hard rules): `ci-licenses` calls `just -f spec/justfile release-verify spec <tag> parts/deployment-1.tar.gz`, with the submodule's tag from `spec_tag` of the `justfile`. `scripts/archive.ts` reads the archive, and the plugin puts the schema from it into the build. A move to a new tag of `spec` takes the file of the same release and moves `spec_tag`;
- the template — `csp.json` at the repository's root, and both layers — `scripts/csp.ts`: the lower layer puts `https:` instead of a source with a place and then removes the other `https://…` of the same directive, the header substitutes the values and adds `frame-ancestors 'none'` if the template does not have it. The template's form is checked by the same rules as at the rollout: a directive is a name, a value is one non-empty string without `;` and `,`, the places are only `{api}` and `{authDomain}`, `frame-ancestors` is only `'none'` (R17 infra);
- the plugin — `scripts/deployment-plugin.ts`, after Vite's HTML plugin: at build time it puts in the lower layer and stops the build if the layer is not the first after `<meta charset>`, and puts into the build `csp.json` and `deployment.schema.json` from the release file of `spec`, below. On the development server and in `vite preview` it serves `deployment.local.json` with `application/json` and `no-cache`, and a file whose `api` is not the origin of this server it does not serve: 404 and a line in Vite's log. `vite preview` also sends the header by `csp.json` with the values of `deployment.local.json`;
- the client of `api` — `src/api/` (R18): the connect-es clients of the services of `novatrail.api.v1`, on the Connect transport for the browser, binary protobuf, a deadline of 30 s — the limit of `api` itself —, and of 90 s for `FindTasks` and `SubmitWork`, theirs (R73). The interceptors, from the outside in: the device's actions — "account gone" erases everything of the account and signs out, "no access" erases what is left of the former one, `unimplemented` is the offer to reload, except for `GetLegalTexts`, for which it is "no text in force" (R27), `GetQuota` (R40), `PutWork` (R18) and `GetDebug` (R72); the sign-in — the ID token in `Authorization` on everything except the four open views and the legal texts, and `unauthenticated` without a cause — one refresh of the token and one retry, the cause is read before; a retry of `unavailable` and of a network failure after pauses of 0.5, 1, 2 and 4 s, with the same message, and "the request is still going" — up to the limit of its method, 30 or 90 s, with the pause from `Retry-After`; a call that its deadline cut off is not retried. A failure of a call is `ApiError` with the field `failure`, and its words and the words of the outcomes are `src/api/words.ts` (section 3.8, the glossary). The screens take the client through `useApi()`, and the reads through `useRequest()`;
- the proxy of the paths of `api` — the services of the contract, `/novatrail.…`, and the files of the device parts of packages, `/packages/…` — on both servers, to the address of "The local run" above (R19, R44);
- the debug mode — `src/debug/` (R72; R163 spec): the button and the window of the models of a task from paper, in every build, for an account with `api`'s debug right, in every profile of it: `gate.ts`, in the main chunk, asks `GetDebug` once an account, and the window's code, a chunk of its own, loads only once it answered; any refusal — `permission_denied` without the right, `unimplemented` beside the stub of `tutor` — shows nothing and is no error; the calls, each attempt of a call apart (R73), and their sum are the tab's memory, and a Check exports as a case of `tutor-eval`;
- the sign-in — `src/signin/` (R19): `service.ts` is the narrow interface of the sign-in service, `firebase.ts` is that service on Firebase Auth, with the window's error codes, `SignInScreen.tsx` is the sign-in screen, with the language's pill (section 3.8) and Google's button (R61). `local.ts` is the local build's sign-in by `api`'s development token, which `firebase.ts` loads by a dynamic import under `import.meta.env.DEV` alone, so that a build has neither the branch nor the module; `token-path.ts` is its path, which `scripts/local-token.ts`, the development server's plugin, serves from the volume's file, read at each request (R44). `Boot` makes the service one per page and only after comparing the project with the registry, and at `blocked` shows the screen of another tab; the window's language is the screen's language. The account — `src/account/` (sections 3.2 and 3.10);
- the open views of a package — the four methods of `PackageService` — and the legal texts, `GetLegalTexts`, go by a transport of their own: Connect GET, without a token, without `Connect-Timeout-Ms` and without a single header, with the digest in the address, so that the request is simple, without a preflight, and the HTTP cache can hold its answer ([contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md), section 15). Its retry, `CAUSE_PACKAGE_UNKNOWN` and `unimplemented` are the same as the client's. `deployment.example.json` is a sample of `deployment.local.json` with places for the values.

**Tests.**
- In Node:
  - the check of the file — every rule: an extra and a missing field, an unknown `service`, `api` with a path or `http` not on loopback, another `format` —, and all the examples and reading vectors of the part `deployment/` from the file of its release;
  - an answer with a code not 200, not 5xx, not 408 and not 429, with a redirect, not `application/json` or with a body larger than 16 KiB is the screen "This app isn't set up", and 5xx and a network failure are "No connection"; before the check there is not one request except for the file itself;
  - a `projectId` or `apiKey` other than in the registry erases the Firebase Auth database and the web storage, but not the profiles' databases;
  - the building of the header and of the lower layer by `csp.json`, and the plugin of the local run refuses if `api` in `deployment.local.json` is not the origin of its server.
- The built page: a test in Node starts `vite preview` and opens the page through `playwright`, which is installed anyway (section 3.1), in Chrome Headless Shell, WebKit and Firefox in turn (R45); each engine leaves its own trace in a frame it refuses (measurements 4). A CSP violation — the event `securitypolicyviolation` — fails the test. The page in a frame shows its screen, and without the file and with a wrong file, the failure screens. Also the sign-in window under the header: Google's answers are stubs, and a request to a host that is not in the list above fails the test. No file of the build names the path of the development token (R44).
- The path in a browser — `just walk` by hand, not in CI: it needs `api`'s `just dev-app` (R44). `walk/path.test.ts` starts the development server of `vite.config.ts` and walks the first slice's path in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox, each in a new context, signed in by the development token: Home with the line of the tutor's time, a lesson — Continue, back by its bar, a reload and «Continue the lesson», its tasks ended by their solution —, a task of the skill map in handwriting as the stub of `tutor` reads its crop — square «Can't read this», taller than wide «Not yet» with the tutor's words and «Explain differently», wider than tall «Correct» (R66 api) —, «Try it again», a reload and «Continue the task» with the strokes kept, the section for adults with the tutor's time and the progress by the lesson's name, the sign-out with no question and the account's databases kept, and «Erase everything on this device» with the heartbeats' database gone once the tab is closed. A request to a host the sign-in does not name, or an error in the console, fails it. A response of 4xx of the origin fails it in every engine unless it is `api`'s refusal with a cause the build knows, `Refusal` in Connect's error, which it notes; the line Chromium and WebKit log for such a response is left to the response. A lesson the profile has open, by the last `GetAccount`, is continued by «Continue the lesson» in place of «What's next», back by the bar to the segment it opened at, and a task an earlier walk left is begun again; the walk notes which of the two it was. A task's beginning refused for the quota of the day stops it with the quota's message (R44; measurements 6). It walks an own key too (section 3.6), and keeping works switched on, its `PutWork` and "This device" with no warning (section 3.2). Its AI calls go to the stub and spend the quota of the day of the test account's profile (R55 api): with it spent, the walk stops at its first step and says so. What it found is measurements 3.
- In CI: the lower layer stands in `<head>` right after `<meta charset>`, and there is no `deployment.json` in the archive. `ci-test` builds the tree once more into its own folder, packs both builds and compares the bytes and the layout (`scripts/release.ts --check`). The code of the rollout of `infra` itself does not run in CI: its `unpackBuild` and `cspHeader` (`tools/deploy-check/check.mjs` of `infra`) accepted the archive once, by hand (sources 7.5), and a divergence from it is caught by the check of the pull request and of the release of `infra`.
- The deployed site — by hand after a rollout, `just site-check <origin> <version>` (section 3.1; R42), not in CI: it goes to the network. `scripts/site.ts` holds the site to the release's archive that `release-verify` accepted:
  - `/` and `/index.html` are `index.html` of the release, with `no-cache` and exactly the CSP header that `csp.json` of the release gives with the values of `/deployment.json`, `frame-ancestors 'none'` included;
  - every other file of the archive — `assets/`, `csp.json`, `deployment.schema.json`, `NOTICE` and `LICENSE` — is at its path, in the release's bytes;
  - `/deployment.json` is read by `loadDeployment`, as at the start;
  - no answer sends `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy: same-origin`;
  - in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox the page starts without a violation of the CSP and asks nothing but its origin before the tap of "Sign in with Google" — WebKit also what the SDK loads for Safari —, the first of it after `/` and the build being `/deployment.json`;
  - after the tap the page asks no host but those above, the window opens on `https://<authDomain>/__/auth/handler`, the hidden iframe of `authDomain` comes into the page, and the project's `authorizedDomains` let the site's host in by the SDK's rule above; then the window is closed, and nobody signs in. What the window itself asks is Google's pages, under their own policy.

  Its own test, `scripts/site.test.ts`, runs the same on a server of the test's that serves the archive of `build/` with the headers of the rollout of `infra`, with Google's answers as the stubs of `scripts/google-stub.ts`, which the test of the built page shares. The check holds a release to the code of the tree it runs from — the label of the button, `loadDeployment`, `scripts/csp.ts` —, while the header's directives come from `csp.json` of the release itself.

### 3.8. Interface languages

Decided by R10. The interface of `app` is in English and Russian, by the recommendation of О-2; new interface languages are stage 5 (product 11 spec). A learner has three languages — the interface language, the explanation language and the target language (concepts 13 spec) —, and `app` decides only the first. A module has one explanation language, a BCP 47 tag in the manifest, and the same content in another language is another module, with its own progress (R46 spec). There is no target language in the slice: it has no language modules (R41 spec).

**Which text is in which language.**

| Text | Language | Where the language comes from | `lang` |
|---|---|---|---|
| the strings of `app` on the learner's screens, verdicts and reasons | the learner's interface language | the profile's field; with no field, the device's language | `<html lang>` |
| the legal pages `#/legal` — the list, the sheet's head, the strings of the pages of texts and of notices | the device's: the sign-in screen and the section for adults open them (R31) | the adult's choice on this device, otherwise `navigator.languages`, otherwise English | `<html lang>`; `NOTICE` and `LICENSE` have `en` |
| the module's license page — `#/license/<digest>` and `#/adults/license/<digest>`: the sheet's head and the strings | the language of the screen it leads back to: from Subjects, the learner's interface language, from the section for adults, the device's, and before a learner is chosen, the device's too (R32) | as on those screens | `<html lang>`; `LICENSE` and `NOTICE` have `en`, the module's name has the explanation language from the manifest |
| the page about the tutor `#/tutor` — the sheet's head and the learning materials | the learner's interface language while a learner is chosen, otherwise the device's (R33) | as on those screens; the text is the dictionaries of `app` | `<html lang>` |
| the page "Accessibility" | the language of the screen it was opened from | the text is the dictionaries of `app`: the build's standard and nonconformances are the same in all copies; whom to contact is the copy's operator, from `api` (R115 spec) | `<html lang>` |
| the screens before a learner is chosen — sign-in, the choice of the learner, the start of work — and the section for adults | the device's | the adult's choice on this device, otherwise `navigator.languages`, otherwise English | `<html lang>` |
| the failure screens (section 3.7) | the device's, and the line for whoever deployed the copy is in English | the same | the line for the operator has `en` |
| the module's texts — the name, the statement, the chips, the steps, the techniques, the lesson, the pictures' `alt` | the module's explanation language | `languages.explanation` of the manifest (R46 spec) | on each block |
| the tutor's words | the module's explanation language | the same (concepts 10 spec) | on each block |
| the captions and the row of buttons of the canvas | the interface language | the attribute `lang` of the element, which `app` sets (R36 spec) | the element sets it |
| the statement, the workbook's lines and the parts of the example inside the canvas | the module's explanation language | the property `manifest` (R36 spec) | the element sets it |
| the names of anchor skills | English | R50 spec | `en` |
| legal texts and consent | English, and the line saying the text is in English, and the gist of the consent, in the screen's language | privacy 7 spec; R116 spec | `en` on the text |
| the numbers, sizes and dates of `app` | the screen's language | `Intl` | — |
| voicing (section 3.5) | the language of each text | as above | — |

**The learner's language.**
- The field "interface language" is in the learner's profile on the server (concepts 13 spec; privacy 4 spec). The adult sets it in the section for adults — for a new profile the default is the language the section is open in — and changes it there too. The learner has no switch on their screens: an accidental tap would take a child into a language they do not read.
- With no field, the device's language, and `app` does not write it back: the adult chooses the language.
- A tag this build does not know — a newer build or another copy wrote it — is looked up by truncation, below, then the device's language is taken, then English. `app` does not overwrite the field, and the section for adults says that this language is not in the build. `api` stores any valid BCP 47 tag, without a list of its own: otherwise a new interface language would wait for a release of `api` (R92 spec).
- When the adult changes the language, the other tabs of `app` learn of it through `BroadcastChannel`, as of the own key (section 3.6): the channel `novatrail-profiles`, which carries the profile from the response of `UpdateProfile` (R29).
- `app` creates a new profile with the language of the screen it is created on — so the contract requires: "On a new profile `app` sends the language of the section for adults". The owner profile is created so too (R29).
- The interface language does not go into an AI call: the tutor speaks in the module's explanation language (concepts 10 spec; R92 spec).

**The device's language** is for the screens where there is no learner yet or where an adult reads: sign-in, the choice of the learner, the start of work, the failure screens and the section for adults.
- The adult's choice on this device is, on the sign-in screen, the language's pill, `src/i18n/LanguagePill.tsx`: a pop-over named "Language: English", by the language shown, whose list is a button for each language; in the section for adults, the language buttons, `LanguageButtons` (R17). Each language is named by its own name — "English", «Русский» — and with its own `lang`. These are buttons, not a list `<select>`, which would change the screen on a choice (WCAG 3.2.2; section 3.9). A tap changes the screen's language at once, without a reload, and closes the pill's pop-over.
- Without a choice, the first language of `navigator.languages` that the lookup of RFC 4647, "Lookup", finds: the tag is truncated from the right, subtag by subtag — `ru-RU` → `ru` —, until a language of the build is found, and a single-letter subtag goes together with the next one, as in `canvas`. There is no "best match", as with the CLDR matcher: in MathTrail it gave Kazakh and Belarusian to Russian (sources 8.1). If none is found, English.
- The choice lies in the device settings — a small IndexedDB database of its own (section 3.2; R04).
