Signing out and the sign-in of another account do not erase it: the sign-in screen is seen before the account. "Erase everything on this device" and the browser itself erase it. The account has no language of its own.
- `app` draws the first screen once it has read the device settings: the database is small, and the language must not change under the finger. If the database did not open, `navigator.languages`. The screen "in a frame" (section 3.7) opens no storage and takes `navigator.languages`. The other tabs of `app` learn of a change of the device's language through `BroadcastChannel`.
- The built `index.html` has `<html lang="en">` and `<noscript>` in English and Russian, and the Russian paragraph has `lang="ru"`: before the script nobody knows the language (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R09).
- Google's sign-in window is in the screen's language: before the sign-in `app` sets `languageCode` of Firebase Auth, and it goes into the sign-in window if the provider can do that (sources 8.1). That it does come out so on devices is not checked: Д10 of [run/DEBT.md](../run/DEBT.md).

**Texts from `api`.** Legal texts and consent are pages of `app` (architecture 3.13 spec), and in a self-hosting copy they are the texts of its operator, which the copy's `api` serves (R115 spec). The texts are in English only, and the English text is the one in force: a text that is accepted has no translations that could diverge (privacy 7 spec; R10). On a screen in another language, above the text there is a line of `app` in the screen's language saying that the text is in English, and the text itself has `lang="en"`. The gist of the consent — who the operator is, which data and why, to whom they go, how to withdraw it — `app` shows briefly and in the screen's language beside the English text (R116 spec). `app` gets the texts by the method `LegalService.GetLegalTexts` — Connect GET, without a token: the policy is read before sign-in too. It names the screen's language by the field `interface_language`, not by the header `Accept-Language`: the language of the section for adults is not necessarily the browser's language. The body of the text comes in English, Markdown without HTML, pictures and links, and the gist in the request's language, if the texts' manifest carries it, otherwise in English. Its language is named by the field `summary_language`, and by it `app` sets its `lang` (R130 spec; [the contracts text](../spec/contracts/README.md), section 15). A version may have a translation into an interface language — the fields `translation` and `translation_language` —, and on a screen in that language `app` shows it below the line saying the English is in force, and before the English text, with its own `lang` and the mark "for reading only": the translation has no force of its own, and a consent names the version, not the translation (R141 spec; R10). The consent request names the version the person saw; a version that is no longer current gives `CAUSE_TEXT_NOT_CURRENT`, and `app` reads the texts anew and asks for consent again (R131 spec). **Code** (R27): `src/legal/texts.ts` is `GetLegalTexts` by the open transport past the HTTP cache, with the screen's language and with `unimplemented` of an older `api` as "no text in force", `body.ts` and `Markdown.tsx` are the body by the lexer of `marked` as React elements, without a string of HTML, and HTML, a link and a picture as their own text, `LegalText.tsx` is a text as a disclosure in place; the invite code screen shows `privacy-policy` and `terms-of-use` and asks for the tick, and `Session.createAccount` names their versions; the tick holds to the versions shown. The browser tests hold: the sign-in path with the texts in `en`, a screen in `ru` with the translation and the `lang` of each part, a failure to read and "Try again", `CAUSE_TEXT_NOT_CURRENT` with the tick cleared and the focus on it, and axe on a screen with the texts open.

**Two languages on a screen.**
- `<html lang>` is the screen's language: on the learner's screens, the language of their profile, on the others, the device's language (WCAG 3.1.1).
- Each block of the module's text and of the tutor's words has its own `lang`, the module's explanation language (3.1.2): "Screen readers can load the correct pronunciation rules" (sources 8.1). The name of a module, a lesson or a skill inside a string of `app` is a placeholder element with its own `lang`, not a piece of the string.
- `app` sets the canvas element's `lang` to the screen's language: the buttons' captions are in it, and the element itself sets the manifest's language on the statement, the workbook's lines and the parts of the example (R36 spec; R32 canvas). The number of a part of the example is in the element's language, and the part itself in the manifest's language (R42 canvas).
- The module's card on Home, on Subjects and in the section for adults names the explanation language if it is not the screen's language — by the name from `Intl.DisplayNames`, and in Russian with a lower-case letter, as a language's name stands inside a phrase: JavaScriptCore writes it with a capital, «Английский», and V8 and SpiderMonkey, «английский» (sources 8.2; measurements 2). The names of anchor skills are English (R50 spec), with `lang="en"`, and they are not translated: a translation would touch R50 spec and the format (R91 spec).
- Voicing is in the language of each text, in its own voice (section 3.5).

**Translations.**
- A dictionary per language is a TypeScript module: `src/i18n/en.ts` and `src/i18n/ru.ts`. The keys are dotted, the first word being the screen: `task.check`, `device.erase-all`. English is the source, with `as const`. A language's dictionary type is derived from it: a string key has a string with the same placeholders, which the type finds by the pattern `{name}`, and a plural form has an object of that language's categories, for Russian `one`, `few`, `many` and `other`. The Russian dictionary `satisfies` this type: `tsc` lets through no missing key, no extra key, no placeholder with another name and no missing category, so a pull request without a Russian string does not pass `ci-lint` (section 3.1). A note to the translator is a JSDoc comment on the key.
- Placeholders are by name, `{count}`, `{module}`; there is no concatenation of strings: languages have different word orders. A placeholder that needs its own `lang` is an element, not a string.
- Plural forms are an explicit marker with the categories of CLDR. English has two — `one` and `other` —, and Russian four — `one`, `few`, `many` and `other` —, and the integer 0 is `many`, while `other` is only fractions (sources 8.1, 8.2). `Intl.PluralRules` chooses the category. `other` is always there, and if the browser returned a category the dictionary does not have, `other` is taken.
- The numbers, sizes and dates that `app` writes are `Intl.NumberFormat` and `Intl.DateTimeFormat` with `[language, "en"]`, the names of languages are `Intl.DisplayNames`, the sorting of profiles and modules is `Intl.Collator`. `Intl` does not touch the module's numbers and what the learner might copy into an answer: in Russian it writes a decimal comma, and the format reads `1,5` as two numbers ([the format text](../spec/format/README.md), sections 15 and 20).
- Codes from the server — the verdict, the reason of "unreadable" (section 3.3), the choice's reason code, the outcome of the own key — `app` translates by a typed choice. The choice's reason code is the closed `ReasonCode`, nine values with `REASON_CODE_ROTATION`, "in rotation", without parameters: each has words for the adult in progress, at the attempt, in `en` and `ru`, and the code is not shown to the learner (R100, R107 spec). A code the build does not know gets a generic string, not an error: a newer `api` may send a new code (section 3.7). The words of the codes are `CHOICE_REASON_KEYS` in `src/adults/progress.ts`, and only there: no file of the learner's screens knows them or the field `reason_code`, and a test holds this (R35).
- The pseudo-language — English, made one and a half times longer and with diacritics — the tests build from the English dictionary themselves, and it does not go into the build.

**Rules of strings.**
- A string does not give away the learner's gender, nor do the tutor's words (R70 spec; concepts 10 spec). In Russian, without the past tense and short adjectives about the learner: «Здесь всё верно», not «Ты верно нашёл». The mode «сам», "self", is «Самостоятельно» on its button. In the section for adults the same is written about a pseudonym: «Урок пройден: Маша», not «Маша прошла урок».
- On the learner's screens, in Russian, the learner is not addressed: on the buttons and in the step's lines, the infinitive, «Проверить», «Отметить, что известно и что найти», and elsewhere impersonal phrases, «Нужна помощь взрослого». «Ты» to an adult learner and «вы» to a child are equally awkward, and the infinitive suits everyone. In English "you" does not give away gender and will do.
- In the section for adults, in Russian, «вы».
- The pseudonym and the names of a module, a lesson and a skill stand where they are not declined: «Профиль: Маша», not «профиль Маши».
- AI does not speak of itself in the first person: «Прочитано: 6», not «Я прочитал 6», as the examples of `spec` write — that is the masculine gender and a person where AI reads (R123 spec; R91 spec).
- The voicing button is "Read aloud", «Озвучить», not "listen", «прослушать»: concepts 8 spec keeps that word for an action of the text canvas.
- Strings are short: a child of 6 reads them too. They have no external links (privacy 1, 7 spec).
- The screen's words may differ from the terms of the vocabulary of concepts 15 spec: the documents keep the terms, and the screen keeps the words of the glossary, and one is not corrected by the other.

**Glossary** — the words that all screens take the same way. The code writes the full phrases by the rules above, and the author reads them in the pull request.

| the page "Own AI key" | Own AI key | «Свой ключ» | the section for adults (section 3.6) |

The canvas's captions — "Pen", "Eraser", "Hand", "Undo", "Clear", "Next", "Demo", and on a task from paper "Show the task", "Hide the task" and the grid's "Grid: dots", "Grid: lines", "Grid: blank" and "Grid: squares" —, the names of the regions "Sheet", "Workbook" and "Worked example" and of the rows "Sheet buttons", "Workbook buttons" and "Worked example buttons" — are held by the table of `canvas` (R36 spec; R33, R42, R88 canvas; R11; sources 2.4, 8.1). The strings of `app` that speak of them take the same words, from the table of words of architecture 6 spec: the words that more than one UI package shows are held by it, not by this glossary (R91 spec). A word of `app` that another package will need too goes there as a row «в реестр spec». "In order" is the screen's word for the mark that the documents call "sequential" (R58 spec).

**The name of a tutor's mark** is read by the screen reader, and `app` writes it in its own words (concepts 15 spec), in the screen's language: "Tutor's mark 1 of 2". On a workbook the line and the box are added to it: "Tutor's mark 1 of 2: line 3, box 2". On a handwriting sheet the place cannot be named in words: a group label is an internal letter of the device (concepts 15 spec), and a box in fractions of the crop has no words. The mark relates to the tutor's question, and the name leads to it by the number. The element will not accept an empty name (R26 canvas). From `api` come the mark's kind and its place, and the contract carries no reference to the tutor's phrase: the mark relates to the words of the reply that brought it (R97 spec).

**A path: a Russian interface over an English task.** A learner of the category `under_13`, the language in the profile `ru`. A module of olympiad maths, the explanation language `en`, the task `ladder.yaml` (concepts 16.1 spec; section 3.3).

| What is on the screen | Language | `lang` |
|---|---|---|
| the module's card on Home — the name and the line saying the module is in English | the name English, the line Russian | `en` on the name |
| the buttons «Проверить», «Подсказка», «По шагам», «Самостоятельно», «Шаг готов» | Russian | `ru` from `<html>` |
| the task statement — the canvas shows it above the sheet (R51 spec) | English | the element sets it by the manifest |
| the step "Understand the task" and the chips "The rungs are 30 cm apart." | English | `en` on the block |
| the step's line «Отметить, что известно и что найти» | Russian | `ru` |
| the canvas's captions «Перо», «Ластик», «Выше», «Ниже» | Russian | `lang="ru"` on the element |
| the verdict «Пока нет» | Russian | `ru` |
| the tutor's question | English | `en` on the block |
| the mark's name «Пометка наставника 1 из 1» | Russian | `ru` |

The screen reader reads each text with its pronunciation, and voicing reads the question in an English voice and «Пока нет» in a Russian one (section 3.5).

**Where the prototype diverges** ([the prototype's README](../spec/draft/README.md)): the prototype's section for adults has "Language of the lessons: Same as this app", while the module sets the language of the lessons, and format v0 has no translations (R46 spec). The documents take precedence.

**Tests.**
- In Node: the plural categories of each dictionary are those that `Intl.PluralRules(language).resolvedOptions().pluralCategories` gives, and `other` is always there; the lookup by truncation — `ru-RU` and `ru-Latn-RU` give `ru`, `en-GB` gives `en`, and `kk` and `be` give English; the profile's field with no value and with an unknown tag; the language of the texts from `api` is in the request; the generic string for an unknown code.
- In the browser — Chromium, WebKit and Firefox (section 3.1): `<html lang>` is the screen's language; the blocks of the module's text and of the tutor's words have the explanation language, through `:lang()`, as in `canvas`; the canvas element has the `lang` of the screen's language; the screens in the pseudo-language and in Russian at a width of 320 CSS px have no sideways scrolling and no clipped text (section 3.9).

**Code** (R17):
- the dictionaries are `src/i18n/en.ts` and `src/i18n/ru.ts`, the type is `src/i18n/types.ts`: `Translation` holds the keys and the language's plural categories, and `SamePlaces` the placeholders by the string literals, and a Russian dictionary without a string, with an extra key or with another placeholder name does not pass `tsc`. The categories are `CATEGORIES`, `en` has `one` and `other`, `ru` has `one`, `few`, `many` and `other`; a test checks them against `Intl.PluralRules`;
- the words are `src/i18n/words.ts`: `text` fills the placeholders by name and takes values only for a key that carries them, and `Intl.PluralRules` chooses the plural form, and an unknown category is `other`; the placeholder element is `src/i18n/Phrase.tsx`; `Intl` with `[language, "en"]` is `src/i18n/intl.ts`; the words of a code are `keyFor` in `src/i18n/codes.ts`, and an unknown code is `common.unknown`;
- the language is `src/i18n/language.ts`: the lookup by truncation, the device's language, and the learner's language — the profile's field, otherwise the device's language. The learner's screens — the profile's header and the screens under it — are in the learner's language: `src/i18n/LearnerLanguage.tsx` wraps them, and one place writes `<html lang>`, `src/i18n/page.ts` — the device's language from `Boot`, while the learner's screens do not hold their own (R29). The profile's language is set by the profile page of the section for adults, with the radio buttons "English" and «Русский», each with its own `lang`. `Boot` reads the choice from the device settings together with the request for `/deployment.json` and draws the first screen once it has read it, but waits no longer than a second; in a frame, only `navigator.languages`. The choice is the buttons of `src/i18n/LanguageButtons.tsx` in a `fieldset` with the legend "Language", each with the language's name in that language itself and with its own `lang`, the chosen one `aria-pressed`; the other tabs learn of the choice, if it was written, through one permanent `BroadcastChannel` `novatrail-settings` of the tab, and the chosen button is visible without color too — underlined. The buttons stand on the sign-in screen and in the section for adults, and Google's sign-in window is in the screen's language: `Boot` sets `languageCode` of Firebase Auth at each change of language (R19);
- the built `index.html` has `<noscript>` in English and Russian, the Russian paragraph with `lang="ru"`;
- the pseudo-language is `src/test/pseudo.ts`, not in the build: English with diacritics, one and a half times longer, with the placeholders as they are.

**Later.** Each new interface language — stage 5 (product 11 spec) — is a dictionary, a check of a voice for it (section 3.5) and the same language in the tables of the UI packages: each package carries each interface language (R91 spec). Right-to-left writing comes with the first such interface or module language: then `dir` will stand beside `lang`.

### 3.9. Accessibility

Decided by R11. Accessibility is when everyone can use the interface, including people with impairments of sight, hearing or motor skills; it is not the opening of a skill and not access to content (concepts 4, 15 spec). The learners of the slice are children from 6 and adults, on a tablet and a computer (product 3, 10 spec).

**The standard** is WCAG 2.2, W3C Recommendation 12 December 2024 (sources 8.1):
- level AA in full, but for the declared nonconformances below;
- beyond it, two AAA criteria: 2.5.5 Target Size (Enhanced) — touch targets no smaller than 44 × 44 CSS px, as in `canvas` (R36 spec) — and 2.3.3 Animation from Interactions — motion that an action starts is turned off by `prefers-reduced-motion`;
- AAA in full, no: W3C itself does not advise it. WCAG 3 is a Working Draft, and until it is a Recommendation, `app` does not look at it (sources 8.1).

WCAG 2.2 AA covers WCAG 2.1 AA, which the laws require where they name a level, but for 4.1.1 Parsing — in 2.2 it is "Obsolete and removed". The beta's markets are not chosen (П-2), so the standard is chosen by the learners, and the laws are below.

**Declared nonconformances** — the page "Accessibility" names them.

| What | Criteria | Why it does not meet them | What there is instead | When it goes away |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| an answer in handwriting — an answer slot `anywhere` and `sheet`, a workbook's box | 2.1.1 Keyboard, A; 2.5.1 Pointer Gestures, A; 2.5.7 Dragging Movements, AA | handwriting is an input technique, and the function is entering an answer: "the input technique (handwriting) requires path-dependent input but the underlying function (text input) does not" (sources 8.1) | the choice of an option `choice`, by tapping and from the keyboard; a draft drawing is under the exception of 2.1.1 anyway | with a second way of answering, О-25 |
| moving the sheet — the hand under any pointer, one finger in pen mode and two fingers always drag it, no button moves it — and a task from paper's tracing paper, dragged by its grip | 2.5.7 Dragging Movements, AA | the element's own scrolling, which 2.5.7 counts; `canvas` declares it (architecture 3.10 canvas; R84 canvas, R87 canvas, R88 canvas) | the arrow keys on the grip; the wheel, the trackpad and the arrow keys in the sheet's region, across and down; the page's own scrolling of an element without a height | no date: only by a change of R90 spec |
| the print of the workbook's lines — the numbers, the pieces of text and the demo's values in the boxes — does not take the user's text spacing | 1.4.12 Text Spacing, AA | `all: initial` takes the inherited spacing off it, and each piece is fitted to its own width of the layout: the place of a piece and of a box is set by the page rule of `spec`, `format/page.yaml`, not by the device's font (architecture 3.10 canvas; R74 canvas) | the statement above the workbook and the rest of `app` take the spacing; «Крупнее» on the canvas's bar enlarges the print to twice the page's fit (R73 canvas) | no date (R11): the spacing only if the page rule of `spec` gives the lines room for it (В77 `canvas`) |
| sending an answer by "Check" — without confirmation and without undo | 3.3.4 Error Prevention, AA | an answer is "user test responses": it gets a verdict at once and goes into evidence, and 3.3.4 requires reversal, checking or confirmation; a confirmation before each check would get in a child's way | a wrong answer does not end the attempt: after "Not yet" the learner corrects the work and checks again, and an unreadable work is returned by "unreadable" (R56 spec) | with a second way of answering, if it allows correcting an answer before the verdict: the model always counts a submission whose verdict the learner saw, and only one undone before the verdict may go uncounted (О-25; R11) |
| a frame drawn by hand round a task the AI missed on the photo of a page | 2.1.1 Keyboard, A | a frame is a place on the photo, given by a drag or by two taps; two taps need no path, so 2.5.1 and 2.5.7 hold (R71) | the AI's frames are picked and left out from the keyboard, and Retake takes a new photo | no date: a frame from the keyboard would need a way to point at a place on the photo |
| solving a task from paper — on the sheet and on its tracing paper | 2.1.1 Keyboard, A | a task from paper has no answer slot and no options: its solution is handwriting alone (R162 spec; R71) | moving and hiding the task, the list of the tasks, Hint and Check from the keyboard | no date: with a second way of writing a solution, which the platform has not chosen |

The bar of the canvas has no button that moves the sheet: the sheet is dragged, and `canvas` declares 2.5.7, which the page names as moving the sheet; «Крупнее» and «Мельче» zoom the sheet from 50 % to 200 %, which meets 1.4.4, and `canvas` declares 1.4.12 of the workbook's print, which the page names as a place (R73 canvas, R74 canvas, R84 canvas; R11). 3.1.2, which the canvas declares for an element without the attribute `lang`, never happens with `app`, which always sets it (R11). What is declared and what is not is checked on the element itself, `src/canvas/canvas.browser.test.tsx`. The bar sticks in its rail and scrolls inside its glass in a low window (1.4.10, 2.4.11).

The page "Accessibility", `#/legal/accessibility`, is among the legal pages of `app`, `#/legal`, visible before sign-in too (architecture 3.13 spec; R31; section 3.10). On it are the standard; the six places where `app` does not meet it, by the table above, each with the criteria, why, what there is instead and when this changes; how this is checked — CI, and no checks by people yet (Д10); and whom to contact. The standard, these nonconformances and what replaces them are properties of the build, the same in all copies, and their text is in the dictionaries of `app`, in the screen's language, by the rules of section 3.8; the author proofreads it. Whom to contact is the copy's operator: `api` names the operator (R115 spec), not the build. The EAA expects such information "in the general terms and conditions, or equivalent document" (sources 8.1). The contract has no field for the operator's contact: the operator's name and contacts are the section Who we are of their privacy policy, which the copy's `api` serves and the rule `legal.operator` holds in every version of it (R115, R131 spec; the 2026-11-12 amendment to R115 spec). The page leads by the button "Who runs this copy and how to reach them" to the page `#/legal/operator`, which shows that section (R46; section 3.10), with a request not to write a child's personal data. The test `src/legal/LegalPages.browser.test.tsx` checks the set against R11: 2.1.1, 2.5.1, 2.5.7 — for handwriting and for moving the sheet —, 1.4.4 and 1.4.12 for the print, 3.3.4, and 2.1.1 for a frame drawn by hand and for solving a task from paper are there, and 3.1.2 is not.

**The theme** is R17, `src/theme/theme.css`: the roles and colours of the mockup's README, «The style», with the replacements of its «Contrast» (R148 spec), each role in a comment. `src/theme/contrast.node.test.ts` holds the pairs of «Contrast» — text 4.5:1, an edge, the focus ring and a mark 3:1, a translucent colour over what it stands on — and fails on a colour token no pair names:

| Tokens `--nt-…` | Colours |
|---|---|
| `page`, `page-owner`, `card` | `#f2f2f7`, `#f5f5f7`, `#ffffff` |
| `text`, `text-muted`, `text-muted-on-grey`, `segmented` | `#1d1d1f`, `#6e6e73`, `#636366`, `#3a3a3c` |
| `accent`, `accent-text`, `accent-tint`, `on-fill` | `#0071e3`, `#0066cc`, its tint 0.1, `#ffffff` |
| `correct`, `not-yet`, `error` | `#1e7a35` for `#248a3d`; `#c93400` twice |
| `hint`, `hint-text`, `next`, `tutor-words` | `rgba(255,204,0,0.2)`, `#9a5b00`, `#a85a00`, `#23308c` |
| `grey-08`…`grey-16`, `disabled`, `edge` | `rgba(118,118,128,…)`; `#8e8e93`, exempt; `#8e8e93`, 3.26:1 to a field's white fill |
| `state-mastered`, `-review`, `-progress`, `-ready`, `-locked` | `#30a14e`, `#c77700`, `#0a84ff`, `#3d8be0`, `#8e8e93` |
| `tooltip`; `glass`, `glass-panel` | `#1d1d1f`, opaque (В86 canvas); white 0.76 and 0.86, alone over the page |
| `sky`, `sky-glow`, `sky-pill`; `sky-card`, `sky-fill`, `sky-note` | the way in's sky, its brightest glow, the language pill's glass; a step's card, a field's fill and the note's on it (R61) |
| `sky-muted`, `sky-note-text`, `sky-caption`, `sky-quiet`, `sky-edge`, `sky-link`, `sky-error`, `sky-correct`; `sky-off`, `sky-off-fill` | on the card: the mockup's translucent whites made opaque, its link, error and tick; a disabled button, exempt |
| `who-lead`, `who-line`, `who-start`, `who-glass`, `who-why`; `who-off`; `who-under-…` | "Who is learning?" on the sky: white 0.7, 0.6, `#2b66f6`, the glass white 0.12 of "Sign out" and "Add a learner", white 0.9; "Add a learner" closed, exempt; the sky where brightest under "Sign out", the line, the glass and the focus ring, which no rule paints (R61) |
| `own-link`, `own-rows`, `own-value`, `own-hint`, `own-edge`, `own-more`; `own-ring` | the owner's profile on the sky: `#8fb8ff`, the rows white 0.07, white 0.85, 0.55, the field's edge 0.5, the chevron 0.4; the rows' ring 0.1, exempt (R61) |
| `google-edge`, `google-text` | Google's light theme on white, `on-fill` (sources 4.8 spec) |
| `paper-from`, `paper-mid`, `paper-to` | `#b85000`, `#c8331a`, `#c81e46` for the gradient `#ff9f0a` → `#ff5e3a` → `#ff375f` of the card of a task from paper, on which white misses 4.5:1; white holds 5.02:1 at the least (R71) |

Besides: radii, sizes — a target 44, `.nt-main` 52, type 14 for the body —, weights — 600, a heading 700 as the template draws it (В71) — and motion, 0.15 s on `cubic-bezier(.2,.8,.2,1)`. A button is the mockup's pill, its border transparent for forced colours. The canvas takes `-ink`, `-paper`, `-mark` — the tutor's words —, `-accent` and `-glass` for its bar, and `-top: 0px`, for `app` has no sticky header and the mockup centres the bar in the whole screen (R17); the skill map `-paper`, `-ink` and `-accent`. The font is the system one (R11). Inside a step's card of the way in, `.nt-welcome .nt-column`, the generic tokens — the text, the muted text, the accent's text, the error, the card, a field's fill and edge, a disabled button — are the sky's by `var()`, so each step keeps its screen's own rules, and the test pairs the sky's tokens on the card.

**The layout from the width** — R59, by the mockup's README, «Two sizes» and «Pop-overs, not panels» (R148 spec), from the width alone, not the kind of device; a media query reads no custom property, so each writes `600px`:
- below 600 CSS px the phone's layout, one column of the whole width, padded 16; from 600 the tablet's, a phone in landscape and a computer too. `Screen`'s `layout` is then `column`, 40rem, by default; `reference`, the frame of R79, 1120 at most, centred on the page's colour — Home and Subjects —, and `map` in it, the skill map's; or `canvas`, the whole window — the task screen and the lesson; `welcome`, the sky and a step's card, and `profiles`, the way in's (R61; section 3.10); `adults`, a column of 40rem under the top bar, the section for adults' (R68); `sheet`, the mockup's legal text, a card of 560, below 600 the whole screen, and `failure`, a card of 460 in the window's middle (R69). A bar, `.nt-bar`, keeps the width of the screen under it, by `:has()`;
- the catalog's cards: one column, 12 apart; from 600 two in a row at any width, 16 apart; Home's modules a column (R59; R79);
- the tutor's panel floats over the sheet at the window's right from 600, and below 600 is a sheet from the bottom (R67, section 3.3); the lesson's segments are a pop-over (R64);
- `src/app/PopOver.tsx`, a button and a modal `<dialog>` named by the button's words: from 600 it hangs from the button where `hang.ts` puts it — below it, or above where only above has room, or on the side of more room, scrolling inside; at its left edge, or its right where the left would leave the window, or centred (R61); 12 from it and 16 inside the window —, the panel's glass, radius 20, growing from the corner nearest the button in 0.2 s; below 600 a sheet from the bottom, white, radius 28 above, the page dimmed by 0.22, with ×, "Close", rising in 0.3 s. Escape, a tap outside — a press and a release outside its box, not a drag from inside — and a choice, by the `close` its content is given, close it; the focus goes to the first target inside and back to the button, which Safari does not focus on a click. The screens move their lists and choices into it in their tasks;
- the checks: `src/theme/layout.browser.test.tsx` at 320 — a zoom of 400 % of 1280 —, 390, 599, 600, 834, 1087, 1088, 1194 and 1600 px, and `src/app/PopOver.browser.test.tsx` at 600, 834 and 1194 and at 390, 599 and 320, with axe-core, the targets and no sideways scroll, in the three engines; `hang.node.test.ts` in Node.

**Buttons without a caption** — R60, the closed list of R90 spec, a task from paper's in R60 itself: `src/app/IconButton.tsx`, the icon alone, 44 × 44 — the grabber 88 × 44 —, named by its caption, `aria-label`, with R60's tooltip of the same words, `aria-hidden`, in the top layer (`tip.ts`; 1.4.13). Every other button shows its caption: `captionlessOffList` of `src/test/a11y.ts` fails a screen's button with no text and a name off the list, but for a frame over the photo of a page, which shows the task it frames, its tick or its number in its corner (R71). The icons, `scripts/icons.ts`:

| Button | Caption | Icon; filled |
|---|---|---|
| back; close | Back; Close | `arrow-back-ios-new`, mirrored; `close` |
| ‹ › | Previous task, Next task | `chevron-left`, `chevron-right`, mirrored |
| the speaker at a text; the switch of voicing | Read aloud; Voicing | `volume-up`; filled while on |
| Help; the tutor's button | Help; Tutor | `help`, filled while open; `sms` |
| the grabber of the phone's sheet | Full screen, Half screen | none: a bar of 36 × 5 |
| the notifications of the frame (R79) | Notifications | `notifications`, filled |
| a card's skill map; a request's microphone and Send (R74; R79) | Skill map; Voice comes later, Send | `account-tree`; `mic`, `arrow-upward` |

The frame's places from 600 to 72em show their icon alone, their words their name and tooltip, `src/app/Tooltip.tsx` (R60; R79). `INSTEAD` holds the captions of a back or close button that name where it goes: "Back to Home" of the task screen and of a task from paper, "Leave the lesson" (R64), "Back to the section" and "Close the section" of the section for adults' top bar (R68), and "Back to the list" of a sheet (R69).

**How `app` holds the criteria** that matter most to this application:

| Criterion | What `app` does |
|---|---|
| 1.1.1 Non-text Content, A | a module's picture has its `alt`, and an empty `alt` only a decoration ([the format text](../spec/format/README.md), section 14); an icon is `aria-hidden`, its button named by the caption |
| 1.3.1 Info and Relationships, A; 4.1.2 Name, Role, Value, A | buttons, lists and headings are HTML elements; the strips of steps and of segments are lists with `aria-current`, and a segment that does not open is `aria-disabled` with a line saying why (sections 3.3 and 3.4); `app` gives the canvas element's name to the element itself (architecture 3.3 canvas) |
| 1.3.4 Orientation, AA | the orientation is not locked |
| 1.4.1 Use of Color, A | the verdict of an answer slot, a step and a segment passed are also shown by a word or a sign, not only by color |
| 1.4.3 Contrast (Minimum), AA; 1.4.11 Non-text Contrast, AA | text no less than 4.5:1, borders, the focus ring and marks 3:1; the theme is light |
| 1.4.4 Resize Text, AA; 1.4.10 Reflow, AA; 1.4.12 Text Spacing, AA | sizes are in `rem`; zoom is not forbidden — neither `maximum-scale` nor `user-scalable=no`; at a width of 320 CSS px, no sideways scrolling, and the canvas's sheet is a two-dimensional layout, which 1.4.10 excepts; the print of the workbook's lines is the nonconformance 1.4.4 and 1.4.12 above |
| 2.1.1 Keyboard, A; 2.1.2 No Keyboard Trap, A | everything but handwriting is from the keyboard; the canvas's row follows the APG (R36 spec) |
| 2.2.1 Timing Adjustable, A | there is one time limit — the closing of the owner profile, and the adult sets its term (section 3.6); the learner has no timers (product 7 spec; section 3.3) |
| 2.2.2 Pause, Stop, Hide, A; 2.3.1 Three Flashes or Below Threshold, A; 2.3.3 Animation from Interactions, AAA | the flashing of "Check" is one per pause, shorter than 5 s, no more often than 3 times a second, and with `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` a still highlight (section 3.3); the transitions of screens with it are without motion |
| 2.4.2 Page Titled, A; 2.4.3 Focus Order, A | each screen has its own document title in the screen's language; on a change of screen the focus is on its heading `h1`, on a change of step on the step's name, on a change of the lesson's segment on the segment's heading `h2`; while the learner writes, the focus does not move |
| 2.4.7 Focus Visible, AA; 2.4.11 Focus Not Obscured (Minimum), AA | the focus ring is visible; a sticky header would set `--novatrail-canvas-top` (R28, R84 canvas) — `app` has none, and sets 0 —, and the sticky bottom bar `scroll-padding`, and they do not cover the focus |
| 2.5.3 Label in Name, A | the caption on a button of `app` is visible, and the accessible name is its text, but for the closed list above, which has no visible label, and the profile's pill, named "{pseudonym}, profile menu", its words in its name (R63), and the lesson's line, "{line}: segments of the lesson" (R64); an icon is beside the caption; a row of the lesson's segments is named by its words — the place, out of sight beside a tick, the kind and how far it is (R23, R64) |
| 2.5.5 Target Size (Enhanced), AAA | each target of `app` is no smaller than 44 × 44 CSS px |
| 3.1.1 Language of Page, A; 3.1.2 Language of Parts, AA | section 3.8 |
| 3.2.2 On Input, A | the language and the settings are changed by a button, not by a choice in a list that would change the screen itself |
| 3.3.1 Error Identification, A; 3.3.3 Error Suggestion, AA | an error of the PIN and of the own key is in words at the field, tied to it by `aria-describedby`; the PIN names how many tries are left and when input opens — by the response `PinCheck` and the refusal `CAUSE_PIN_LOCKED` (R98 spec; section 3.6) |
| 3.3.4 Error Prevention (Legal, Financial, Data), AA | "Erase everything on this device", erasing a profile, and "Remove key" are confirmed; sending an answer — "user test responses" — is the nonconformance above |
| 3.3.7 Redundant Entry, A | the own key is entered again when the owner profile has closed — this is the exception "required to ensure the security of the content" |
| 3.3.8 Accessible Authentication (Minimum), AA | sign-in is on Google's pages; the PIN field accepts pasting, the password manager is not blocked, and there are no `autocomplete` hints (section 3.6) |
| 4.1.3 Status Messages, AA | `role="status"` is on the verdict, "The tutor's words came", a "Continue" that has appeared and "Your work isn't saved on this device"; the tutor's full text does not go into the live region. The line "no voice" by the button "Read aloud" is not a status but its description, `aria-describedby`: it stands from the start rather than appearing, and a status at each button would be read on each screen (R36) |

**Beyond WCAG, for children.**
- The caption is visible, but for the closed list, and the icon beside it helps whoever does not read well yet (R78 spec).
- Voicing of the tutor's words and the button "Read aloud" (section 3.5): for the youngest, voicing is on by default.
- There are no timers and no competitions (product 7 spec), and the flashing at a pause is one.
- Voicing and a screen reader would speak at once, and `app` does not know whether a screen reader is running. So the tutor's words do not go into the live region whole, and the section for adults advises whoever listens to a screen reader to turn voicing off.

**UI packages.** The standard of R11 is the standard of all UI packages, `app`, `canvas` and `skill-map`: by R90 spec (О-24). What a package does not meet yet, it declares itself, and `app` names it on the page "Accessibility". In every package the caption beside an icon is visible (the second 2026-11-02 amendment to R78 spec). What follows from the standard for each (R90 spec):
- `canvas` — a sheet past the page that scrolls in two directions, which 1.4.10 allows it, the page fitted to the width and zoomed by the bar to 200 %, which meets 1.4.4 (R73 canvas, R74 canvas); the lines and boxes of the workbook in the accessibility tree; the keyboard into the sheet and out of it; the bar of buttons sticks in its rail and scrolls inside its glass when the window is low; the frames of `exportWork()` do not change with the theme and forced colors; its own axe-core;
- `skill-map` — a text alternative to the graph, `lang` on the name of each skill, mastery not by color alone, the keyboard and moving without dragging;
- WebKit bug 277290: in Safari a scroll container without focusable content cannot be scrolled from the keyboard (О-24). `app` scrolls with the page, and gives a scroll container of its own only with `tabindex="0"`, the role `region` and a name.

**What CI checks** — the recipes `ci-lint`, `ci-test` and `ci-engines` (section 3.1):
- Biome — the rules of the group `a11y`, by default: of 38, 37 are on (sources 8.1);
- the screens' tests find elements by role and name, `getByRole`, so a button without a name fails the test, and `toHaveAccessibleName` checks the name;
- checks of its own in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox (R45): each target of `app` is no smaller than 44 × 44 CSS px, at a width of 320 px too; `lang` on the blocks of the module's text and of the tutor's words — through `:lang()`; the screens in the pseudo-language and in Russian at a width of 320 px — without sideways scrolling and without clipped text; Vitest projects with `forcedColors: "active"`, as at `canvas`, in Chromium and Firefox, and with `reducedMotion: "reduce"`, where there is no blinking;
- the contrast of the theme's colors — in Node, by the WCAG formula: text — no less than 4.5:1, borders, the focus ring and marks — 3:1;
- axe-core, at the version of `package.json`, on every screen, except the subtree of the canvas element: `canvas` checks it in its own CI, and in `app` a violation of the package would fail others' pull requests. axe-core is under MPL-2.0, as a named exception `dev` (section 3.1). In Deque's words, it finds "on average 57% of WCAG issues" (sources 8.1).

**What a person checks**: "Tools cannot check all accessibility aspects automatically" (sources 8.1). Before the first child and with each new screen — Д10:

| Device | With what | What |
|---|---|---|
| iPad, Safari and the app from the "Home Screen" | VoiceOver, Switch Control, Voice Control | the task screen and the lesson, the choice of the learner, the PIN; voicing together with VoiceOver |
| iPad, Safari | page zoom, "Smart Invert" | nothing is clipped, marks are visible |
| Android, Chrome | TalkBack | the same as with VoiceOver |
| Windows, Chrome and Firefox | NVDA, Narrator; forced colors | names, focus order, the language of parts; borders and buttons in forced colors |
| Mac, Safari | VoiceOver; keyboard only | the whole path without a mouse |
| a computer, any browser | zoom 200%, width 320 CSS px, text spacing 1.4.12 | nothing is lost but the workbook's print, which is declared |

**Code** (R17):
- the theme is `src/theme/theme.css`, and `src/boot/Boot.tsx` includes it: sizes in `rem`, a target no smaller than 2.75rem — a `summary` too —, the focus ring an `outline` by `:focus-visible`, long words wrapped by `overflow-wrap: anywhere`, every transition and animation only under `prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference`, and `forced-color-adjust: none` nowhere. The contrast is `src/theme/contrast.ts`, and a test in Node reads the tokens from `theme.css` itself and checks each pair; `theme.browser.test.tsx` holds the computed styles, and `axeViolations` waits for transitions to end;
- the screen is `src/app/Screen.tsx`: `<main lang>`, `<h1 tabIndex={-1}>`, the document title "{screen} — NovaTrail" in the screen's language — on the home screen just "NovaTrail" — and focus on `h1`, with no ring, when one screen has replaced another, — not on the first screen of the page, under StrictMode too, and not while the screen stays the same;
- the checks are `src/test/a11y.ts`: `axe-core` by the rules with the tags `wcag2a`, `wcag2aa`, `wcag21a`, `wcag21aa` and `wcag22aa` — the standard of R11, without best-practice, — outside the subtree of `novatrail-canvas`; targets no smaller than 44 × 44 CSS px, but for a frame over the photo of a page, whose place and size are those of the task it frames (2.5.5's exception Essential; R71); the page without sideways scrolling, and at each element content no wider than the element itself. `src/a11y/screens.browser.test.tsx` runs them on each screen — "in a frame", "no connection", "not set up", "erased", "can't be read", the sign-in with the language's pill, the home screen with the offer to reload — in `en`, `ru` and the pseudo-language, at a width of 1024 and 320 CSS px, and checks the name of each button against its caption. The projects `forced colors` and `reduced motion` are files of their own in `src/a11y/`;
- `lang` on the blocks of the module's text — on the module's name, the step, the chips, the techniques, the items, the options and the solution, — and the task screens "by steps", "On my own", after "Not yet" and solved are in `screens.browser.test.tsx`; there too the lesson at an explanation, a worked example, a task and a task not begun, the lesson end screen and "A lesson in progress" (R23). The blinking of "Check": the project `browser` keeps the animation — three flashes of 1 s each, — and the project `reduced motion` a still outline without animation (R20).
- what the canvas declares for the page "Accessibility" — on the element itself, in `src/canvas/canvas.browser.test.tsx`: «Крупнее» on the bar grows the print of a workbook to twice the page's fit and «Мельче» takes it back, and the print keeps its share of the page, which fits the element's width at 20rem and at twice it, in the area «Тетрадь», which takes the focus and scrolls across (1.4.4 — met); the bar of a sheet and of a workbook has the hand and no "Up", "Down", "Left" or "Right" (2.5.7 — a nonconformance); under the text spacing of 1.4.12 on the page and on the element, the statement takes it, and the widths of the pieces of print stay the same (1.4.12 — a nonconformance); the area «Лист» and "Sheet" are in the attribute's language, and carry it as a `lang` of their own, which Firefox matches too, though it gives a shadow tree no language of its host (measurements 4; R15) — `app` has no 3.1.2. A canvas that changes any of this will fail the test, and the page's list is read anew (R11).

**The comparison with the interface mockup** (R58), `just mockup-compare`, in CI the job Mockup; what it measured is measurements 9:
- the environment: the mockup's own server, `spec/tools/mockup/serve.mjs` — or that of the spec folder the recipe names, `NOVATRAIL_MOCKUP_SPEC` —, on a free port of 127.0.0.1, its page at a state and a device, the three engines of the stage `toolchain`, each with `FONTCONFIG_FILE` at `mockup/fonts.conf`, which draws `system-ui` and `sans-serif` by FreeSans;
- `mockup/engines.node.test.ts` — every state of the first slice on the iPad and the phone in the three engines at once: `data-ready="true"`, no error, and the same shapes (`readShapes`, `disagreements`), each engine's snapshot of `#frame` in `mockup/out/`;
- `mockup/screens.browser.test.tsx` — each compared screen of `app`, drawn as in the screen tests, at each size, against its state by its regions; the command `mockupCompare` of `mockup/compare.ts` reads both sides in Node (`readRegion`, `readElement`, `compareRegion` of `scripts/mockup.ts`) and writes `<state>.<device>.<engine>.mockup.png`, `.app.png` and `.json`, the differences; the regions and their `Find` are `mockup/regions.ts`. A screen with `until` is reported, not held;
- a screen's task adds its states to `SCREENS` with their regions, each region's exceptions — `followsText`, `contrast`, `differs`, of properties or of the box's `x`, `y`, `width` and `height` — by its row of the mockup's README or a decision, `devices` for one size, and drops `until` from the screens it brings to the mockup;
- the canvas's bar, in `task-pill`, `task-empty`, `task` and `example` (the 2026-11-17 amendment to R58); the home screen, `home` and `home-legend` (R62; the second 2026-11-17 amendment to R58); the lesson, `lesson`, `lesson-segments`, `example` and `example-demo` on each size and `lesson-end` (R64).

There are no forced colors on iPad and Mac: WebKit always answers `forced-colors: none` (sources 8.1; measurements 2). So they are checked on Windows.

**Laws** — what was read (sources 8.1). The beta's markets are not chosen, and what of this concerns NovaTrail is the question П-25 in the register of `spec`. The platform has no lawyer: legal questions are decided by the author on Claude's analysis (R112 spec).

| Where | Law | What it requires | What the primary source did not show |
|---|---|---|---|
| EU | European Accessibility Act, Directive 2019/882, — from 28 June 2025 | accessibility of e-commerce services — "the online sale of any product or service" — and information about it; microenterprises are exempt. Learning is not among the services | the countries' laws transposing it; whether the standard EN 301 549 V4.1.1, which equates itself with WCAG 2.2 AA, is named in the Official Journal — as of 7 September 2026, V3.2.1, WCAG 2.1 AA, was in force |
| USA | ADA, Title II, the 2024 rule | WCAG 2.1 AA for the websites and apps of state and local government bodies, including through contracts, — from 26 April 2027 and 2028 | — |
| USA | ADA, Title III; Section 508 | for Title III the DOJ has no technical standard; Section 508 — WCAG 2.0 AA for federal agencies | — |
| UK | Equality Act 2010 | reasonable adjustments by the service provider, in advance; for the public sector gov.uk requires WCAG 2.2 AA | — |
| Russia | the federal law «181-ФЗ»; the standard «ГОСТ Р 52872-2019» | on private websites «181-ФЗ» is silent; the GOST follows WCAG 2.1 and is voluntary; resolution No. 102 of 7 February 2026 is about the websites of state bodies | whether the GOST is in force now: docs.cntd.ru did not answer |

A public school in the USA that takes `app` in self-hosting or by contract answers for WCAG 2.1 AA itself, and the declared nonconformances will not cover that for it (П-25).

**Later.** The second way to answer is О-25; the dark theme — by `prefers-color-scheme`, once the same test checks its contrast; a font for children is a question of its own: fonts with Cyrillic are almost all under OFL-1.1, and it is not in the list of licenses (R12 spec).

### 3.10. What else and in what order

What the card assigns to `app` outside "To decide when detailed" (architecture 3.1 spec), the questions of `spec` about the screens of `app`, the screens built outside sections 3.1–3.9, and what is open, by epic; the order of the epics is [RUN.md](../RUN.md) and [run/EPICS.md](../run/EPICS.md). R05, R06, R08, R09 and R11 leave the layout of screens here — the screen after the check, the conversation line, where an essay is reread, the PIN of the section for adults, the choice of the learner, the legal texts and the place of the page "Accessibility". The conversation line of the first slice is buttons of requests, without the learner's own text (architecture 3.1 spec). The epics, each with its archive once done; what a done one left open is rows of [run/QUESTIONS.md](../run/QUESTIONS.md) and [run/DEBT.md](../run/DEBT.md):
- **E2 — the first app** — [plan/E2.md](plan/E2.md);
- **E3 — the first slice with adult learners** — [plan/E3.md](plan/E3.md);
- **E5 — the interface by the mockup** — [plan/E5.md](plan/E5.md): the look of `app` by the mockup of `draft/mockup/` (R148 spec), each difference named by its decision and its region of the comparison (R58);
- **E4 — before the first child:** children's profiles, consent, export and deletion, and their screens by the mockup (repositories 6 spec; R116, R119, R121 spec) — its row in [run/EPICS.md](../run/EPICS.md).

**What the card assigns to `app` outside "To decide when detailed".**

| What | What holds | What is open |
|---|---|---|
| the account owner's sign-in | by the Firebase Auth window (R09; R19; R03 api; section 3.7) | — |
| the start of work — the learner's age and country | the owner states them, and the platform keeps the age category, the country and the date when the age was stated (privacy 3.1, 3.3 spec; R06 spec); that the owner is an adult is their statement "I am 18" with a date (R118 spec); learners from the EU, the EEA and the UK `api` does not accept yet, with the refusal `CAUSE_COUNTRY_NOT_ACCEPTED` (R98, R131 spec); the country is one of the codes `Country` of the part `consent-age/` (R134 spec); the invite code with the statement, the texts `privacy-policy` and `terms-of-use` and their acceptance, and the owner's profile — below, "The way in" (R19; R27; R61) | children's profiles — E4 |
| the parent's consent | a signed form whose scan the operator checks, and before the public launch also a card transaction (R116 spec); the record of consent on the server (privacy 4 spec); a child's profile in two steps, `RequestChildConsent` and, after the consent, `CreateChildProfile` (R98 spec); the contract of consent — the request code on the form, `SendConsentForm` with the scan and its states, a repeat after "not accepted", `CancelChildConsent`, the version of `parent-consent` in `RequestChildConsent` and `RestateAge`, and from the country's age of digital consent the teen's own consent `GiveOwnConsent` by `teen-consent` and its withdrawal `WithdrawOwnConsent`, with an export before it; to the child on their screen `child-notice`, to the teen `teen-notice` (R132, R136, R141 spec); the age anew — `RestateAge` on `CAUSE_AGE_NEEDED`, on `age_check_due` and on a change of country (R134 spec); for learners from the USA, a separate screen of the teen's consent to optional processing, where refusal is the most prominent, without asking again for a year, a notice at 18 and the device's signals as a refusal (R140 spec; privacy 3.4 spec); the gist of a consent in the screen's language beside the English text (section 3.8); the words of `CAUSE_TOO_MANY_CONSENTS` (R26) | the screens — E4 |
| the learner's grade | the module whose manifest names grades asks it; the answer may be outside the list and closes nothing (R49 spec) | the question screen — with the first such module, as `modules` decides whether the manifests of the first batch name grades |
| the choice of the learner on a shared device | the account's profiles come from `api` after sign-in, the chosen one only in memory (section 3.2); the owner profile with a lock (section 3.6); "Who is learning?" — below, "The way in" (R19; R61) | the choice among children's profiles and the lock of the owner profile — E4 |
| the PIN of the section for adults | 4–6 digits, the hash at `api`, no more than 5 failed tries in 15 minutes (R04 api); the field and the error, and the answer to a wrong PIN is `PinCheck` (section 3.6; R98 spec) | the screens: the PIN is set, changed and reset by `SetPin`, `ChangePin` and `ResetPin` — the last by a fresh sign-in (R98 spec; R04 api) — E4 |
| progress and the skill map | by the package `skill-map`, by ready data from `api` — `GetSkillMap` and `GetProgress` of `ProgressService` (R99 spec), — both on the learner's screen and in the section for adults (architecture 3.3 spec): below, "Skill map" (R34) and "Progress in the section for adults" (R35), with a module's level (R56; R157 spec); the reason code of the engine choice — `ReasonCode` — only the adult sees (concepts 12 spec; R100, R107 spec; section 3.8); a skill chosen on the skill map holds until the learner asks for something else (R107 spec); for an anchor skill at "unknown", the modules that teach it — `teaching_modules` of `AnchorSkillState` (the 2026-11-11 amendment to R99 spec) | the line telling the child on their screen that the adult sees their progress ([DPIA](../spec/docs/dpia.md), section 6, risk 16) — E4 |
| three entries | Home, the skill map and "A task from my paper" (product 4 spec; architecture 3.1 spec): below, Home and Subjects (R79), the skill map (R34) and "A task from my paper" (R71) | the words after a correct answer of a task from paper (R162 spec) |
| the task screen | the canvas, answer slots, the choice of an option, the panel of steps and the hint (sections 3.3 and 3.4); the text of an item of a sheet with the options of `choice` is beside the sheet, and the statement is the canvas above the sheet (architecture 3.1 spec; R51 spec); the layout (R20; R65); the entry is the open attempt from `GetAccount` and "Try it again"; handwriting (R21), the hint and showing the solution (R22), a task of a lesson under its header (R23), a new task by the engine choice (R24), the whole sheet — the binding `sheet` — on a screen of its own, `EssayScreen.tsx` (R53) — section 3.3, "Code", and section 3.4, "Code" | — |
| after the check | the verdict of a slot and of an item, the reasons `blank` and `notation` (R56 spec) and `illegible`, `ambiguous`, `no_final_answer` and `several_final_answers` (R86 spec; section 3.3); what was read is "Read: …" (section 3.8); "unchecked" on a withdrawn version (R63 spec); feedback by the rubric on an essay and "Done", which closes its attempt (R75, R87 spec); from the fourth wrong answer, "call an adult" or "show the solution" (R82 spec; section 3.3); the screen — section 3.3, "Code" (R20; R21; R65) | — |
| the conversation line | in the card (architecture 3.1 spec): in the first slice the buttons of requests "Hint" and "Explain differently", without the learner's own text (product 10 spec; R22); "explain differently" is one AI call per rung, without the work, with the task's past questions, and in the mode without AI it is not there (R82 spec) | the learner's own text — a capability of `tutor` of its own, later |
| the disclosure that the learner talks with AI | to all learners; for organizations that serve minors, Anthropic requires it (privacy 5.3 spec); "Report a problem" for the adult at each reply of the tutor — `AttemptService.ReportProblem` —, and learning materials on safe use (R123 spec): below, "Tutor — AI" (R33) | the texts of the materials by age and the button at a child's profile — E4, by П-26 |
| help for a learner under 18 and the reminder not to write names | to a learner under 18, at a work with a rubric and in the section "Help", open from any screen, where to turn: to an adult and to the helplines of their country, by name and number, without links, by a list with a date of checking (R127 spec); the reminder is the camera's line before the photo of a page (R71) and Help's line for an adult (R63), and none stands before "Check", by the author's word (R53) | a new version of `parent-consent` spec, which still says the app reminds the child, before the first child — Д288 `spec` | "Help", the list of helplines by country and where it lies — E4; reporting a problem by the learner themselves — П-26 |
| keeping artifacts on the server | by the account owner's choice for the profile, off by default; turning it on is a consent by the text `storage-consent`, with its version, by `SetWorkStorage`, and turning it off erases the copies on the server (R113, R131, R136 spec): below, "Section for adults", and section 3.2, "Works on the server" (R30) | the list of the works on the server and the erasure of one — Д26; `GetWork` on another device — Д27; a teen with their own consent, and keeping turned on by an adult shown to a teen after their consent by a screen of its own (R141 spec) — E4 |
| the section for adults | profiles, tariff, own key, settings, export and deletion (architecture 3.1 spec); the default mode (R05), voicing (R07), own key (R08), language (R10), the closing period of the owner profile (R11), the page "This device" (R04); for a child's profile, the setting "ask before showing" the solution, on by default (R82 spec); the platform AI quota as a share, not a sum (concepts 14 spec): below, "Section for adults" (R29) | tariffs beyond the closed slice — О-14; children's profiles and the PIN — E4 |
| export and deletion | the export is assembled by `app` — from the device and from the part of `api`; deletion is confirmed by a fresh sign-in (architecture 4.5 spec); what comes from the device — section 3.2; the format is the part `export/`: a ZIP of the manifest of `app`, the part of `api` byte for byte, `api.zip`, per profile a file of what the device holds — with the time and pressure of the strokes, which the server does not see —, and the stored works as files of their own beside `api.zip`, which `app` reads one at a time by `GetWork` (R135, R151 spec; the 2026-11-10 amendment to R09 spec; R49); the parts of `api` are `ExportProfile` and `ExportAccount` (R98 spec), and for a learner with their own consent also their `ExportProfile` with `ACTION_LEARNING` (R136 spec); of a child consent a minimal record remains for 5 years (R117 spec), and of an essay the server keeps only the submission ends and the marks of the criteria (R87 spec) | the screens — E4; deleting the Firebase user leaves the "Sign in with Google" connection in the owner's Google account (T42 `api`), which revoking the Google token on deletion would remove — the row В52 `api` |
| legal texts | pages of `app` (architecture 3.13 spec), by `LegalService.GetLegalTexts` (R130 spec); in a self-hosting copy, the texts of its operator (R115 spec); only in English, and the line that the text is in English and the gist of a consent are in the screen's language, with a version's translation without force of its own where there is one (privacy 7 spec; R116, R141 spec; the 2026-11-11 amendment to R130 spec; section 3.8); Markdown without HTML, pictures and links; the page "Accessibility" (R11); a new version shown before it applies (the 2026-11-13 amendment to R130 spec; the 2026-11-15 amendment to R149 spec): below, "Legal pages" (R27; R31; R46; R69) | a way to withdraw consent beside a new version of the policy — E4 |
| the licenses of modules | `LICENSE` and `NOTICE` of the device part are served by `api`, and R62 spec quotes from Apache-2.0, §4(a): "You must give any other recipients of the Work or Derivative Works a copy of this License": below, "Licenses of modules" (R32) | which licenses third-party modules have — О-10 |
| module widgets | the widget host of `canvas`, in an iframe sandbox (R16 spec) | stage 4 |

**The questions of `spec` about the screens of `app`** — what `app` does while they are open, and what decided the others:
- **О-13, ranks and persona.** Until it is decided — a neutral tutor without ranks and a persona; the persona's voice will come with it (R07). Ranks on the skill map are also a detailing of `skill-map`.
- **О-14, tariff and platform AI quota.** In the first slice platform AI is without payment, within a quota (product 10 spec): in dollars per family day, with caps of the profile and of the account, and the learner and the adult see its share, not the sum (concepts 14 spec). The day ends at midnight by the family's clock, so with requests to platform AI `app` sends the device's clock offset from UTC, in minutes (R118 spec; concepts 15 spec). The quota says when it comes back — "today", "tomorrow" or a date by the device's calendar (R37) —, and the block "Tutor" names the quota and the limit of platform AI both under the rung's fallback text and after a correct answer without words. The share comes by `GetQuota` (the 2026-11-12 amendment to R98 spec), and `app` shows it (R40): to the learner as "Tutor time today: {percent} used" with its bar at the foot of the card "Next up" on Home, and at a cap reached by the words of the quota's refusal; to the adult as the section "Tutor time" at the bottom of the profile page: the shares of the profile and of the account, that the cap is reached and when the quota renews. Without a percentage — the tariff has no caps — the learner sees no line, and the adult sees that platform AI is closed; from an `api` without `GetQuota` there is neither the line nor the section. Payment — stage 3.
- **О-5, the mode without AI** — a free path, open to children too (product 9 spec). The choice of an option is already checked without AI (architecture 4.2 spec); the whole mode will come when `spec` decides it and modules give tasks with input and choice.
- **О-23, presentation style.** Voicing is in the presentation style (R07), and the default mode is a field of the profile of its own, not in the style (the 2026-11-02 amendment to R05). Neither goes into an AI call. The rest — with `tutor` and О-13.
- **П-26, reporting a problem by the learner themselves.** "Report a problem" is the adult's (R123 spec), and to a child the section "Help" says whom to turn to: the button is seen by a learner of the category `adult` alone (R33), until E4 decides how the adult reports at a reply of the tutor to a child. The recommendation: a teenager who gives consent themselves (R116 spec) reports themselves, in their own profile, about the tutor's words; decide before the beta with teenagers.
- **О-26, the hint ladder of a child who solves with the tutor's panel closed.** With the panel closed "Check" gives the verdict without the rung's words (R55), so a child can reach the fourth wrong answer and the offer to show the solution having read no rung. The recommendation: for a child, from the fourth wrong answer the offer comes in the panel, which opens with the last rung's words; decide with the children's screens of E4.
- **Decided by `spec`:** showing the solution — to a learner of the category `adult` from the start of the attempt, to a child from the fourth wrong answer, with the adult's setting "ask before showing" and the refusal "an adult is needed", showing ending the attempt with the outcome "solution shown"; a hint on request — before the first wrong answer "givens and unknown" and the technique, after it the words of the current rung once more, without AI, and "explain differently" once per rung (R82 spec; section 3.3); what a module may ask a learner under 18 to write about — made-up and general topics, with the reminder and "Help" at `app` (R127 spec) — `app` shows the reminder at the camera and in Help, none before "Check", by the author's word (R53; Д288 `spec`). To a child, the setting and the adult's action behind a PIN — E4.

**Home, Subjects and their frame** — `src/subjects/` and `src/app/Shell.tsx`, by the author's design (R79; R24; R62; R63):
- **Data:** the catalog and the connected modules of `ModuleService` — `ListCatalog`, `ConnectModule`, `DisconnectModule` and `ListConnectedModules`; the name and the explanation language of a module from the open view of the manifest of its current version, and a module without a current version by the name from the manifest of the former version that the shared cache holds, otherwise by its ID; each card's counts by `GetSkillMap` and its graph (R62); the open attempt — `GetAccount`, and its task by `GetOpenAttempt` (`src/task/openTask.ts`); what comes next — `ChooseNext` of `LearningService` (R99 spec).
- **The frame:** from 600 CSS px a row — the places Home, Subjects, the cognitive map shut with "Soon", and after a rule "Grown-ups" and Help, from 600 to 72em each but the page's its icon alone —, and at its end the tutor's pop-over, the notifications and the profile's pill, `LearnerMenu` short; below 600 the five places a tab bar at the bottom. "Grown-ups" holds the hint mode, `HintModeChoice` — "Questions only" or "Show where", set by a learner of the category `adult` by `SetHintMode`, a child's read only, "set by a grown-up" (R76) —, This device and "All settings". Help, `src/app/Help.tsx`, for a learner of the category `adult`: the tutor is an AI and a program checks every answer, what to do when the AI read the work wrongly, the name is made up, and Privacy Policy, Terms of Use and Accessibility by `openLegal` of the learner, with no external link or helpline (R63).
- **Home** (`MySubjects.tsx`, `HomeCard.tsx`): one card at the top — "Next up", the task open by its name, else the lesson begun, else "What's next", and the tutor time at its foot; "Tasks from my paper", "Open camera" to a new page (R71) and "Your pages" (R77) —; a card of each module connected, by name in the order of the screen's language; the legend of the five states and two marks below them from 600 CSS px, on a phone behind "What the marks mean" as a sheet (R62).
- **Subjects** (`SubjectsPage.tsx`): "Connected · {n}", each card with "Skill map", "Disconnect" and "License", then "More on this server" with "Connect" and "License", each card its name in its explanation language (`lang` on the heading), "Explanation language: …" when it is not the screen's, and "Not available now" without a current version, each button described by the name; each list by name, `Intl.Collator` of the screen's language. The answer of `ConnectModule` and `DisconnectModule` is at once the new list of connected ones, and the focus goes with the card to its button in the other list; a manifest that did not come — the name by the ID, "The names of some modules didn't come" and "Try again".

**A task from my paper** — `#/paper`, `src/paper/`, the mockup's camera, pick and solve (R71):
- **The camera:** `app`'s own, the device's camera at the back in a video, "Take photo", "Choose from photos" and "Close", and the line "Keep names out of the photo"; with no camera the photos alone. No finding of the page, no flash. Its top bar is the mockup's dark glass, white on it.
- **The photo:** drawn anew on a canvas, a JPEG with its long side at most 3 200 px and at most 4 MiB, nothing of the file's metadata (privacy 6 spec); `FindTasks` takes it once, each try with a key of its own. Frames found on it turned upright, `turnDegrees`, turn it the same way, its corners white, before they show, and the page goes on with it turned (R162 spec).
- **The pick:** the frames found, picked, each a button with `aria-pressed`, and every frame's cross — it and a drawn frame's corner stand on the frame's corner, kept inside the photo by half a target, so that a frame at the photo's edge puts neither past the screen's; a frame dragged or made by two taps, its key none that a task begun on the page has had, so that it begins an attempt of its own, and its corner; "Select all", the count and Start, the limit at the bar's head; the top bar's Back and "Your page", in its middle the finding, a turning ring, then "{n} tasks found · {k} framed by you", and "Remove all frames" and Retake at its end. A refusal or an outcome that closes AI shuts Start.
- **The request about the page** (R74; R165 spec): the tutor's button and panel of the task screen, `src/task/TutorPanel.tsx`, on the pick too, the debug button above it and hidden while the panel is open; in the panel the tutor's line of what it takes, the conversation and Undo, and the field, at most 200 code points, growing down in its blue edge, with the microphone, off, and Send, icons alone (R60). A request goes to `FindTasks` with the frames on the page, found and drawn, in the order of the page; the frames it gave take the place of those found, the learner's own staying, and the program's line says how many, or that none came and the page stays. Undo brings back the frames before until the learner changes any; the request and the conversation live in `PaperContext` alone, and the debug log shows the request from there.
- **Start:** the crop of each frame picked and not begun yet cut on the device as a PNG, then `BeginPaperAttempts` in the order of the page, with the page's ID, so that `api` closes the open tasks of another page alone; the attempts, the crops and the work on each in `PaperContext`, and from then on the page in the profile's history (R77).
- **Your pages** (R77), the mockup's `home-pages` and `solve-kept`, its rows as the author's `Vashi-stranicy-1a`: a pop-over from the card's button, the pages newest first, each a thumbnail of its photo beside when it was taken and how many tasks it began, and the line that they are kept on this device until the profile is deleted and go into its export; a page opens on the screen of solving with every task begun on it and its work — one whose frame the learner removed before a later Start too, its frame back on the page —, no verdict. A task whose attempt is over — solved, or closed by `api`, which a Check or a Hint learns — is only to be looked at: its sheet inert, no Hint on the screen or in the tutor's panel, and its Check says "This task is already over".
- **The solving:** the top bar's Back, the task's name — "Task n", "Your frame, task n" — with "n of N · from your page" under it but on a phone, and ‹, the counter that opens the list of the tasks begun with "Add", back to the page, and › — the counter named "Tasks, n / N", its caption in its name (2.5.3); each task its own `<novatrail-canvas>` with `paper`, its history and `tracing` kept on its changes (R87 canvas); the row Hint · Check. Check sends `PaperWork` of `exportWork()`, the task's crop and the work's, and the page keeps the context of each Check answered, once, with the history's length at its press, and no verdict; nothing written is "Write your solution first". Its card is "Reading your work…", then the verdict by the key api derived — "Correct", "Not yet", the work not read, "Not checked" —, or "Couldn't check this one" for a task with no key, with Hint and "Next task"; one solved or with no key takes no Check again (R71).
- **The tutor on the solving** (R75; R148 spec): the tutor's button and panel of the task screen, `src/paper/SolveTutor.tsx` in `src/task/TutorPanel.tsx`, opened by it and by any Hint, never by a verdict; in its head the share of the quota and the voicing; in its conversation the line on what comes here, "Hints for this task come here, a question at a time. A program checks every answer.", with no line that the tutor is an AI (R33), the verdict as a line while it is open — the card above the row while it is closed — and the hint in a bubble, kept till another task, Check or Hint and said while the voicing is on; at its foot Hint till the task is solved and "Next task" once its Check is over, the card above the row the same; no "Explain differently" till a task from paper has words of its own. A failure of a Check or a Hint stands in the open panel. Beside it the sheet and the foot end where it begins; on a phone its sheet hides the row and the canvas's bar of tools.

**The way in** — by the mockup's states `signin` … `who` (R61; R148 spec):
- **Where:** the sign-in and the invite code's two steps are `WelcomeScreen`: the sky, the name and the trail to the spark, on the sign-in the language's pill (section 3.8), and each step on a dark card, "Privacy and legal information" at its foot (R31), placed by the width as R61 has it. The sign-in: what NovaTrail is, Google's own button with its "G" (R61; R12 spec), and the note for grown-ups with its "18". Each step's button looks off until its step is given but keeps the focus, and pressed says what is missing and focuses it (R20). The code alone first, in four boxes of four, "Back" signing out; then the statement "I am 18" and the acceptance, each text's name a link to its sheet (R27; R69), and "Back" to the code, kept; what is typed and ticked outlives a visit to the legal pages, until the account is made or the owner signs out. "Create account" sends the code, the statement and the versions read together; a code `api` did not take brings the code's step back, with its line and the focus. The owner's profile, the layout `profiles`: the same sky and stars, the name above, and a dark card — the avatar, the set's own until one is chosen, "Choose an avatar" opening the set in a pop-over centred below it (R59), and the rows "Pseudonym" and "Country", the country's row opening the list in a pop-over; "Continue" waits for both. "Who is learning?", the layout `who`: the sky with stars of its own, the brand and "Sign out", a glass pill, above; each profile a dark card its one button covers, the line whose it is its description, "Start" its look; "Add a learner" a glass card, disabled, with its line why, until E4 (R61).
- **The avatars:** the set of `app`, a picture of Material Symbols Rounded, outlined, white on a gradient of the mockup's, in a circle — under Apache-2.0, as every icon of the set (section 3.1, "Icons"). The profile's field `avatar` keeps the code; no picture is uploaded (R148 spec). The order is the choice's, the first the set's own:

  | code | picture | gradient |
  |---|---|---|
  | `peak` | `landscape` | `#8e8e93` → `#3a3a3c` |
