  A profile created with none chosen sends no field (R98 spec keeps it optional) and shows the set's own, as does a code this build does not know — one a newer build gave (`account.proto`). A new avatar is a row here, its words in both dictionaries and its icon in `scripts/icons.ts`; a code is never reused for another picture.
- **The countries:** the 249 codes of ISO 3166-1 alpha-2, the type `Country` of the part `consent-age/`, in the screen's language (`src/account/countries.ts`); which of them the deployment takes, `api` alone says, by `CAUSE_COUNTRY_NOT_ACCEPTED`: neither the deployment configuration nor the contract gives the list. A list of the deployment's is a row «В реестр spec» of [run/QUESTIONS.md](../run/QUESTIONS.md).
- **Where it differs from the mockup**: R61, by the regions of the comparison (R58).
- **Code:** `src/signin/` — `Welcome.tsx`, `SignInScreen.tsx`, `google-g.svg` —, `src/i18n/LanguagePill.tsx`, `src/app/Brand.tsx`; `src/account/` — `InviteScreen.tsx`, `OwnerProfileScreen.tsx`, `ChooseScreen.tsx`, `Stars.tsx`, `FormFailure.tsx`, `avatars.ts`, `Avatar.tsx`. Checked: the comparison of the eleven states in three engines (`mockup/screens.browser.test.tsx`); the screens' tests — the profile with an avatar and without one, a code not taken, a country not taken, the avatars on the choice, `avatars.node.test.ts` —; axe-core, the targets and 320 px in `src/a11y/screens.browser.test.tsx`.

**Section for adults** (R29):
- **Routes:** `#/adults` is the first page, `#/adults/<page>` the others; the profile is not in the address but in the section's memory (R14). The profile page without a chosen profile — after a reload — and an unknown page lead to the first page, in their place in the history.
- **Where:** `AccountGate` shows the section in place of the choice of the learner or of the learner's screens, while the account is open. The entry is "For grown-ups" in the profile's menu (R29); without a PIN until E4 (R56).
- **The section's header** — the mockup's top bar (R68): the heading in its pill, "For grown-ups" under it but on the first page, "Back to the section" an icon before it but on the first page, and "Close the section" × at its other end, home: to the choice of the learner or to Home.
- **The look** — by the mockup's rules of style (R68): `SectionScreen` of `src/adults/SectionScreen.tsx`, the layout `adults`; each group a card; the profiles rows with their avatars; the profile page's avatar above the form, changed in a pop-over (`AvatarChoice`) and saved with it; a module's map in the frame of the learner's. Checked: the section's tests in three engines; axe-core and 320 CSS px (`src/a11y/screens.browser.test.tsx`).
- **First page:** "Profiles" — a button "Profile: {pseudonym}" for each, the owner first; "This device" and the button "See what is on this device"; "Language on this device" — the buttons of the device's language (section 3.8); "Privacy and legal information" and the button "Read the privacy policy and other texts" to the legal pages (R31); last, "New versions of the texts" while a text has a next version (R46; below, "Legal pages").
- **"This device"** `#/adults/device` (R04): for each profile, the unfinished tasks, the space and the warning about losing works, and for the whole site, the browser's estimate, persistent storage, Safari's seven days and the "Home Screen"; "Erase the profile's data on this device" and "Erase everything on this device" — with a question. "Erase everything" is a sign-out and the erasure of everything of the account, and on top of it the device's settings and the heartbeat database. In detail — section 3.2.
- **Profile page** `#/adults/profile` — one form with "Save": pseudonym, the default mode (section 3.3), the language of the learner's screens (section 3.8) and voicing with its speed (section 3.5). `UpdateProfile` — only with the fields that changed against what was shown; nothing changed — no request. From the answer the tab takes the settings and the keeping of works, not the attempt and the runs, and sends the profile to other tabs by the `BroadcastChannel` `novatrail-profiles`. Under the form, "Keeping works on the server" (R30): whether it is on, and turning it on is a consent in place of the button: the text `storage-consent` through `LegalText`, the words of R127 spec and a tick of explicit consent, which holds to the version shown; `SetWorkStorage` with `ACTION_ADULT` and this version, on `CAUSE_TEXT_NOT_CURRENT` — the text anew and the tick again. Turning it off is at once, without a question. The answer goes the same way as the answer of `UpdateProfile`. Turning it on puts every work the profile keeps on this device on the server by `PutWork`, and turning it off takes the marks of the works on the server off this device (section 3.2, "Works on the server"; R30). Under the keeping, "Modules", a line about them and the button "See the modules and their licenses" (R32); under them, "Progress" and the button "See the progress" (R35); at the bottom, "Tutor time" (R40).
- **How it grows:** a page of the account or of the device is a part of the hash and a button on the first page, a page of the profile a part of the hash and a button on the profile page (R29): "This device" — `#/adults/device`; the own key — `#/adults/own-key`, from the first page (R47); the profile's modules — `#/adults/modules`, and the license of a version `#/adults/license/<digest>` (R32); progress — `#/adults/progress`, and a module's map in it `#/adults/progress/<module ID>` (R35). Keeping works on the server (R30) and "Tutor time" (R40) are sections of the profile page: `GetQuota` asks for a profile.
- **Code:** `src/adults/AdultSection.tsx` — the header, the first page and the choice of the page by the hash, `src/adults/ProfilePage.tsx` — the form, `src/adults/DevicePage.tsx` — "This device", `src/adults/WorkStorage.tsx` — keeping works on the server and the consent, the works it sends and the marks it takes off, `src/adults/ModulesPage.tsx` — the profile's modules, `src/adults/ProgressPage.tsx` and `src/adults/progress.ts` — progress, `src/adults/QuotaSection.tsx` and `src/account/quota.ts` — "Tutor time", and `src/adults/order.ts` — the order of profiles; `src/account/settings.ts` — the form by the profile, what changed, the profile from the answer with the tab's attempt and runs (`withSettings`, `asGiven`) and the message to other tabs; `Session.profileChanged` and `Session.profileFailed`; `AccountGate` — the section and the channel. Checked in Chromium: the path from the choice of the learner and from the header, the request only with the changed fields, the message to another tab and its profile on the learner's screens, the default mode in an open attempt, the first page instead of a page without a profile and an unknown one; "This device" — what is seen per profile, a profile without a database and a database that cannot be read, persistent storage, both erasures with a question and focus, and "Erase everything" through `AccountGate` to the sign-in screen; keeping works — off by default, the consent with the text and the words of R127 spec, the version of the text shown in `SetWorkStorage`, `CAUSE_TEXT_NOT_CURRENT` — the text anew and the tick again, without a text in force — a request without `text`, turning off without a question, refusals, a failure to read the text and the focus after each step, and through `AccountGate` turning on reaching another tab, with the warning of "This device" about losing works standing with keeping turned on too; "Tutor time" — in Node the request by action, `unimplemented` without a reload, another failure, the learner's and the adult's lines in `en` and `ru` and the day by the device's calendar, and in Chromium the section under "Progress" with both shares and the day, a cap reached, a tariff without caps, no section from an `api` without `GetQuota`, a failure and a retry, and on Home the line of the share, without the account's share, the words of the refusal at a cap reached, no line without a percentage, without `GetQuota` and at a failure, and again by "Try again" of the lists; the accessibility checks — on the first page, the profile page with an error, with the consent unticked, with keeping turned on and with a cap reached, "This device" and its question before "Erase everything", and Home with the share, in `en`, `ru` and the pseudo-language, with a language that is not in the build, at 1024 and 320 px, and in forced colors.

**Legal pages** (R31):
- **Routes:** `#/legal` — the list, `#/legal/privacy-policy`, `#/legal/terms-of-use` and `#/legal/storage-consent` — the texts by ID, `#/legal/accessibility` and `#/legal/notices`; `#/legal/<id>/next`, a text's next version, and `#/legal/operator`, who runs the copy (R46); an unknown page — and a family text — leads to the list. The family texts get no pages: the consent screens show them, E4.
- **Where:** `AccountGate` shows them at any step of the session, before sign-in too, in the device's language. The policy and the terms open first as a sheet over the screen, `LegalSheet` (R69), from "Privacy and legal information", their names in the tick and Help; its "Read the full text" opens the page. "Read the privacy policy and other texts" is on the section's first page. Each page is a sheet of the mockup's legal text (R69): "Back to the list" before its heading but on the list, "Close" × at the other end, to where they were opened from: from the section — to its first page, while its account is open, otherwise home; where from — in memory, and after a reload — home (R31). A page opens straight from the notice of next versions on the section's first page, and the operator's page from the page on the AI tutor, and "Close" leads back there (R46).
- **Text page:** "Version {version}, in force since {date}" as a chip, the gist below its caption, the line that the English text is in force, the translation and the body — open, `LegalContent`, read at each opening past the cache (R27); no version in force, or a failure and "Try again", said. While the text has a next version, a note after the gist: "Version {version} of this text applies from {date}" and the link "Read version {version}" (R46; R69).
- **Next version's page** `#/legal/<id>/next` (R46): "{text}: the next version", "Version {version}, applies from {date}", "Until then, version {version} is in force", the line that a consent given in the app can be withdrawn before it applies, "Read the version in force" and the version as `LegalContent` shows the current one; without a next version — "This text has no next version now", and without a current one there is neither "until then" nor the way back.
- **Who runs this copy** `#/legal/operator` (R46): the section Who we are of the policy in force, from its heading of the second level to the next of the first or the second, in English — `sectionOf` —, with the version and the day of the policy, the line that the English text is in force on a screen not in English, and "Read the whole privacy policy"; a policy without the section and a copy without a policy say so. The list leads to it by an item after the three texts, "Accessibility" by its button (section 3.9), the page on the AI tutor by "Who runs the app".
- **Notices of third-party code:** `NOTICE` and `LICENSE` of the build as they are, `<pre lang="en">` with line wrapping; the copy is from the bundle, not from `/NOTICE` of the hosting: `app` loads none of its parts at run time (R16 spec), and `vite preview` and a self-hosting copy might not serve `/NOTICE` (section 3.1).
- **A new version before it applies** — the promise of the section "Changes to this policy" of the policy and CGS §42-520(b)(3) (sources 3.11 spec) —, by `next_texts` of `GetLegalTexts` (the 2026-11-13 amendment to R130 spec), which gives the version of the nearest day (the 2026-11-15 amendment to R149 spec; R46). A sheet names it with the way to its page; in place, `NextLegalText`, the consent to keeping works follows `storage-consent` by its own, a disclosure titled with the day, and with no current version, with no tick; the tick, `CreateAccount` and `SetWorkStorage` name the current version alone. After signing in, `NextVersions` at the end of the section's first page, and nowhere else until the bell of the main menu carries it with every notification (Д45): "New versions of the texts", each with its day and "Read {text}, version {version}", and while a profile keeps its works on the server, the line that this can be switched off before then; no next version, an `api` without the field and a failure show nothing. An owner without children has only keeping works to withdraw (R30): their learning rests on the contract (R116 spec), and the account's deletion is E4.
- **Code:** `src/legal/LegalPages.tsx` — the list, the pages, `NextTextPage`, `OperatorPage` and `LegalScreen`, their sheet with its head (R69); `src/legal/LegalSheet.tsx`; `src/legal/notices.ts`; `LegalContent` and `NextLegalText` in `src/legal/LegalText.tsx`; `PAGE_TEXTS`, `readLegal`, `nextOf` and `pageNext` in `src/legal/texts.ts`; `sectionOf` in `src/legal/body.ts`; `src/legal/NextVersions.tsx`; the route `LEGAL` in `src/app/hash.ts`. Checked: in Node — `readLegal` with the next versions, without the field and with `unimplemented`, and `sectionOf` — up to the next heading of its level, to the end, not of the third level or another text; in the three engines — the list and the header, a text on a Russian screen with the version, the gist, the translation and the body, without links, a text without a version in force and an `api` without `LegalService`, a failure and "Try again", "Accessibility" with its set of R11, the notices byte for byte, an unknown page, the line and the button of the next version on the text's page, the next version's page with the day, "until then", the withdrawal and the way back, without a next version and without a current one, the operator's page on a Russian screen, without the section and without a policy; the sheet from the tick, the foot and Help, the tick and `CreateAccount` on the current, and in the consent to keeping works; the notice on the section's first page and none on the choice of the learner, with the line on keeping works and without it, two texts with next versions of one number, none at a failure, and "Close" back; through `AccountGate` — the path from the sign-in screen without signing in, from the section back to the section, and from the page on the AI tutor to the operator and back, signed in and not; the accessibility checks — on the list, the policy page with a translation and a table, "Accessibility", the notices, the next version's page, the operator's page, the choice of the learner and the section's first page with the notice, in `en`, `ru` and the pseudo-language, at 1024 and 320 px; the test of the built page — "Accessibility", the operator's page and the policy's page, under the CSP header, without violations, with `GetLegalTexts` at each opening and no other request beyond `/deployment.json`; the path of `walk/` — on the section's first page, the notice exactly when `api` gives `next_texts`; `legal-privacy`, `legal-terms` and `legal-access` against the mockup (R69).

**Licenses of modules** (R32):
- **What:** `LICENSE` and `NOTICE` of the device part of one version — as they are, each in `<pre lang="en">` with line wrapping, like the notices of third-party code, — and above them the module by the manifest of this version: the name in the explanation language, the version and the license by its SPDX identifier. The files are a `GET` without a token at the address of `api` ([the text of the contracts](../spec/contracts/README.md), section 15, "Files of a device part"): `200` — the text, `404` for `NOTICE` — "The module has no file NOTICE", and for `LICENSE`, without which the format does not assemble a package, — "No file LICENSE came with the module"; `410` — "This version of the module is no longer given" and `CheckPackages`; `503` — a retry by `Retry-After`; a network failure — its words and "Try again". The files are read one after the other, and the manifest separately: a manifest that is slow or does not come does not hold up the texts. There are no links on the page: a link to a file would lead to another origin (privacy 7 spec).
- **Routes:** `#/license/<digest>` and `#/adults/license/<digest>`; the package digest is an ID of content, not of a learner (R14). A route without a digest leads home or to the first page of the section, in its place in the history.
- **Where:** at a card of Subjects with a current version — connected and in the catalog — the button "License" (R79), described by the module's name; the page `#/license/…` is shown by `AccountGate` at any step, like the legal pages, a sheet of the mockup's legal text with its close × home (R69), and in the language of the learner's screens while a learner is chosen. In the section — the profile's modules page `#/adults/modules` from the profile page: the connected modules by `ListConnectedModules`, cards named as on Subjects, at each version — "License", and at a module without a current version — "Not available now" without a button; the license is a page of the section, under its header and with "Back to the modules", while a profile is chosen.
- **The language of the texts** of a third-party module the format does not name, and `app` gives them `lang="en"`: the official modules are Apache-2.0 in English (R20 spec), and the licenses of third-party ones are О-10. The language of `LICENSE` and `NOTICE` is the language of the license, and there is no field for it until the answer to О-10 (R38).
- **Code:** `src/packages/LicensePage.tsx` — the page, its route and its sheet; `src/packages/license.ts` — reading the files; `text` and `isDigest` in `src/packages/files.ts`; `src/adults/ModulesPage.tsx`; `src/subjects/CardHeading.tsx` — what a module card says, shared by Subjects, Home's cards and the section; the route `MODULE_LICENSE` in `src/app/hash.ts`. Checked: in Node — `200`, `404`, `410` with one `CheckPackages`, `503` and a broken-off body for `LICENSE` and `NOTICE`; in Chromium — the page with the texts as they are and `lang="en"`, a module without `NOTICE` on a Russian screen, the texts without a manifest that does not come, a withdrawn version, a `LICENSE` that is missing, a failure and "Try again", a route without a digest, the profile's modules and the path to the license and back, the license after a reload without a chosen profile; through `AccountGate` — from the card on Subjects in the profile's language and back, from the section in the device's language and by the address before sign-in; the accessibility checks — the cards with "License", the license page and the profile's modules, in `en`, `ru` and the pseudo-language, at 1024 and 320 px; the test of the built page — by the address before sign-in, under the CSP header, without violations and links, with requests only to `api`.

**Tutor — AI** (R33):
- **Disclosure:** the adult's Help (R63), the first bubble of the tutor's pop-over at the top right of the frame (R79), and the page on the tutor, `#/tutor`, below; the panels of a task, a lesson's task, an essay and a task from paper open with no line of it, by the author's word: it is plain to anyone (R33; R67; R75) — whether that is enough for a child is Д287 `spec`. Always, not once: there is no mark "already seen" on the device.
- **Learning materials** — the page `#/tutor`, "How to use the AI tutor safely", six sections: it is AI, and the verdict is the program's; it can be wrong; what goes to AI and what does not, and not to write names and personal things; it is for learning; it is not someone to talk to about personal things; if something is wrong — "Report a problem" and the emergency service. The content is from the platform's documents, not from the providers' terms: those require materials but do not name their content (R33). `AccountGate` shows the page at any step, before sign-in too, in the language of the learner's screens while one is chosen, a sheet of the mockup's legal text with its close × home (R69); there are no links on it. No screen leads to it yet — Д46.
- **"Report a problem"** — a button under the tutor's words in the panel's conversation, which opens its form in a modal pop-over (`PopOver`; R67), 440 wide 40 below the window's top in its middle, on a phone a sheet: the words quoted on grey, then "What is wrong with the words?" — six kinds in the order of `ProblemKind`, required, and "Something else" with a text; "What else to say, if anything" — up to 1000 characters, `maxLength`, with a request not to write names; lines that the words will go with the report and that in danger right now — the emergency service; "Send" and "Cancel". `ReportProblem` carries the words as the block showed them, the reply that brought them — `TUTOR_REPLY_RUNG`, `TUTOR_REPLY_EXPLAINED_DIFFERENTLY` or `TUTOR_REPLY_CORRECT_ANSWER`, — and the rung for the first two; the operator reviews it within 3 days (R123 spec). The idempotency key is one per report, as the form holds it: a retry with the same one, an edit or a refusal with a new one. The answer is "Thank you: the report is sent. It will be reviewed by {date}" by `review_by`, and the button at these words is no longer there.
- **Who sees the button** — a learner of the category `adult`; at a profile of another category — E4, by П-26 (above, "The questions of `spec`").
- **Code:** `src/tutor/report.ts`, `src/tutor/ReportProblem.tsx`, `src/tutor/TutorPage.tsx`; the line and the place of the button — `src/task/TutorWords.tsx`, the words with their reply — `Said` in `src/task/Task.tsx`; the disclosure — `src/app/Help.tsx` and `HomeTutor` of `src/app/Shell.tsx`; the route `TUTOR` in `src/app/hash.ts`. Checked: in Node — six kinds, the conditions of sending and the request with a rung and without; in Chromium — the line of disclosure and no button for a learner who is not `adult`; a report about the words of a rung — the kind, the text, the key, `review_by` and focus; a retry after a lost answer and after a failure — with the same key, and `api` holds one report; an edit — with a new key; the key at `CAUSE_STILL_GOING` and a new one after a refusal; the correct answer words without a rung; the words with which the attempt went on, and "Explain differently" with its rung; "Something else" without a text, "Cancel" with focus and an answer without a day; the disclosure in Help and in the tutor's pop-over on Home; through `AccountGate` — the page in the profile's language and back, before sign-in by the address and an unknown part of the hash; the accessibility checks — the page, the button, the form with an error and the form after a correct answer in `en`, `ru` and the pseudo-language, at 1024 and 320 px, and the form in forced colors; the test of the built page — `#/tutor` under the CSP header, without violations and links, without requests to `api`.

**Skill map** (R34):
- **Routes:** `#/skill-map/<module ID>` — the module's map, in the frame of R79 as a place of Subjects; `#/skill-map` alone, or with extra parts, goes to Subjects in its place in the history. A module ID is an ID of content, not of a learner (R14). The entries are the skill map's button on a card of Home and "Skill map" on a connected card of Subjects. `MapScreen`: "Subjects" back above the heading "Skill map", the module beside it — a pill of its tile and title whose pop-over lists the modules connected, the one shown ticked, to open another's map —, and a white frame below, a region named "Skill map: {module}", which scrolls inside, the keyboard by the focus of the buttons in it, with the map's lines, the modules to connect and the element (R34).
- **Map:** `GetSkillMap` of `ProgressService` with `ACTION_LEARNING` — the state of the skills and the anchors and the package digest on which it was computed, — and by this digest, from the shared cache of open views, the skill graph with tiers, `GetSkillGraph`, and the manifest, `GetManifest` (R99 spec). The element is `view="learner"`, `lang` is the language of the learner's screens, the headings of its bands are `h2` under the screen's heading "Skill map: {module}". For a module without a graph — "This subject has no skill map". Above the element, when an adult set the module's level, "The level was set by an adult on {date}" by `level_set_at`, the date in the screen's language (R56; R157 spec): every learner sees it. The theme is `--novatrail-skill-map-paper`, `-ink` and `-accent` in `src/theme/theme.css` — the background, the text and the main color of the theme of `app`, whose pairs already hold the contrast (section 3.9).
- **Choosing a skill:** "Practise" on a skill's tile — the event `skillchoose` — sends `ChooseNext` with the skill's full ID, `<module>/skill/<skill>`, and from there as "What's next": a lesson opens, a task begins (R24). While an attempt is open, first comes the question of "Something else": "The task in progress closes…", "Go on" and "Cancel" with focus back on "Practise". While the choice is going, the status line above the map says "Opening what's next…", and another press of "Practise" does nothing; "Nothing to choose" — by the same line. "Go on", like "Cancel", returns focus to "Practise". The skill is held by `api` (R107 spec), and "What's next" and "Something else" send `ChooseNext` without a skill and return the choice to the engine. The idempotency key of the choice is one per skill, until the choice has ended with an answer or a refusal. The code is the shared hook `useEngine` in `src/next/`, which `NextStep` uses too.
- **Modules for an anchor** — beside the map, as the section "Modules to connect" above it: the element does not read `teaching_modules`, which only the stub of `api` was seen to fill — Д43. These are the modules of all anchors whose mastery is "unknown" or a value the build does not know, — each once, as cards as on Subjects, with the name from the manifest of the current version by the digest from `ListCatalog` and "Connect" — `ConnectModule`. A module that the catalog does not give the profile is "Not available now" without a button. After "Connect", in place of the button — "Connected: it is on Home now" with focus, and the map's state is read anew, without blinking: the anchor may become "not mastered". The list is the one there was when the map was opened, and it does not change until the next opening. The anchors are not named one by one: `app` has no names of anchors — they are in the shared taxonomy of the format's release and in the element itself.
- **For the adult:** `readModuleMap` in `src/skills/map.ts` reads the map with any action, and `ModuleSkillMap` in `src/skills/SkillMap.tsx` draws the element with `view="adult"` and the level of the screen's headings — without `onChoose`: the adult does not choose a skill (R35).
- **Code:** `src/skills/` — `jsx.ts`, `map.ts`, `SkillMap.tsx` and `SkillMapScreen.tsx` — the screens and their route; `src/next/useEngine.ts`; the label — `isSequential` and `sequentialOf` in `src/subjects/modules.ts`, `src/subjects/useSequential.ts` and `CardHeading`; the route `SKILL_MAP` in `src/app/hash.ts`; the keys `skillmap.*` and `subjects.sequential` in the dictionaries. Checked: in Node — reading the map with the cache of views, without a graph and a refusal, the modules for an anchor — "unknown", "mastered", "not mastered", an unknown value and a retry, the label and its graph; in Chromium — the element's properties, attributes and event, the list of maps and the path to a map and back, `ChooseNext` with a skill and a task, the line "Opening…" and a press during the choice, "nothing to choose", the question at an open attempt with "Cancel" and "Go on" and the focus after them, a module for an anchor in `ru` — the name and "Connect", a module outside the catalog, connecting with focus and the new state, a failure of connecting and a retry, an anchor "mastered" without the section, a module without a graph, a refusal and "Try again", the label on Home and the entry to the map; the accessibility checks — the list of maps and a map with an open skill and a module for an anchor, in `en`, `ru` and the pseudo-language, at 1024 and 320 px, with the element's subtree; the test of the built page — the element under the CSP header: its styles, the lines of the graph and "Practise", without violations and without requests of its own.

**The way back** (R57): every learner's screen but Home and Subjects has a way back — "Back to Home", `src/app/HomeButton.tsx`, where R57 puts it: the task screen, a task or a lesson not read, their ends; on the skill map "Subjects" above its heading (R34); inside a lesson its "Leave the lesson". Tested in three engines: each leads to `#/home`.

**Progress in the section for adults** (R35):
- **Routes:** `#/adults/progress` — the progress of the profile chosen in the section, and `#/adults/progress/<module ID>` — the module's map in the adult's view; without a chosen profile — after a reload — both lead to the first page of the section, in their place in the history. The entry is the section "Progress" on the profile page, under "Modules", and the button "See the progress". The language is the device's.
- **Page:** "Progress: {pseudonym}" and a line that why each task was given is seen only here. Under it:
  - "Skill maps" — the connected modules as cards named as on Subjects, with "Open the map"; the map is the element `skill-map` with `view="adult"`, the device's language and the headings of bands `h2`, by `GetSkillMap` with `ACTION_ADULT` and the open views from the shared cache (the block "Skill map" above), without choosing a skill, and "Back to the progress"; above the element, the part "Level" (R56): the band of `level_band` by its title in the manifest, "The level was set by an adult on {date}" by `level_set_at`, and "Easier: {band}" and "Harder: {band}", the bands next to it, by `SetModuleLevel`; `CAUSE_BAND_NOT_NEXT` reads the map again;
  - "Traps that repeat" — from the first page of `GetProgress` and only from it: `description` as the heading and `trap_advice` — "Advice", with the `lang` of the module's explanation language, the module, in how many closed attempts and the last time;
  - "Attempts" — newest first, by a page of `GetProgress`, and "Show more" by `next_page_token` with focus on the first new attempt, and for a page without attempts — on the last one shown or on the heading "Attempts". A failure of the first page is under this heading. An attempt has "Task {task}", the module, the lesson, the version, the result by the close reason and the wrong answers, the wrong answers, when it was begun and closed, "Why it was given" — or "Why its lesson was given" — with the words of the reason code and "Chosen", and without `chosen_at` — "The learner began it again: the program chose nothing".
- **How they are named:** a task — by the local ID from the full ID: the format has no names of tasks. A lesson — by its name `lesson_title`, with the `lang` of the module's explanation language, and when `api` no longer reads the package of the attempt's version and the name is empty, by the local ID. A solved attempt — closed by a correct answer, by showing the solution or by "Done" — has "Skills": the names `skill_titles` as a list in the screen's language, each with the `lang` of the module's explanation language; another attempt does not have them — the task's skills are closed while it can be begun again (R40). A module — by the name from the manifest of the current version of the connected module, otherwise from the newest manifest of the cache, otherwise by the ID; the cache is read by the same `cardsOf` as the cards, and only for the modules that are not among the connected ones or whose name did not come.
- **Reason code** — the nine values of `ReasonCode` in words for the adult (R35; section 3.8), an unknown one by a general line. It is not on the learner's screens: `GetProgress` and the field `reason_code` are known only to `src/adults/ProgressPage.tsx` and `src/adults/progress.ts` (concepts 12 spec; R100 spec).
- **Code:** `src/adults/progress.ts` — the routes, the pages, the IDs of records, the result and the words of the codes; `src/adults/ProgressPage.tsx` — `ProgressPage` and `ProgressMap`; `src/skills/level.ts` — `levelOf`, the band of the level and the bands next to it, and `src/skills/Level.tsx` — `LevelCorrection` and `LevelSetBy` (R56); `src/subjects/ModuleCards.tsx` — the shared module card with one button, its list and the profile's connected modules, by which both the list of skill maps and the profile's modules are built; `mapTitle` and `MapHeading` in `src/skills/SkillMap.tsx`. Checked: in Node — the pages by the token, the routes, the IDs of records, the result of each close reason and of an unknown one, the words of the nine codes in `en` and `ru`, all different, and the general line of an unknown code, and a walk over the sources of `src/` — that `GetProgress`, `reason_code` and the words of the codes are only in the section's progress; in Chromium — the path from the profile page, the traps with the module's language, the attempts with the result, the time and the words of the code, "Show more" with focus — and for a page without attempts, — the lesson by its name and the skills of a solved attempt with the module's language, and a lesson without a name — the ID, an unknown code and close reason, the traps of the second page not shown, a failure of the next and of the first page and a retry, an empty progress, a failure of the list of modules and of the map with a retry, the map in the adult's view by `ACTION_ADULT` and back, the words in `ru`, the pages without a profile; the level, its ends, `CAUSE_BAND_NOT_NEXT`, failures and a press while one goes, and the learner's line; through `AccountGate` — the learner's screens in the profile's language — Home, the task, "Something else" and the skill map — without a single text of a code and without `GetProgress`, and in the section — all nine; the accessibility checks — the progress page and the map in the adult's view with an open skill, in `en`, `ru` and the pseudo-language, at 1024 and 320 px.
